**Owner:** the architect. **Schema:** `data-access.v1.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12). Each call's request, response and errors are `$defs` named `<call>.request`, `<call>.response` and `<call>.errors` (02-contracts section 2 item 2); `<call>.exchange` pairs a request with its outcome, which is what every fixture is. **Checked by:** `npm run contracts:check`. **Contract test:** `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, T-084), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 migrated: each fixture's request gives its outcome; 1,000 parallel `reserve` calls on one slot give exactly one success (BP section 10, card T-041); a repeated commit writes nothing twice; parallel claims never hand one row to two senders. A change after the freeze is a new file, `data-access.v1.1.schema.json` (additive) or `data-access.v2.schema.json` (breaking, with the CTO's OK), with new fixtures (build prompt 6.4). **v1.1:** `data-access.v1.1.schema.json`, contract test `tests/contract/data-access.v1.1.test.ts` (QA, T-116), the same way against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 and K-07 v1's schema addition migrated. **v1.2:** `data-access.v1.2.schema.json`, contract test `tests/contract/data-access.v1.2.test.ts` (QA, T-128: every v1.2 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused), and its database side `tests/contract/data-access.v1.2.db-side.test.ts` (QA, T-478), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1, K-07 v1's schema addition and K-29 v1 migrated. **v1.3:** `data-access.v1.3.schema.json`, contract test `tests/contract/data-access.v1.3.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.3 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused) and its database side (QA, a new task beside T-1138's own tests), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1, K-07 v1's schema addition and K-34 v1 migrated. **v1.4:** `data-access.v1.4.schema.json`, contract test `tests/contract/data-access.v1.4.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.4 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused) and its database side (QA, a new task beside T-1161's own tests), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1, K-34 v1 and K-22 v1 migrated (K-22's seed gives an owner two handles). **v1.5:** `data-access.v1.5.schema.json`, contract test `tests/contract/data-access.v1.5.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.5 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused); no database side, since v1.5 adds no database call (item 44 is the consumers' mapping). **v1.6:** `data-access.v1.6.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.6 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after T-1197), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 and K-23 v1 migrated (K-23's `seed-company-two-kinds.json` gives the barbershop its kind of work; the practice's rows are written by the test: section "Fixtures"). **v1.7:** `data-access.v1.7.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.7 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after the database engineer's `openChatOnText`), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 migrated (K-22's `seed-owner-id-upload.json` gives Sam his email handle). **v1.8:** `data-access.v1.8.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.8 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after the database engineer's `sendState` read), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 and K-29 v1 migrated. **v1.9:** `data-access.v1.9.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.9 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after T-1227), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 migrated. **v1.10:** `data-access.v1.10.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.10 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after the database engineer's three reads), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 migrated. **v1.11:** `data-access.v1.11.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.11 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after the database engineer's read), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1 and K-23 v1 migrated. **v1.12:** `data-access.v1.12.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` (QA, a new task: every v1.12 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, a new task after the database engineer's reads), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1, K-22 v1, K-29 v1, K-34 v1 and K-36 v1 migrated. **v1.13:** `data-access.v1.13.schema.json`; its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` and its database side `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` (QA, T-1292: every v1.13 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused, each with its mend; each exchange through db/repo's calls), against local PostgreSQL with K-04 v1.1, K-22 v1.2 and K-36 v1 migrated (0001 to 0034).

**Not here:** the state reads (read turn state, a handle's targets, a recipient's send state, a handle's sealing key), which are v1.2 (T-072, section "v1.2" below; QUESTIONS.md M2-action-adapters.1, NQ-RT-1, NQ-RT-2); the outbox and part states and moves, which are K-07's (F3); the event kinds and the shapes of `why`, `approvedBy` and `proofs`, which are K-08's (F3).

## The calls

Every call reads and writes PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**: each one is a decision or a write ("Decision reads go to the primary, uncached": arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 536, 538). None of v1's calls is search or a report, so none reads the replica, and none goes through Hyperdrive's 60 s read cache (arch 10 line 504).

**Scope** (arch 10 line 546; K-04 v1 rule 4): `runtime` means the unscoped side K-04 names for the jobs that work across businesses by handle; `business` means the Tool API side, with the setting `rosie.business_id` (K-04 rule 4's proposal) set to the request's business for the transaction; `per write` means the transaction sets `rosie.business_id` to each write's own business before that write (`set_config(..., true)`), so a turn touching two businesses' bookings (arch 03 line 159) never reads or writes a third's, while writes with no business (sign-up before there is one, Rosie's own lines) and the tables K-04 scopes `none` (turns, handles, phones, persons, threads) go on the runtime side.

| Call                | Item                 | Scope                     | Repeating it                                                                                                         | Time budget                                                     |
| ------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `commitTurn`        | 1 commit a turn      | per write                 | Safe: the turn id is the key; a repeat writes nothing and returns the first commit (`repeated`)                      | Inside "Gate and commit ≤ 300 ms" (arch 04 line 223)            |
| `writePlan`         | 2 write a plan       | runtime                   | Safe: a row with a plan keeps it (`alreadyPlanned`)                                                                  | None in the sources                                             |
| `unplannedRows`     | 2 (after a crash)    | runtime                   | Safe: a read                                                                                                         | None in the sources                                             |
| `claimOutbox`       | 3 claim outbox rows  | runtime                   | Never hands a row twice; a repeat claims only rows still unclaimed, so a lost reply strands its rows (open gap 1)    | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" (arch 04 line 227)                          |
| `markPart`          | 3 mark a part        | runtime                   | Safe: a second receipt or "sent" for the same key changes nothing (`repeated`)                                       | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s"                                             |
| `markRow`           | 3 held, stopped, ... | runtime                   | Compare-and-set: a repeat gets `versionChanged` and changes nothing                                                  | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s"                                             |
| `armClock`          | 4 arm a clock row    | runtime                   | Safe: a later receipt never moves it; the first arming comes back (`repeated`)                                       | None in the sources                                             |
| `cancelClock`       | 4 cancel a clock row | runtime                   | Safe (`repeated`); a fired row stays fired (`alreadyFired`)                                                          | None in the sources                                             |
| `dueClocks`         | 5 due clock rows     | runtime                   | Safe: a read                                                                                                         | None in the sources                                             |
| `fireClock`         | 5 fire a clock row   | runtime, writes per write | Safe: fires once; a repeat returns the first firing (`repeated`)                                                     | As a turn's gate and commit, 300 ms (default; arch 04 line 223) |
| `reserve`           | 6 reserve            | business                  | Safe: the reservation id is the caller's; the same request returns its outcome (`repeated`), another is `idConflict` | None in the sources                                             |
| `release`           | 6 release            | business                  | Safe: a released row comes back as released (`repeated`); only the releasing call reports a promotion                | None in the sources                                             |
| `extend`            | 6 extend             | business                  | Safe: the one extension is given once (`alreadyExtended`)                                                            | None in the sources                                             |
| `readActiveBundle`  | 7 active bundle id   | runtime                   | Safe: a read                                                                                                         | None in the sources (turn start)                                |
| `readBundleValue`   | 7 a value on KV miss | runtime                   | Safe: a read                                                                                                         | None in the sources (turn start)                                |
| `publishBundleCopy` | 7 (NQ-PB-5)          | runtime                   | Safe: the same values again say `repeated`; other bytes under a key are `valueMismatch`                              | None (deploy)                                                   |
| `setActiveBundle`   | 7 (NQ-PB-5)          | runtime                   | Safe: already active says `repeated`; called only by the deploy script's step 3 (BP 5.4)                             | None (deploy)                                                   |
| `parkTurn`          | 8 park a turn        | runtime                   | Safe: a repeat says `repeated`; a committed turn is never parked (`alreadyCommitted`)                                | None in the sources                                             |

Every call can also answer `transient` (a serialization failure 40001, a deadlock 40P01 or a lost connection): nothing was written, and the same request may be sent again.

## How each call works on K-04 v1 as frozen

These are the rules the database engineer builds (C-152) and QA's contract test checks (T-084). Each call is one transaction.

1. **`commitTurn`** inserts the `turns` row as `committed` (QUESTIONS.md NQ-RT-3's default: the 60 s lease lives in the actor, so the row is written once). A unique violation on the turn id means a repeat: a committed turn returns its first commit (`turnId`, `committedAt`, its outbox rows by `turn_id` in commit order; `written` and `promoted` empty, since this call wrote nothing; K-04 keeps no copy of a result), whatever the repeat's body; a parked turn returns `turnParked`. Then, in this order: every row in `versionsRead` is read `FOR SHARE` (`FOR UPDATE` when the turn writes it) and compared, and every reservation to confirm is locked `FOR UPDATE`; a reservation that is not `held`, or is held past its `held_until`, refuses the commit with `reservationLost` (limit 5, checked before its version); any other version mismatch refuses it with `versionChanged`, listing every row that changed (limit 4: arch 06 line 305). Then the writes: state writes (an update raises `version` by one), open questions opened (status `waiting`) or closed, clock rows made (`waiting`, or `armed` with `due`) or cancelled, outbox rows with no plan (K-04's default state; `commit_seq` from its identity column, so commit order is the database's), reservations confirmed (`booked`, with the booking, and the reservation's saved-time clock row cancelled) or released (as `release` does, promotion included), and the event-log entries, each carrying the turn id, its trace id and its bundle. A database rule that refuses a write (EXCLUDE 23P01, CHECK 23514, unique 23505, foreign key 23503) rolls everything back and answers `constraint`.
2. **`writePlan`** locks the row; a row that already holds a plan answers `alreadyPlanned` (checked before its version), else the version is compared and `plan` and `final_parts` are written together (K-03 v1 `rowPlan` and `finalParts`; that the plan's `row` is this row is a code check). **`unplannedRows`** lists a turn's rows whose `plan` is null (K-04 `outbox_rows_unplanned_idx`).
3. **`claimOutbox`**, with no claim-time column on outbox rows (K-04 v1): recipients are taken one at a time, earliest open `commit_seq` first, each under a transaction-level advisory lock keyed on the recipient's handle id (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`); a recipient whose lock another claim holds is skipped, which is `SKIP LOCKED`'s meaning per recipient. Under the lock the recipient's open rows are read in `commit_seq` order `FOR UPDATE` and walked: a row held for text hours (`held_until` after `now`) is passed and reported in `passedHeld` (arch 02 line 125, DD); a row claimed earlier and not finished, or one with no final parts yet, stops the walk for that recipient (commit order); any other row is claimed: its state moves to K-07's claimed state and its version rises by one, and each part gets its row under the key `<row>/<part>/<try>` (a new part try 1 with `attempts` 1 and its K-03 send kind; a part the provider never accepted keeps its stored key with `attempts` raised: arch 11 line 576). The claim is that state move under compare-and-set, so two dispatchers never hold one row. How a claim that is never finished lapses (a dispatcher that died) is K-07's (open gap 1).
4. **`markPart`**: `sent` records the provider's ids and `sent_at` (first time only); `delivered`, found by the provider's message id, records `delivered_at` once and says `firstDelivery` when no part of the row was delivered before, so the caller arms the question's clock (arch 02 line 127); `failed` records `failed_at` and the error code, and with `retry` writes the next try's part row under a new key (arch 01 line 65); `attempt` raises `attempts` for a retry under the same key after a timeout. **`markRow`** is limit 8's outcome (arch 06 line 313): `held` sets `held_until` and returns the row to the open rows, to be claimed again after it; `droppedStopped` and `withdrawn` close it; `stale` clears `plan` and `final_parts`, so `unplannedRows` lists it and it is composed again from today's record (S13-57). The state words are K-07's.
5. **`armClock`** finds the row (or the question's `question` clock row), locks it, and moves `waiting` to `armed` with `due_at`, `due_zone` and `armed_at`; its open question moves from `waiting` to `open` in the same transaction (default 8). An armed row answers `repeated` with its first arming; a fired or cancelled one `clockClosed`. **`cancelClock`** moves a waiting or armed row to `cancelled`, and with `withdrawQuestion` withdraws its open question in the same transaction (the passed-on path: arch 02 line 123).
6. **`dueClocks`**: for a handle, its armed rows due by `now`, earliest first, and `nextDueAt`, its earliest armed row after `now`, where the actor sets its one alarm (K-04 `clock_rows_handle_due_idx`); for the sweep, armed rows no handle owns due by `now` and handle rows due before `missedBefore` (K-04 `clock_rows_due_idx`; arch 10 line 540). A read: the firing does the compare-and-set.
7. **`fireClock`** locks the row: `fired` answers `repeated` with the first firing; `waiting` or `cancelled` answers `notArmed`; a version mismatch `versionChanged`; a due time after `firedAt` answers `notDue` unless `by` is `fireNow` (staging only, BP 9.4; the Tool API allows it on staging alone, K-21). Then, as `commitTurn` does, the firing's turn row (when given) and its writes, and the row moves to `fired` with `fired_at`, all in one transaction (arch 10 line 508). Its event-log entries carry the `jobId`, and the turn id when there is a turn (K-04 `event_log_turn_or_job`). Per QUESTIONS.md NQ-RT-4's default the firing is a turn on the handle's actor whose only input is the clock row; its one event id is the clock row's id (default 9).
8. **`reserve`** runs under the reservation's business. The same reservation id with the same request answers its outcome again (`repeated`), with another request `idConflict`. An inactive resource, or one of another business, is `notBookable`. **The cap of 2 saved times** (arch 05 line 247): for a mobile, the call first locks its `phones` row `FOR UPDATE`, so two parallel reserves for one mobile run one after the other, then counts its `held` rows whose `held_until` is after `at` across every business, through a database function that runs unscoped and returns only those rows' ids and ends (row-level security would hide the other businesses'); for an agent token, which has no row in K-04, it takes a transaction-level advisory lock keyed on the business and the token, then counts the token's held rows at that business. A third is refused with `savedTimeCap` and both ends. Waiting rows never count ("they wait next in line while saving another": A4-18). A worker, room or vehicle: the held row is inserted in a savepoint with `held_until` = `at` + the door's minutes; EXCLUDE refusing it (23P01) means `overlap` if a booked row overlaps or `waitIfSaved` is false, else a `waiting` row whose `queued_at` is `at` (A4-18). A class session: `class_seats.taken` rises by `seats`; the CHECK refusing it (23514) is `seatsFull` with the capacity and seats taken. A held row gets its saved-time clock row, id `<reservation id>/saved-time`, kind `saved_time`, armed and due at `held_until` in the business's time zone (CQ-05's default), owned by the mobile's handle or, for an agent token or a booking, by the sweep, firing `{"release": <reservation id>, "why": "expired"}`.
9. **`release`** locks the row: a released row answers `repeated` with its first release; `expired` needs a held row whose `held_until` has passed (`notExpired` otherwise); else the version is compared (null for the clock's expiry). The row moves to `released` with `released_at` and `released_why`; a class session's `taken` falls by its seats; its saved-time clock row, if not fired, is cancelled. **Promotion** (A4-18; K-04 `queued_at`): when the row was held or booked, the waiting rows of the same resource whose time overlaps the released time are read `FOR UPDATE` in `queued_at` order, a tie to the lower id (compared, never parsed: CQ-09; K-04 `reservations_waiting_queue_idx`). Each in turn: its mobile's or token's cap is checked as `reserve` checks it (two held already: `skipped`, `savedTimeCap`); then, in a savepoint, it becomes `held` with `held_until` = `at` + 10 minutes (the standard saved time, S12-01; it must have an end: K-04 `reservations_held_has_end`), which EXCLUDE refuses (23P01) when another held or booked row overlaps it (`skipped`, `overlap`; it stays waiting). The first one held is promoted, with its own saved-time clock row, and the walk stops. Class sessions have no waiting rows (`reserve` answers `seatsFull`), so nothing is promoted there.
10. **`extend`** locks the row: not held, or past its end, is `notHeld`; the `ownAi` door is `neverExtended` (MC C3); `extended` already set is `alreadyExtended`; else `held_until` rises by 10 minutes, `extended` is set, and the saved-time clock row's due time moves with it (Q-12).
11. **`readActiveBundle`** reads the `bundles` row with `active` (at most one: `bundles_one_active`); **`readBundleValue`** reads `bundle_values` by key and returns the stored bytes as UTF-8 text with their size and sha256, which the reader checks (K-01 v1 `storedValue`). **`publishBundleCopy`** inserts the registry row and every value once (K-04 checks the bytes and sha256 against the value); the same bundle again with the same values answers `repeated`. **`setActiveBundle`** locks the active row, compares it with `expectActive` (`activeChanged` otherwise), clears it and sets the new one with `activated_at`; an id already active answers `repeated`; an id with no registry row answers `notPublished`. Rolling back is the same call with the earlier id (tour line 658).
12. **`parkTurn`** inserts the `turns` row as `parked` with `tries` 3 and `parked_at` (arch 11 line 576), the one "nothing changed" outbox row and its event-log entries; a parked turn answers `repeated`, a committed one `alreadyCommitted`.

## v1.1 (T-071): effect rows, provider events, and the claim

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): 02-contracts K-05 ("v1.1 at F3 adds the effect calls": claim effect rows, record an effect's result, record a provider event once by its event id; arch 02 line 109) and section 9 F3; and K-07 v1 (`../outbox-effects/README.md`), whose moves tables and call map leave these moves to "K-05 v1.1", with the database engineer's notes on K-07 (T-016's verification). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.1.schema.json` holds every v1 definition under its own name: the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.schema.json`, which stays as it is; the changed ones (`writes`, `commitResult`, `claimedRow`, `markPart.request`, `markPart.response`, `markRow.request`, `markRow.response`, the `errors` of `commitTurn`, `fireClock`, `markPart` and `markRow`, and the exchanges and responses that refer to them) take only new optional fields, new enum values or new error shapes. So every v1 exchange is a v1.1 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every v1 fixture against v1.1), and a v1.1 call always writes the new fields. Every v1.1 call is one transaction on the primary, uncached.

**The schema addition** the v1.1 calls read and write is K-07 v1's, already frozen with it: `../outbox-effects/outbox-effects.v1.tables.json` (`effects`, `provider_events`, `outbox_claims`, in the same schema as K-04's core tables, "migrated with K-05 v1.1"). It is a table list that only adds to the core part; no K-04 v1 file changes. The core part needs nothing more for v1.1 (K-04's `outbox_rows.state` and `outbox_parts.state` are text with no CHECK on their values, so K-07's `waiting_chat` and `superseded` store as they are), so there is no `core.v1.1.tables.json`. The migration is the database lane's (C-151), not this contract's.

### The calls v1.1 adds or changes

| Call                      | Item                               | Scope                     | Repeating it                                                                                  | Time budget         |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| `commitTurn`, `fireClock` | 1, 5: + effects, + waiting rows    | as v1                     | As v1                                                                                         | As v1               |
| `claimOutbox`             | 3: + the claim, + the re-claim     | runtime                   | Never hands a row twice; a lost reply's rows are claimed again once their claim lapses (60 s) | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" |
| `renewClaim`              | 3: a part about to go              | runtime                   | Safe for the holder: a repeat renews again; `firstTryAt` is set once                          | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" |
| `markPart`                | 3: + Retry-After, message id, row  | runtime                   | As v1; a repeat writes no follow-up row                                                       | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" |
| `markRow`                 | 3: + `waitingChat`                 | runtime                   | As v1 (compare-and-set)                                                                       | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" |
| `dispatcherGivesUp`       | 3: sending to failed, + follow-ups | runtime                   | Compare-and-set: a repeat gets `versionChanged` and writes nothing                            | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" |
| `writeAllSet`             | 3: the one short "all set"         | runtime                   | Safe: the row id is the key; the same id says `repeated` and writes nothing                   | None in the sources |
| `claimEffects`            | v1.1: claim effect rows            | runtime                   | Never hands a row twice; a lost reply's rows are claimed again once their claim lapses        | None in the sources |
| `recordEffectResult`      | v1.1: record an effect's result    | runtime                   | Compare-and-set: a repeat gets `versionChanged`                                               | None in the sources |
| `serviceBack`             | v1.1: outage to running            | runtime                   | As `claimEffects`                                                                             | None in the sources |
| `recordProviderEvent`     | v1.1: record a provider event once | runtime (unscoped)        | Safe: a known (provider, event id) says `repeated` and writes nothing                         | None (gateway, 1 s) |
| `reconciliationRefund`    | v1.1: the reconciliation's refund  | runtime, writes per write | Safe: the effect id is the key; the job run again says `repeated`                             | None in the sources |

The gateway answers within 1 s (arch 02 line 109), so `recordProviderEvent` sits inside it; the sources give it no budget of its own.

### How each v1.1 call works (K-04 v1 and K-07 v1 as frozen)

13. **`claimOutbox`** (v1 item 3, with K-07's claim): a claim writes or updates the row's `outbox_claims` row (`claimer`, `claimed_at` = `now`, `lapses_at` = `now` + 60 s, `claim_no` 1 or raised by one, its own `version` raised) and returns it as `claim`, with `claimedFrom`. Under the recipient's lock the walk now also takes a `sending` row whose claim has lapsed (`lapses_at` ≤ `now`), or that has no claim row (claimed before v1.1 was migrated): claimed again by compare-and-set (row version and `claim_no`), its unsent parts under their stored keys with `attempts` raised as v1 says, and `first_try_at` and `next_try_not_before` kept. A `sending` row with a live claim stops the walk, as in v1. A `waiting_chat` row is passed, as a held row is (K-07: it waits for the chat to open, not for the dispatcher), and is not listed in `passedHeld`; `planned` and `stale` rows stop the walk; finished rows (`sent`, `delivered`, `dropped_stopped`, `withdrawn`, `failed`, `superseded`) are skipped.
14. **`renewClaim`** is the dispatcher's call just before each send of a part, a first send or a retry under the same key (K-07: "renewed by 60 s at each part attempt"; `firstTryAt` "written by the dispatcher at its first send"). It locks the row and its claim: the row must be `sending` and the claim the caller's (`claimer` and `claimNo`), or `claimLost`; a lapsed claim nobody has taken again is still the caller's. The key must be a `pending` part of the row (`wrongState`). A `next_try_not_before` after `at` answers `notYet` with that time. Otherwise `lapses_at` = `at` + 60 s, `first_try_at` = `at` if null, `next_try_not_before` cleared; only the claim row's version rises, not the outbox row's.
15. **`markPart`** (v1 item 4, with K-07's moves): the part moves as K-07's `partMove` allows (`wrongState` otherwise: `sent` for a part that is not pending, a receipt for a part neither sent nor failed), and the row moves with its parts in the same transaction: the last part accepted, `sending` to `sent`; every part's latest try delivered, to `delivered` (from `sent`, or from `sending` after a 4001 or 4006); `failed` with `retry` on a part of a `sent` row, back to `checked`, so it is claimed again. Every mark that changes a part raises the row's version by one, and the answer's `row` gives the state and version, so the dispatcher compares them in its next `markRow` (the database engineer's note). `failed` may carry `providerMessageId` (stored in `linq_message_id` when the part has none, so a receipt that comes after a 4001 or 4006 finds it: K-07 open gap 13) and, with `retry`, `nextTryNotBefore`, the provider's Retry-After, stored in the claim's `next_try_not_before`. **A failed safety line** (arch 02 line 125: it "retries every 10 s for 15 minutes and goes at once every other way the person allows"; RULES 4.12; K-07 retry rule 3): with the safety line's first reported failure, `failed` carries `followUps` (`safetyOtherWay` rows, one per other way the person allows) and their `events`, and the call inserts the rows in the same transaction as the failure, as item 17 writes follow-up rows (`planned`, under the failed row's turn id, bundle and trace id; `commit_seq` from K-04's identity column), with one K-08 `outbox_row.created` entry per row whose `why` names the failed row and `safetyOtherWay` (the link K-07 open gap 3 keeps in the event log). The answer lists them in `followUpRows`. A repeat (the part already failed) writes none; a row id already taken refuses the mark with `constraint`. That the row is a safety line, and that only its first failure carries them, are code checks (C-126); its retries every 10 s carry none.
16. **`markRow`** (v1 item 4): the row must be `sending` (`wrongState` otherwise). `waitingChat` moves it to `waiting_chat`, clears `held_until` and deletes its `outbox_claims` row; no clock arms and nothing is sent (arch 06 line 313; RULES 4.1b; MC C9). The other outcomes keep the claim row, so `claim_no` keeps counting the row's claims (a held row's morning claim is claim 2: K-07 `outbox-held-text-hours`).
17. **`dispatcherGivesUp`** locks the row and its claim: `sending` (`wrongState`), the caller's claim (`claimLost`), its version (`versionChanged`), and a `first_try_at` at least 15 minutes before `at` (`tooSoon`, with the time, or null when no part has gone). The row moves to `failed`; the claim row stays as the record of the tries; parts keep their states. **In the same transaction** the rows that follow it are written (arch 02 line 125: "Sends still failing after 15 minutes of tries: the next way the person allows; none left, the owner gets the number to call"; S12-10; K-07 retry rule 5): `followUps`, each a K-07 follow-up kind and a new row as a commit writes one (`followUpRow`): `nextWay`, one row per next way the person allows (such as an email row with no `copyOfRowId`), or, with none left, one `ownerGetsNumber` row to the owner. Each is inserted `planned` with no plan under the failed row's `turn_id`, `bundle_hash`, `bundle_build` and `trace_id` (K-04 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null, and a turn row is written only for texts), with `commit_seq` from K-04's identity column, so it comes after every row committed before; `unplannedRows` lists it with that turn's rows, and it is composed and checked as any row is (`writePlan`), then claimed and sent. `events` (at least one) are appended with the failed row's turn id, trace id and bundle: the move to failed (K-08 `outbox_row.state_changed`) and one `outbox_row.created` per follow-up row whose `why` names the failed row and its follow-up kind, which is the link K-07 open gap 3 keeps in the event log. The answer lists the rows in `followUpRows`. A row id already taken refuses the call with `constraint`, and nothing is written. `followUps` is empty only for a safety line whose other ways already went at its first failure (item 15) or a row to the owner with no other way left (open gap 13). Which ways the person allows is the caller's read (K-05 v1.2's recipient send state; C-126).
18. **The commit's effects** (`commitTurn` and `fireClock`, `writes.effects`): after the state writes, each `write` is inserted into `effects` as `pending` with the turn's id (or, for a firing with no turn, the request's `jobId`), `tries` 0, `runs` 1, under its own business (per write; a contact effect, with no business, on the runtime side: K-07). Each `letGo` locks its effect, compares its version (`versionChanged`) and needs `outage` (`wrongState`), and moves it to `cancelled`. Both are listed in `written` with their versions. A unique key already taken (`effects_idempotency_key_key`) or a K-07 CHECK refuses the commit with `constraint`. The commit logs only what it writes, in `events`, as for every other write: one K-08 `effect.created` entry per effect written, and one entry per effect let go for its move outage → cancelled (`commit-lets-go-outage-effect`). A try's result is never logged by a commit: `recordEffectResult` appends it in its own transaction (item 21).
19. **The commit's waiting rows** (`writes.waitingChat`): with `supersede`, every `waiting_chat` row to `recipientHandleId` moves to `superseded`; with `withdrawForBookings` and `withdrawForQuestions`, every `waiting_chat` row whose `booking_id` or `open_question_id` is listed moves to `withdrawn`. The call finds the rows itself, `FOR UPDATE`, so none is missed, raises each version, appends one event-log entry per row (K-08 `outbox_row.state_changed`: actor rosie, old state `waiting_chat`, the new state, `why` naming the answer row or the closed booking or question; id `<row id>/superseded` or `<row id>/withdrawn`), and lists them in `commitResult.waitingChat`. That the answer row is one of this commit's `outboxRows` to that handle is a code check. The event-log entry is the link K-07 open gap 3 asks for until a column holds it.
20. **`claimEffects`** takes, oldest `created_at` first (`effects_claimable_idx`), `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, `pending` rows and `running` rows whose `claim_lapses_at` ≤ `now`, of the listed `ports` or any. A pending row starts its run: `running`, `first_try_at` = `now`, `tries` 0. A lapsed running row keeps `tries`, `runs` and `first_try_at`. Both get `claimed_by` and `claim_lapses_at` = `now` + 60 s, and a raised version. Rows in `outage` wait for `serviceBack`. It takes no event-log entries: it picks its rows itself (`SKIP LOCKED`), so the caller cannot name them beforehand, and K-08 v1 has no kind for a claim (only `effect.created` and `effect.result`); the run it starts shows in each result's entry (item 21: old state `running` with its tries and runs). An entry for the claim itself is open gap 15.
21. **`recordEffectResult`** locks the effect: `running` (`wrongState`), the caller's claim (`claimLost`), its version (`versionChanged`). `tries` rises by one and `last_try_at` = `at`. `ok`: `succeeded`, with `provider_object_id` and `ledger_line_id`. `declined`: `declined` with `error_code`, never tried again (RULES 3.11). `networkError`: `error_code`; the run's 5th try moves it to `outage`, else it stays `running` with `claim_lapses_at` = `nextTryAt` + 60 s (`at` + 60 s when `nextTryAt` is null). Leaving `running` clears `claimed_by` and `claim_lapses_at` (K-07 `effects_running_is_claimed`). **Every result is logged in the same transaction** (arch 09 lines 457, 492: every write appends who, what, old → new, when, why): `events` is required, at least one K-08 `effect.result` entry for the effect (actor `job`, the effects runner; old and new state, tries, runs and error; `why` the try's outcome, and on the run's 5th failed try the service's outage), appended with the effect's turn id, or its job id when it has none, its trace id, and the turn's bundle (null for a job) (K-04 `event_log_turn_or_job`). The runner's span for the call is K-08's `effectRun`, not an entry here.
22. **`serviceBack`** takes the port's `outage` rows, oldest first (`effects_outage_idx`), `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`: `running`, `runs` + 1, `tries` 0, `first_try_at` = `now`, claimed as `claimEffects` claims. Each runs once more on its same key (arch 11 line 582). Like `claimEffects` it takes no event-log entries (open gap 15); the new run shows in each result's entry (`runs` raised, `tries` from 0).
23. **`recordProviderEvent`** runs on the runtime side, unscoped (the gateway may not know the business, and row-level security would hide another business's row from the conflict's read: the database engineer's note): `insert ... on conflict (provider, provider_event_id) do nothing`; on a conflict it reads the stored row and answers `repeated` with its `receivedAt` and `effectId`, whatever the repeat's body. It writes nothing else; `handled_at` stays null (open gap 11).
24. **`reconciliationRefund`** inserts one `stripe_refund` effect, `pending`, with `job_id` and `booking_id` null (K-07 default 12), under the refund's business, and its event-log entries with the job id (K-04 `event_log_turn_or_job`). The same effect id again answers `repeated` with the stored version and writes nothing; another effect id with a taken idempotency key is `constraint` (23505).
25. **`writeAllSet`** writes the one short "all set" (arch 02 line 125: "once texts work, one short 'all set'"; S12-10: "Once texts work, a short 'all set' goes out. Nothing already emailed is sent again in full"). **Who:** the dispatcher (C-126), once it finds texts to the person work again. **When** texts count as working the sources do not say (K-07 open gap 12): open gap 14 here, so the call carries no rule for it. It locks each of `failedRowIds` (each must be `failed`: `wrongState`, or `notFound`) and inserts `row`, a text row (never email), `planned` with no plan under the first failed row's turn id, bundle and trace id, as item 17 writes follow-up rows, with its `events` (K-08 `outbox_row.created`, `why` naming the failed rows it closes). The row's id is fixed by rule, `<first failed row id>/all-set`, so one failure gets one "all set": the same id again answers `repeated` with the stored row and writes nothing. That the failed rows are this person's and went another way is a code check. K-07 gives the "all set" no follow-up kind of its own, so it is an ordinary row.

### K-07 v1's call map, covered

| K-07 v1 need ("K-05 v1.1")                                                     | v1.1                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The claim's write, renewal and re-claim in `outbox_claims`                     | `claimOutbox` `claim` (13), `renewClaim` (14); fixtures `claim-dee`, `renew-claim-*`          |
| A lapsed `sending` row claimed again, keeping `firstTryAt`, `nextTryNotBefore` | `claimOutbox` (13); `claim-lapsed-reclaimed`                                                  |
| The claim query skips `waiting_chat` rows (database note)                      | `claimOutbox` (13)                                                                            |
| `markPart` failed with the Retry-After and the provider's message id           | `markPart` (15); `mark-failed-retry-after`                                                    |
| `markPart` moves the row with its parts and raises its version                 | `markPart` `row` (15)                                                                         |
| A wrong-state error for `markRow` and `markPart`                               | `wrongStateError` (15, 16; also 14, 17, 18, 21); `mark-row-wrong-state`                       |
| `dispatcherGivesUp`: sending to failed                                         | `dispatcherGivesUp` (17); `dispatcher-gives-up`, `dispatcher-gives-up-too-soon`               |
| The next way after a failed row (`followUps` `nextWay`; retry rule 5)          | `dispatcherGivesUp` `followUps` (17); `dispatcher-gives-up`                                   |
| None left, the owner gets the number (`ownerGetsNumber`; retry rule 5)         | `dispatcherGivesUp` `followUps` (17); `dispatcher-gives-up-owner-number`                      |
| A failed safety line every other way at once (`safetyOtherWay`; rule 3)        | `markPart` failed `followUps` (15); `mark-safety-failed-other-way`                            |
| The one short "all set" once texts work (gap 12: an ordinary row)              | `writeAllSet` (25); `write-all-set`, `write-all-set-not-failed`                               |
| The link from a row to the rows that followed it (gap 3: the event log)        | `outbox_row.created` entries naming the failed row (15, 17, 25)                               |
| `markRow`'s `waitingChat`                                                      | `markRow` (16); `mark-waiting-chat`                                                           |
| The commit's moves of `waiting_chat` rows to `superseded` or `withdrawn`       | `writes.waitingChat` (19); `commit-supersedes-waiting`, `commit-withdraws-waiting`            |
| Effects written by `commitTurn`, or by `fireClock` with no turn                | `writes.effects.write` (18); `commit-writes-effect`                                           |
| `claimEffects`, and a lapsed effect claim taken again                          | `claimEffects` (20); `claim-effects`, `claim-effects-lapsed`                                  |
| `recordEffectResult`: ok, network error, decline, the 5th try's outage         | `recordEffectResult` (21); `effect-result*`, `effect-try-2` to `4`, `effect-fifth-try-outage` |
| `serviceBack`: outage to running, once                                         | `serviceBack` (22); `service-back`                                                            |
| `letGo`: outage to cancelled                                                   | `writes.effects.letGo` (18); `commit-lets-go-outage-effect`                                   |
| `reconciliationJob`: the refund with no turn                                   | `reconciliationRefund` (24); `reconciliation-refund`                                          |
| A provider event recorded once by (provider, event id), unscoped               | `recordProviderEvent` (23); `provider-event-recorded`, `provider-event-duplicate`             |

## v1.2 (T-072): the state reads, and what the builds since have shown

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): QUESTIONS.md M2-action-adapters.1's default (the frontier and the services read stored state through "read turn state", primary and uncached, under the business scope, added to K-05 as an additive minor version), NQ-RT-1's ("get or create a handle's sealing key") and NQ-RT-2's ("a handle's targets"; "a recipient's send state"), which the DAG gives to T-072; and, each additive and from a frozen source, what the builds since have shown (DECISIONS.md DL-103 (5), DL-104): limit 8's reads (T-259), the claimed row's port (K-07 v1 `outboxRow.port`), `markPart` delivered by the part's key (K-11 v1 README "A late receipt after 4001 or 4006"; T-257's open point), the stopped flag read with the renewal (T-259's window), and the claim entries K-08 v1.1 made a kind for (CQ-43; T-1077). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.2.schema.json` holds every v1.1 definition under its own name: the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.1.schema.json` (which refers on to v1), both files as they are; the changed ones (`given`, `claimedRow`, `markPart.request`, `renewClaim.response`, `claimedEffect`, and the responses and exchanges that refer to them) take only new optional fields. So every v1 and v1.1 exchange is a v1.2 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every v1 and v1.1 fixture against v1.2), and a v1.2 call always writes the new fields. There is no schema change: the reads read K-04 v1's core tables and K-29 v1's `consents`, and `handleSealingKey` writes only K-04 rows (item 29).

### The calls v1.2 adds or changes

| Call                          | Item                             | Scope                                                     | Repeating it                                   | Time budget                                          |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| `readTurnState`               | 26: read turn state              | business: the request's (none: runtime, no-business rows) | Safe: a read                                   | Inside "Build the frontier", 150 ms (arch 04 l. 215) |
| `handleTargets`               | 27: a handle's targets           | runtime (the guard: K-04 v1 rule 4)                       | Safe: a read                                   | None in the sources                                  |
| `sendState`                   | 28: a recipient's send state     | runtime (the dispatcher: K-04 v1 rule 4)                  | Safe: a read                                   | Inside "Send ≤ 1 s" (arch 04 line 227)               |
| `handleSealingKey`            | 29: get or create a handle's key | runtime (every table it touches is scoped none)           | Safe: a second call finds what the first wrote | Inside the gateway's 1 s (arch 02 line 109)          |
| `claimOutbox`                 | 30: + the row's port             | runtime                                                   | As v1.1                                        | As v1.1                                              |
| `markPart`                    | 31: + delivered by the part key  | runtime                                                   | As v1.1                                        | As v1.1                                              |
| `renewClaim`                  | 32: + the phone's flags          | runtime                                                   | As v1.1                                        | As v1.1                                              |
| `claimEffects`, `serviceBack` | 33: + one claim entry per row    | runtime                                                   | As v1.1; each claim its own entry              | As v1.1                                              |

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, and can answer `transient`, as every K-05 call does. **Scope** (arch 10 line 546): `readTurnState` is a Tool API read, so it runs under one business's scope; the guard's, the dispatcher's and the gateway's reads run on the runtime side, which K-04 v1 rule 4 names for "the jobs that work across businesses by handle (the actor's guard, the dispatcher, the clock sweep)" (K-29 v1 rule 4: "the guard and limit 8 read consents unscoped"), and each business-scoped row they return names its business, so every read that follows runs under that business's scope (default 25).

### How each v1.2 call works (K-04 v1, K-07 v1 and K-29 v1 as frozen)

26. **`readTurnState`** (the frontier's stored state: arch 02 line 117; arch 03 lines 157 to 175; QUESTIONS.md M2-action-adapters.1) is one read-only transaction under the request's business: it sets `rosie.business_id` to `businessId` (K-04 rule 4's setting) and reads, as one moment: the handle itself (`handles`: kind and address, K-02 v1's `writer.handle`, read for an email handle and a first text too); the handle's people (`person_handles`, `persons`: id, language, version) and their live roles at this business (`person_roles`, `ended_at` null); the handle's phone row (`phones`, null for an email handle or a number whose first turn has not written one); every live open question sent to the handle (`open_questions`, status `waiting` or `open`, by `handle_id`: K-04 `open_questions_handle_live_idx`) at this business, with when it went out and the poll it went out as (K-02 v1 `openQuestion.sentAt` and `pollId`), from the outbox rows that ask it and go to that same handle (`outbox_rows.open_question_id`, with `recipient_handle_id` the question's `handle_id`, under the same scope: K-02's "sent to this handle", so an email copy to another handle never counts) and their parts that have gone. **A part has gone when it is `sent` or `delivered`, never while `pending` and never once `failed`** (K-07 v1's part states): `sentAt` is the latest time such a part went, its `sent_at`, or its `delivered_at` when Linq's acceptance was never recorded (a 4001 or 4006 that delivered anyway, K-07's failed → delivered: item 31; `read-turn-state-delivered-after-4006`); `pollId` is the `linq_message_id` of the latest such part of kind `poll`, which a vote names (K-06 v1 `poll.pollId`), so a poll that failed after Linq accepted it, whose options go again as plain text, gives none (K-02: null when it went out as text; K-07 `outbox-poll-failed-plain`; `targets-poll-failed-plain`); each null until a part has gone (default 41). K-04's note on `outbox_parts` (scoped none) says the dispatcher alone reads it, never a Tool API call: this read is a new reader, so it takes only the columns these two need (`state`, `kind`, `sent_at`, `delivered_at`, `linq_message_id`), through the outbox rows the scope shows, and the database engineer confirms it (T-219); the requested booking heads with their versions and the booking flags private and deceased; the open pauses (`cleared_at` null) on the handle's people or its phone at this business (K-04 `pauses_open_person_idx`, `pauses_open_phone_idx`); the consent rows of the handle's people at this business (K-29 `consents_person_kind_at_idx`), without their exact words, which stay in the row (`wordsSha256` names them; arch 09 line 492); and each requested fact. **The record flags** (arch 03 line 175) are the phone's `stopped`, the bookings' `private` and `deceased`, and the pauses. A STOP or START row has no business, so no business scope reads it (K-29 rule 4): its effect reaches the turn as the phone's `stopped`. **A fact** is named by the caller with its key and its one home, a table, a row's primary key as text and a column (which home a key has is the frontier's and the bundle's: C-075); the call checks the table and column against the frozen table lists and refuses any other with `unknownFact`, so a read is never built from the request's words alone, reads only tables whose rows carry a version (a fact is compared and set by it: limit 4), and returns the value (as K-04's seed fixtures write values) with the row's version. **No column of `sealing_keys` is ever a fact** (rule 7; arch 01 line 93: deleting the key erases the data, so no copy of it may outlive the row): the call answers `unknownFact` for any of them, and the schema refuses one in a request and in an answer (`factTable`; `refuse-read-turn-state-sealing-key-fact`, `refuse-read-turn-state-returns-sealing-key`), so no key, wrapped or not, reaches the Tool API, the frontier, the model's context (K-02 facts) or a gap case's log (arch 01 line 95), under any business scope or on the runtime side. **Nor is a secret's hash, or any column of a table only Rosie's team reads** (rule 7; the security reviewer's N3 on round 2): K-22 v1's `proof_codes.code_hash` (a proof code's keyed hash; the code itself is stored nowhere), `sessions.token_sha256` and `sessions.device_sha256`, which no Tool API call reads or searches (K-22 rule 5); and the tables no business may read, its owner included, since the Tool API has no `SELECT` on them: K-34 v1's `harm_reports` (its rule 4), K-36 v1's `team_pages` and `team_page_recipients` (its rule 2) and K-37 v1's `parked_turns`, `dead_letters`, `gap_cases`, `review_items` and `live_turn_labels` (its rule 2). Each of these tables carries a version, so only this rule keeps them out: the call answers `unknownFact` for one, and the schema refuses one in a request and in an answer (`factTable`, `secretColumn`; `refuse-read-turn-state-session-token-fact`, `refuse-read-turn-state-returns-harm-report`), on the runtime side too (`businessId` null), where no grant or row-level security stands between the read and the row. A booking or a fact's row that the scope hides or that does not exist answers `notFound` with the first one missing, and nothing is returned (another business's row: `read-turn-state-other-business`). **One business per call:** a turn about two businesses' bookings reads each in its own call (arch 03 line 159: choices about other bookings are labeled by business), so no call ever reads a third's. **No business yet** (sign-up, S1-01): `businessId` null runs the read on the runtime side and returns, of the business-scoped tables, only rows whose business is null (no role, no booking, no pause; open questions and consent rows with none), as K-05 v1's scope puts writes with no business on the runtime side; the rows K-04 scopes none (the handle's people, its phone) are read as always. Lists are in id order (K-04 `open_questions` has no time column; ids are compared, never parsed: CQ-09), consents by `at` then id, bookings and facts in the request's order.
27. **`handleTargets`** (the guard: arch 02 line 115, "It then lists the handle's roles, open questions and bookings"; NQ-RT-2) runs on the runtime side and returns, across every business: the handle's people (one handle can be several people: arch 01 line 55) with their live roles per business; every live open question sent to the handle, with its options as sent, versions read, send time and poll id (as item 26 reads them), so the frontier can offer other bookings' options labeled by business (arch 03 line 159); the bookings whose booker or person served is one of the handle's people (K-04 `bookings_booker_idx`, `bookings_served_idx`: "The guard lists a handle's bookings"), in every stage, since the stage picks the hub (arch 03 line 163); and the phone's stopped and chat flags. All lists empty is the doors (arch 03 line 165); the guard maps the answer to the K-09 draft's `guardTargets`.
28. **`sendState`** (limit 8 at send: arch 06 line 313; NQ-RT-2; T-259) runs on the runtime side, takes only the outbox row's id and reads everything else from the row itself (`refuse-send-state-caller-versions`), in one transaction: the row's state and version; its recipient's handle (kind and address) and person; the phone row (stopped, chat open, the Linq contact `linqContactAddedAt`, the service Linq reports) and the handle's chat thread with Linq's chat id (K-04 `threads`, kind `chat`), which together give the chat a send goes to; the newest texts consent row for that contact that covers the row's business, its business's or every business's (K-29 `consents_handle_kind_at_idx`: "limit 8's texts check read a contact's newest texts row"), which for an email contact is the stop record, since only a phone carries the flag; the **audience** for text hours (RULES 4.3), from the recipient person's live roles at the row's business (default 31); the **zone**, the visit's, else the business's (CQ-05), where `visitZone` is null until K-30 freezes, so it is the business's (K-05 v1 default 4); the business's H04 **text hours**; the staging test phone's **override** (BP 9.4; QUESTIONS.md M3-M3b.4: the release flag `staging.text-hours-override` at scope `phone:<number>`, as K-04's `seed-flag` writes it), which the caller honours on staging only; each row of the outbox row's `versions_read` at its **version now**, in that order, a row gone left out (limit 8 counts it changed); the row's booking (stage, the person served, version) and open question (status, version), with **closed** true for a booking done or cancelled and a question answered, defaulted or withdrawn (default 32); and **the record flags the dispatcher checks** (arch 03 line 175: "checked by the gate, the dispatcher ..."): the booking's `private` (A3-50: only the person served, those they added and the business are reached) and `deceased` (A7-81: reminders and sales texts stop), and **paused**, the open pauses (`cleared_at` null) at the row's business on its recipient person or on the recipient's phone, in id order (K-04 `pauses_open_person_idx`, `pauses_open_phone_idx`: "The gate and the dispatcher check a person's pause"; default 42). What limit 8 does with it, and what each flag holds back, is C-125's: T-259 built limit 8.
29. **`handleSealingKey`** (get or create a handle's sealing key: NQ-RT-1; arch 02 line 109; arch 01 line 93) runs on the runtime side in one transaction, under a transaction-level advisory lock keyed on the handle's address (as `reserve` locks an agent token: item 8), so parallel first sights of one number make one handle, one person and one key (T-220: 50 parallel calls, exactly one key). On K-04 v1 as frozen a key is a person's (`sealing_keys` by `person_id`), so the handle's key is a key of one of its people. The call finds the handle by its address (`handles_address_key`), or writes it with `newHandleId`; finds its people (`person_handles`), or, at first sight, writes a person with no name yet (`persons.name` "null until known") with `newPersonId` and their link; then returns the oldest held key among the handle's people (`sealing_keys.created_at`, a tie to the lower person id), else makes a key for the first of them with none (the lower person id): a random 256-bit data key, wrapped under the Workers secret `wrappingKeyName` names, as T-182's `db/repo/sealing.ts` stores keys (NQ-DB-1's default: one environment key wraps every data key). **Every person on the handle has had their key erased** (RULES 4.10; the row stays, `wrapped_key` null): the call writes, as at first sight, a person with no name with `newPersonId`, its `person_handles` link to the handle and that person's new key, and returns it (`created.person` and `created.key` true; `handle-key-erased`), so the number's next text, a stop in other words, START, HELP or a danger text included (RULES 7.2, 4.12; arch 02 line 111), is sealed and reaches a turn rather than meeting a 503 for good (open gap 18, closed). The erased keys stay erased and nothing of theirs is unsealed; the call removes nothing, so the erased person keeps their link (K-04: `person_handles` rows are added or removed, never updated; whether the erasure job also removes it is C-156's: open gap 28). **The key never appears in an exchange** (rule 7; `refuse-handle-key-with-key-value`): the request names the secret, never its value; the response says what K-04's row says (the wrapping secret's name, when the key was made, its version), never its bytes, wrapped or not; and the call hands the caller the data key beside the response, unwrapped as a key object for AES-256-GCM that cannot be exported (T-182's form), which the gateway seals the event with (T-243). "Linked to the person once known" (NQ-RT-1) is `person_handles`: a handle's people are its link to persons, and a person known elsewhere who turns out to write from this handle is linked to it there (identity is K-22's); the key that seals the handle's texts stays the oldest held one.
30. **`claimOutbox`** (v1.1 item 13): each claimed row also carries **`port`**, K-07 v1's `outboxRow.port` "in K-05's names": `linq` for the imessage, rcs and sms channels (Linq's own services on Rosie's one number: DL-031), `email` for the email channel. K-04 `outbox_rows` holds no port column; the claim gives it from the channel, as K-07 ties them, so the schema refuses any other pairing and any SMS port (`refuse-claimed-row-sms-port`; DL-012). Limit 8 refuses a send by any other port before it reads anything (T-259); a `claimedRow` with no `port` is a v1 or v1.1 answer.
31. **`markPart` delivered** (v1 item 4, v1.1 item 15) may also carry **`idempotencyKey`**, the part's key the receipt echoes (K-11 `partKey`: Linq echoes it on `message.sent` and `message.delivered`; the gateway hands it on beside the K-06 event). The part is found by the receipt's message id first (K-04 `outbox_parts_linq_message_idx`); when no part holds it and the key is given, the part under that key is the receipt's if it holds no message id of its own (a 4001 or 4006 gave none: K-11 `error-4006.json`), and the receipt's message id is stored on it, so a later receipt finds it the first way; a part under the key that holds another message id is not the receipt's (`notFound`). The part then moves as K-07 allows, `failed` to `delivered` for "a 4001 or 4006 that delivered anyway", and the row with it (item 15). On SMS, Linq's `message.sent` is the receipt (arch 02 line 127), so the same holds for it; `sent` already names its part by key. A receipt always carries its message id (`refuse-mark-delivered-key-only`).
32. **`renewClaim`** (v1.1 item 14) also reads the recipient's phone row (by the row's recipient handle) in its own transaction, `FOR SHARE`, and returns it as **`recipient`**: stopped, chat open and the row's version (null for an email handle). `FOR SHARE` waits for a STOP whose commit is writing that phone row, so the renewal sees it. It refuses nothing for a stopped phone: what a stop allows (a safety line, a stop reply) is limit 8's rule, C-125's, and the marker of a stop reply is still open (CQ-55 (2)). A deadlock with such a commit answers `transient`, as every call may.
33. **`claimEffects` and `serviceBack`** (v1.1 items 20 and 22) append one K-08 v1.1 **`effect.claimed`** entry per row they claim, in the same transaction, and return it as `claimedEffect.event` (CQ-43; closes open gap 15). The call writes the entry itself, since it picks its rows (`SKIP LOCKED`) and the caller cannot name them: id `<effect id>/claimed/<the effect's version after the claim>`, so each claim of a row has its own; actor `job` (default 24); table `effects` and the effect id; old and new values as K-08 v1.1 names them (`state` always; `claimed_by` and `claim_lapses_at`; `first_try_at` for a run that starts; `tries` and `runs` for a run after an outage), so `old_values.state` names the state it was claimed from; `why` null, since K-08 v1's `why` (a step, a choice, a limit, a safe default, an outage path or a provider event, nothing else: `event-trace.v1.schema.json` `/$defs/why`) has nothing a claim could name, as K-08 v1.1's `event-effect-claimed-*` rows carry none; nothing sealed; appended with the effect's turn id, or its job id when it has none, its trace id and the turn's bundle (null for a job), as a result's entry is (item 21; K-08 default 20), in the effect's business's zone (null, UTC, for an effect with no business).

### The stop and the send (T-259's window)

A STOP can commit at any moment, and the part's send is a call to Linq outside the database, so nothing inside the database can close the window between the last read of the stopped flag and Linq's acceptance of the part: holding a lock on the phone row across the send would hold a transaction open across a network call and stop the STOP's own commit, which must go at once (RULES 7.2). What v1.2 does: the last read before a send is the renewal itself (item 32): the dispatcher renews just before each part goes, reads the stopped flag in that same transaction, `FOR SHARE`, so a STOP that is committing is waited for and seen, and sends with no other read between (T-259's re-read before `sendPart` becomes the renewal's). **What is left:** a STOP that commits after the renewal's read and before Linq accepts the part lets that one part out. An exact opt-out word (STOP, UNSUBSCRIBE, OPTOUT, CANCEL, END, QUIT) is blocked by Linq itself, 403 with code 2024, on every send after it (arch 10 line 524), so what can still go is one part after a stop in other words, which a turn decides and commits inside that gap of milliseconds; the next part's renewal reads the stop (open gap 21).

### What v1.2 leaves for later minor versions

Each waits for its part to freeze, and is listed in the open gaps (16): K-22's `rosie.person_id` per call and a runtime read of a person's live sessions (T-031, in review); `serviceBack` by calendar link (K-27); a `markRow` move for a refused SMS-port row (it needs a K-07 state; CQ-55 (1)); and a commit with no turn (CQ-44). Four more wait for the task that needs them to ask (open gaps 20, 25 to 27): the last 6 messages; a booking's date and time for K-02's `target.visitAt`; K-34's open safety holds; and a booking's reservations and outage effects with their versions.

## v1.3 (T-1137): the walking skeleton's three action-layer reads

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-315's round 1 NEED, items 4 to 6 (`ops/cards/T-315.md`), carded by DL-319. The Tool API's turn face cannot bind three ports, because no K-05 call reads what they need: the commit's `openHolds` (v1.2 open gap 26: no read of K-34's holds); compose input's and finalize's `readRow` (QUESTIONS.md CQ-151 (a): K-04's `outbox_rows` has no step or line column, and the words a row answers are sealed); and the fixed-line inserter's `readFixedLines` (no call answers "Rosie texted this handle about this business before" inside the business scope, and T-220's `sendState` runs only on the runtime side). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.3.schema.json` holds every v1.2 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.2.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.1 and v1), all three files as they are, and changes none. So every v1, v1.1 and v1.2 exchange is a v1.3 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.3). **No schema change:** the reads read K-04 v1's and K-34 v1's tables as migrated (0001 to 0031), with the grants they hold. What they would need more is named in open gaps 29 to 39, each with the K-04 minor version and migration task it needs. **Not here:** the database calls (T-1138, `db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`); binding them in the turn face (T-1141); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), which are K-05 v1.8 (DL-344, DL-364, DL-391 and DL-438: v1.4 is T-1160's safety reads, v1.5 T-1184's sign-up reading, v1.6 T-1196's sensitive switch, v1.7 T-1211's open chat).

### The calls v1.3 adds

| Call                 | Item | Port it serves                                                                                                   | Scope and role                                                                                                          | Repeating it | Time budget                                          |
| -------------------- | ---- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| `readOpenHolds`      | 34   | the commit's `openHolds` (`actions/src/commit/types.ts` `OpenHolds`; C-087)                                      | business: the call's, as `rosie_app`; with none, the runtime side, rows with no business                                | Safe: a read | Inside "Gate and commit ≤ 300 ms" (arch 04 line 223) |
| `readOutboxRow`      | 35   | compose input's and finalize's `readRow` (`ComposeRowRead`, `RowRead`; C-090, C-093)                             | business: the call's, as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()`; with none, the runtime side, rows with no business | Safe: a read | None in the sources                                  |
| `readFixedLineFacts` | 36   | the fixed-line inserter's `readFixedLines` (`actions/src/finalize/fixed-lines/types.ts` `FixedLineFacts`; C-089) | business: the call's, as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()`; with none, the runtime side, rows with no business | Safe: a read | None in the sources                                  |

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, in one read-only transaction (one moment). Besides the scope, every read of a business-scoped table names the business, as item 26 does, so even a role that row-level security does not bind sees no other business's row. Ids are compared byte by byte, never parsed (CQ-09), and every call can answer `transient`. Each request states its business, or null before there is one, so no read runs with no scope by omission (STATE note 22; CQ-70; `refuse-open-holds-no-scope`). No answer carries a sealed value, the words of a text, a handle's address, a phone number, a key or a wrapping key's name (rule 7).

### How each v1.3 call works (K-04 v1 and K-34 v1 as frozen)

34. **`readOpenHolds`** (what is open on the turn's handle at commit time: QUESTIONS.md CQ-65 with DL-119, DL-127, DL-128 and DL-133; arch 06 lines 315 to 329; closes open gap 26). **Role and scope:** with a business it runs as the Tool API's role, `rosie_app`, inside the call's own transaction under that business's scope. `safety_holds` and `safety_outcomes` are business-scoped, and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on both (0016), so the read needs no runtime-only table and no key. With `businessId` null it runs on the runtime side and reads only rows with no business (default 25). It answers `notFound` (table `businesses`) when the business does not exist or its own scope does not show it, so it never answers "nothing open" under a business that is not there. **`holds`:** every open hold on the handle (`safety_holds` with `released_at` null: `safety_holds_open_handle_idx`), in `held_at` order, a tie to the lower id. Each says what it holds back: `holdId`, `releaseRule` and `blocksBooking`, under the names and values of K-09 v1's `safetyHold` (`recordSafetyOutcome.response.hold`, titled `SafetyHold`, which takes no other field), so the adapter cuts each hold to those three and the commit's hold rules read them unchanged (`actions/src/commit/hold.ts` `letsThrough`); and where it came from: `safetyOutcomeId`, `bookingId`, `turnId`, `heldAt` and `version`. Never the outcome's labels, tenses or sealed words (`refuse-open-holds-hold-with-words`). A released hold is not open (default 45). **`unreadEventIds`:** of the request's `eventIds`, each that no `safety_outcomes` row the scope shows names (`safety_outcomes_event_key`), in the request's order. The caller names the turn's texts whose `recordSafetyOutcome` is not among this commit's core actions (an outcome that rides in the commit is written by it), and every id the runtime carries as unread (T-1088's `CommitContext`; DL-133). `OpenHolds.unreadEventIds` is this list, and while it is not empty the commit holds every part but a protective one (`hold.ts` `isHeld`). A text whose safety row is another business's, or has no business, is not seen under a business scope, so it counts as unread: the read fails closed (default 44; `read-open-holds-other-business`). Holds of other businesses on the handle, and those with no business once a business is named, reach the commit through the runtime's unscoped read (default 43). The read and `commitTurn` are two transactions (open gap 32).

35. **`readOutboxRow`** (an outbox row as compose input and finalize read it: arch 07 line 397; K-09 v1 `composeInput`, `finalizeMessage`). **Role and scope:** with a business it runs as `rosie_job` inside the call's transaction, with `rosie.business_id` set to the business and confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()` before the first read (STATE note 22; T-1094's note N2), since it also reads tables only the runtime side holds (`turns`, `persons`, `handles`, `phones`, `outbox_parts`: 0012), as `readTurnState` does (item 26). With `businessId` null it runs on the runtime side and reads only a row with no business. A row of another business, or no such row, is `notFound` (table `outbox_rows`). A row of send class `safety` answers `fixedLineRow` and nothing else (arch 07 line 347: "Safety lines stay fixed text"; STATE note 17; default 49; `read-outbox-row-safety-line`). Otherwise the read returns:
    - **`row`**, what `outbox_rows` holds that the two ports read: turn, bundle, state, version, channel, send class, booking, question, the text row an email copy takes its plan from, whether it holds a plan (never the plan), and its final parts;
    - **`line`**, the step and line the row carries to its reader, from the question it asks (`open_questions.step`, line 0: CQ-151 (a)'s default 2; `from` `openQuestion`), or null for a row that asks no question (open gap 29; `read-outbox-row-no-question`);
    - **`recipient`**, its handle's kind, its person with the name and language K-04 stores, the person's live roles at the row's business in role id order, and its phone's flags (stopped, chat open, chat way, first poll vote, version); never the address (`refuse-outbox-row-returns-address`);
    - **`answers`**, the turn that committed the row (`turns`): the handle its texts came from, and each text's event id in turn order with its safety result (`safety_outcomes.outcomes` and `tenses`, null when the scope shows no row, and for a clock turn's one event id, its clock row's: default 9); never the words (`refuse-outbox-row-returns-inbound-words`);
    - **`slotSource`**, the record K-04 v1 holds for the row's slots: the business head (name, display name, zone, version), the booking head with its flags (v1.2 `bookingHead`), and the question it asks with its options as sent, Clock line, ask count and clock row (kind `question`: state, due time and zone, version), each null when the row has none;
    - **`openQuestions`**, the other live questions sent to the recipient's handle at the row's business that have gone (item 26's rule, default 41), each with the words part of the newest asking row's final parts (arch 07 line 389: "its words as sent"), in id order.

    **The words a row answers** (the card's question to the architect, for the security reviewer's review): **they are handed in by the runtime caller, never opened by this read or by any Tool API path** (default 47). The actor opens a turn's sealed texts under the handle's key at its turn (C-046, `runtime/src/actor/inbox.ts`; v1.2's consumer note), and it already hands their words to the Tool API in K-09's turn-face requests (`buildFrontier.request.texts`; `gateAndCommit`'s `context.texts`), so the turn face holds them for its turn. Its compose-input adapter (T-1141) gives the composer the words of each `answers.texts` event id it holds, only in a reply to their own sender (`answers.handleId` equals `recipient.handleId`: `ComposeRow.inbound.fromHandleId`; arch 07 line 397), and no inbound text otherwise (K-03 `inbound` null). The reasons:
    1. Least privilege, held by what each read selects, not by a grant. Opening words needs a person's key (`sealing_keys`) and the wrapping key, a Workers secret (T-182). Items 35 and 36 run as `rosie_job`, which holds `SELECT` on `messages`, `safety_outcomes`, `handles`, `phones` and `sealing_keys` (0012, 0016), and the Tool API runs in the one Worker that holds every secret, the wrapping key included (`wrangler.jsonc`: one `main`), so neither the role nor the secrets keep the words out of these reads. The barrier is each read's column list and the schema's refusals: T-1138's SQL for the three reads names none of `messages.words`, `safety_outcomes.sealed_words`, `handles.address`, `phones.number` or any `sealing_keys` column, and its tests check that no statement of them names one; and every object v1.3's answers define is closed (`additionalProperties` false), so the schema refuses an answer that carries an inbound text's words, an address or a number (`refuse-outbox-row-returns-inbound-words`, `refuse-outbox-row-returns-address`, `refuse-fixed-line-facts-returns-number`). So no K-05 read returns anyone's words, and a fault in compose input or finalize exposes at most the words the actor handed in for that turn.
    2. Nothing new is exposed: the words already reach the Tool API in the turn's own requests.
    3. The words never pass through a K-05 exchange, a log or a trace (K-08), and an erased person's words stay unopened, since the actor cannot open them either (RULES 4.10).
    4. It is v1.2's rule for keys (default 27: never in an exchange) applied to what they seal.

    What is lost: a compose rerun after the actor has let a turn's texts go gets no inbound text, so the reply is composed from the master line and the facts alone. The conversation (`ComposeRow.conversation`) waits on the same rule (open gap 33).

    **Where each port field comes from** (T-1141 binds the ports from this table; nothing else is read for them):

    | Port field                                                                                                          | From                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
    | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    | `ComposeRow` `outboxRowId`, `turnId`, `state`, `channel`, `copyOfRowId`, `bundleId`, `hasPlan`, `recipientHandleId` | `outboxRowId`, `row`, `recipient.handleId`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
    | `FinalizeRow` `outboxRowId`, `version`, `state`, `channel`, `finalParts`                                            | `outboxRowId`, `row`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
