    | `fixedLineId` (both)                                                                                                | null for every row this read answers; a safety row answers `fixedLineRow`, which the adapter refuses as the handlers refuse a `fixedLineId` (open gap 38)                                                                                                                            |
    | `step`, `line` (both: `FinalizeRow.step`)                                                                           | `line.step`, `line.index`; a null `line` is refused, never guessed                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
    | `flow`                                                                                                              | an A or B step's own (K-01 `stepId`); a shared step's: `slotSource.booking.flow` for a row about a booking, else the first of A, then B, that the row's bundle lists whose graph holds `line.step`, which the caller reads (default 53); held by none: refused (`stepMissing`)       |
    | `role`; `FinalizeRow.reader`                                                                                        | the one of `recipient.roles` the message is for (identity and access, T-308; S13-31), through `readerOf`; with none (v1.5, item 44): the audience of the step line the row carries if its step is in section 1 of its flow and the recipient a person, else refused                  |
    | `name`, `way`, `handlingLine`                                                                                       | `recipient.name` and `recipient.kind` (phone: texts; email: email); the handling line's words are the bundle's (open gap 34)                                                                                                                                                         |
    | `language`                                                                                                          | the text's (decide), else `recipient.storedLanguage`, else the business's (arch 06): the caller's                                                                                                                                                                                    |
    | `source` (`SlotSource`)                                                                                             | `slotSource` (biz.name and display name, the business's zone, the booking head, ask.by from the question's armed clock row), `row.channel`; the rest unfilled (open gap 30); `sensitive`: `businessSensitive` (v1.6, item 45), on when null or missing, as for a row about a booking |
    | `claim`                                                                                                             | code's (T-311, T-313), over `slotSource`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
    | `factSlots`; `situation.about`                                                                                      | not stored (open gap 30): none                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
    | `situation.safetyOutcomes`                                                                                          | `answers.texts[].outcomes`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
    | `conversation`                                                                                                      | not read (open gap 33): none                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
    | `allowedFormats`                                                                                                    | C-128's rule over `recipient.phone` and `row.channel`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
    | `allowedLinks`; `FinalizeRow.checks`, `fallbackText`                                                                | the bundle the row was committed under (`row.bundleId`), at `line.step`, in the flow the `flow` row names (default 53)                                                                                                                                                               |
    | `options`                                                                                                           | `slotSource.question.options`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
    | `openQuestions`                                                                                                     | `openQuestions`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
    | `inbound`                                                                                                           | the words the runtime caller hands in for `answers.texts` (above)                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
    | `FinalizeRow.calendarFile`                                                                                          | none for a row about no booking; a booking row's file is not read (open gap 35)                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
    | `FinalizeRow.question`                                                                                              | `slotSource.question` (`id`, `version`) with its `clockRow` (`id`, `version`); null for a row that asks no question; a question with no clock row (`clockRow` null) is refused, never null: the passed-on path withdraws a question only with its clock row (default 54)             |

36. **`readFixedLineFacts`** (what the fixed-line inserter needs about the row's handle and business: arch 07 line 361, "on Rosie's first text to a handle about one business (DD), the opener ... and the privacy-notice link; and on a number's texts until one is delivered, the STOP, HELP and rates line"). **Role and scope:** with a business it runs as `rosie_job` inside the finalize call's transaction, with `rosie.business_id` set and confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()` before the first read (STATE note 22), since `phones` is a runtime-only table (0012; T-220's `sendState` reads it on the runtime side only). With `businessId` null it runs on the runtime side and reads only a row with no business. The request names only the row: its handle and business are the row's own, so no caller can ask about another handle (`refuse-fixed-line-facts-caller-handle`). A row of another business, or no such row, is `notFound`. It returns, under the port's own names:
    - **`textedAboutBusiness`**, from K-04's opener mark (`openers_sent` for the row's recipient handle and business: "Whether Rosie's opener for a business has gone to a handle"): true when the mark names another row; false when there is none, or when it names this row, which then is the first text and carries the opener whenever it is finalized (`fixed-line-facts-opener-row`). With no business, `openers_sent` can hold no mark (its `business_id` is not null), so it is true when an earlier row to the handle with no business (a lower `commit_seq`) carries K-01's `opener` in its final parts' `fixedLines` (default 50);
    - **`openerRowId`**, the row the opener went on, or null;
    - **`optOutLineDeliveredAt`**, `phones.optout_line_delivered_at` of the recipient handle's phone (by `handle_id`: `phones_handle_key`), or null for an email handle, a number with no phone row, or one to which no text carrying the line was delivered; never the number (`refuse-fixed-line-facts-returns-number`).

    `FixedLineFacts.lines`, the row's other fixed lines, is not a database fact here (open gap 36).

### What v1.3 leaves for later minor versions

K-05 v1.8 adds `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102; DL-344 renumbered them from v1.4, which T-1160's safety reads took, DL-364 from v1.5, which T-1184's sign-up reading took, DL-391 from v1.6, which T-1196's sensitive switch took, and DL-438 from v1.7, which T-1211's open chat took). Open gaps 29 to 39 name what each read would read once K-04 stores it, each with the K-04 minor version and migration task it needs; until then every read fails closed where it cannot say (a null `line`, an unread text, a fixed-line row).

### Consumers of v1.3

Each moves to v1.3 in its own task that needs it; v1, v1.1 and v1.2 consumers keep working.

- **Action-layer engineer:** C-087 one commit per turn (`readOpenHolds` behind `CommitPorts.openHolds`), C-090 compose input (`readOutboxRow` behind `ComposeInputPorts.readRow`), C-093 the finalize call (`readOutboxRow` behind `FinalizePorts.readRow`, T-313's port) and C-089 the fixed-line inserter (`readFixedLineFacts` behind `FixedLinePorts.readFixedLines`, T-314's port), all four bound in the Tool API's turn face, C-074, by T-1141.
- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1138 (`db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`). v1.3 needs no migration.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.3` and its database side, new tasks. The fixture part may join `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, as v1.2's did (T-128, DL-124): it adds v1.3 to that file's versions and C-089, C-090 and C-093 to its side list, and then moves this list into the section "Producers and consumers", whose every component that file's side list must hold.

What two consumers must hold to:

- **T-1141** names in `readOpenHolds.eventIds` the turn's texts whose `recordSafetyOutcome` is not among the request's core actions, with every unread id the runtime's `CommitContext` carries, and cuts each of `holds` to K-09 `safetyHold`'s three fields. It refuses (fails closed) a row whose `line` is null, and in finalize a row whose question has no clock row (default 54); gives `ComposeRow.flow` by item 35's flow rule (default 53) and reads the step's links, checks and fallback text in that flow, testing the rule on `read-outbox-row-s1-02` with K-01's `bundle-v0-skeleton.json`: flow A; fills `SlotSource.sensitive` true for a row about a booking until the switch is read (gap 30), maps `fixedLineRow` to the call's fixed-line refusal, gives the composer only the words of `answers.texts` the runtime handed it, only when `answers.handleId` is the recipient's, and never reads words from the database.
- **T-1138** runs `readOpenHolds` as `rosie_app` and the other two as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()`. It tests the `businessId` null path (no row or hold with a business is returned) and a read under another business's scope, and selects none of `messages.words`, `safety_outcomes.sealed_words`, `handles.address`, `phones.number` or any `sealing_keys` column in these reads, the barrier item 35's reason 1 rests on, since no grant keeps them out (`rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on `messages` and `safety_outcomes`, `rosie_job` on all five tables: 0012, 0016): its tests check that no statement of the three reads names one.

## v1.4 (T-1160): the safety service's reads

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1140's handoff 1, carded by DL-344. T-318's safety and hand-offs service (C-104, `actions/src/domain/safety`) reads K-34's outcomes and holds and three K-04 facts through its `SafetyReader` port (`ports.ts`: `outcomeOfEvent`, `outcome`, `hold`, `holdOfOutcome`, `outboxRowWritten`, `ownerOf`, `businessZone` and `arrivedAt`), and no K-05 call backs any of them: v1.3's `readOpenHolds` gives the commit its open holds only (open gap 26), and T-1138 builds v1.3's three reads alone. So the default reader answers `notBuilt` for every read, every safety record fails closed, and the walking skeleton's turn 1, whose one text reads No and whose record rides in its commit (K-09 v1.5 `commit-s1-01.json`; T-1140's `runCore`), cannot commit. **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.4.schema.json` holds every v1.3 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.3.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all four files as they are, and changes none. So every v1, v1.1, v1.2 and v1.3 exchange is a v1.4 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.4). **No schema change:** the reads read K-34 v1's `safety_outcomes` and `safety_holds` and K-04 v1's `outbox_rows`, `person_roles`, `person_handles`, `handles` and `businesses` as migrated (0001 to 0031), with the grants they hold. **Not here:** the database calls (T-1161, `db/repo/safety-reads.ts`); binding them as the service's reader (T-1141); `arrivedAt`, which needs a K-04 home for a text's K-06 `receivedAt` (T-1155; open gap 40); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-344, DL-364, DL-391, DL-438).

### The calls v1.4 adds

| Call                       | Item | `SafetyReader` method it serves                                | Scope and role                                                                           | Repeating it | Time budget                                                                               |
| -------------------------- | ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readSafetyOutcomeOfEvent` | 37   | `outcomeOfEvent`: a text's safety record, by its event id      | business: the call's, as `rosie_app`; with none, the runtime side, rows with no business | Safe: a read | Inside "Gate and commit ≤ 300 ms" (arch 04 line 223) when a turn's commit runs the record |
| `readSafetyOutcome`        | 38   | `outcome`: a safety outcome, by its id                         | as item 37                                                                               | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |
| `readSafetyHold`           | 39   | `hold`: a safety hold, by its id                               | as item 37                                                                               | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |
| `readSafetyHoldOfOutcome`  | 40   | `holdOfOutcome`: the hold one outcome set                      | as item 37                                                                               | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |
| `readOutboxRowWritten`     | 41   | `outboxRowWritten`: whether an outbox row is written           | as item 37                                                                               | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |
| `readBusinessOwner`        | 42   | `ownerOf`: the business's owner and the handle they write from | business: the call's, as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()`; a business always   | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |
| `readBusinessZone`         | 43   | `businessZone`: the business's time zone                       | business: the call's, as `rosie_app`; a business always                                  | Safe: a read | As item 37                                                                                |

`arrivedAt` has no call in v1.4 (T-1155; open gap 40).

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, on **the call's own transaction under its business scope** (`ports.ts`: "on the call's own transaction under its business scope (a row of another business is not seen, K-04 v1 rule 4)"), so a record or a release reads what its own commit then compares; ids are compared byte by byte, never parsed (CQ-09), and every call can answer `transient`. **Each request states its business**, the call's own (the Tool API caller's `businessId`), never one the service picks (default 55). Five take null before there is one and then run on the runtime side, reading only rows with no business (default 25); `readBusinessOwner` and `readBusinessZone` take a business only, since an owner and a zone are a business's (`refuse-business-owner-no-business`). **With a business, each call first finds the business in its scope** (`businesses` by id) and answers `notFound` (table `businesses`) when the business does not exist or the scope does not show it, so a null or false answer always means none in this business, never no such business (default 56), as `readOpenHolds` never answers "nothing open" under a business that is not there (item 34). Besides the scope, every read of a business-scoped table names the business, as items 26 and 34 to 36 do, so even a role that row-level security does not bind (a table's owner, until T-1093) sees no other business's row. **A row of another business is never seen** (K-04 v1 rule 4; K-34 v1 rule 4): it answers as no row does, null or false (default 57). **No answer carries a sealed value, a person's words, a handle's address, a phone number, a key or a wrapping key's name** (rule 7): each value is the port's own shape (default 60), and every object v1.4's answers define is closed (`additionalProperties` false), so the schema refuses an answer that carries more.

### How each v1.4 call works (K-04 v1 and K-34 v1 as migrated)

37. **`readSafetyOutcomeOfEvent`** (`SafetyReader.outcomeOfEvent`: a text's safety record by its event id, K-34 `safety_outcomes_event_key`). The record's key is the text's event id (K-09 v1 `recordSafetyOutcome`: "one outcome row per text, and a repeat returns the first"), so `recordSafetyOutcome` reads it first: a row found is a repeat, answered from the stored rows with nothing written; none, and the record writes its rows in the turn's commit (`record.ts` default 1). **Role and scope:** with a business it runs as the Tool API's role, `rosie_app`, on the call's own transaction under that business's scope: `safety_outcomes` is business-scoped and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on it (0016), so the read needs no runtime-only table and no key. With `businessId` null it runs on the runtime side and reads only rows with no business (default 25), as `readOpenHolds` does (item 34): the read itself names `business_id is null`, since `rosie_job` with no scope sees every business's rows. **Columns:** the fifteen `StoredOutcome` names, and no other: `id`, `event_id`, `source`, `handle_id`, `person_id`, `business_id`, `booking_id`, `turn_id`, `read_at`, `timed_out`, `labels`, `tenses`, `danger_word_kinds`, `outcomes` and `lifted_to_unsure`, answered under the port's names (`storedOutcome`). **Never `sealed_words`** (each label's evidence words, who is at risk and who wrote it, and the danger words matched: K-34 README rule 6), nor `tries` and `danger_word_lists`, which the port does not read (default 60). `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on the whole table, so, as for item 35's words (its reason 1), the barrier is the read's column list, which T-1161's tests check names none of them, and the schema's closed object (`refuse-safety-outcome-with-sealed-words`). **The skeleton's turn 1:** the No record of Sam's first text reads its own event under the barbershop's scope before its commit and finds nothing (`read-safety-outcome-of-event-s1-01`: `safetyOutcome` null), so it writes the row in that commit. A text whose row is another business's is not seen (`read-safety-outcome-of-event-other-business`): a record under this scope writes it again, and its commit's insert meets `safety_outcomes_event_key` (23505), so the commit answers `constraint` and writes nothing (open gap 42).

38. **`readSafetyOutcome`** (`SafetyReader.outcome`: a safety outcome by its id). A release reads the outcome its hold names (the danger text's) and the "we're safe" read it is given (`releasedByOutcomeId`), to check that read holds no danger, came from the held handle's own chat in a turn, and arrived after the danger text (`release.ts` defaults 12 and 13; `read-safety-outcome-alex-safe`). Role, scope and columns as item 37; a row the scope does not show answers null (`read-safety-outcome-no-business`: with no business, the barbershop's read is not returned).

39. **`readSafetyHold`** (`SafetyReader.hold`: a safety hold by its id, K-09 v1 `releaseSafetyHold.holdId`). **Role and scope:** as item 37: `safety_holds` is business-scoped and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on it (0016). **Columns:** all fifteen of `safety_holds` (none is sealed), under the `StoredHold` names (`storedHold`), with its `version`, which a release compares and sets (K-34 README rule 2; limit 4). A released hold answers its first release (`release.ts`; CQ-80: never un-released; `read-safety-hold-alex-released`), an open one its rule and version (`read-safety-hold-alex-open`), and one the scope does not show null, which the release answers `notFound` (`read-safety-hold-other-business`). A release time and its reason come together or not at all, as K-34's `safety_holds_released_pair` holds them (`refuse-safety-hold-released-without-why`), so no reader takes a half release for an open hold.

40. **`readSafetyHoldOfOutcome`** (`SafetyReader.holdOfOutcome`: the hold one outcome set, K-34 `safety_holds_one_per_outcome`), open or released: a repeated record names it while it is open, and a released hold holds nothing (`record.ts` default 1; `read-safety-hold-of-outcome-alex-threat`). Role, scope and columns as item 39. One hold or null, never a list (`refuse-safety-hold-of-outcome-two-holds`); a No read set none (`read-safety-hold-of-outcome-none`), as turn 1's record of Sam's text sets none.

41. **`readOutboxRowWritten`** (`SafetyReader.outboxRowWritten`: whether an outbox row with this id is written). A repeated record names its checked line's row, whose id is made from the text's event id (`ids.ts`), only when the first record wrote it (`record.ts` `repeatAnswer`; `outbox-row-written-alex-safety`, `outbox-row-written-not-yet`). **Role and scope:** as item 37: `outbox_rows` is business-scoped and `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on it (0012). **Columns:** the row's `id` alone, never `plan` or `final_parts`, which hold the message's words (K-03 v1 `rowPlan`, `finalParts`), nor any other (`refuse-outbox-row-written-returns-words`). A row the scope does not show answers false, so not even its existence reaches another business (`outbox-row-written-other-business`).

42. **`readBusinessOwner`** (`SafetyReader.ownerOf`: the business's owner and the handle they write from, to whom a checked line to the owner goes: A3-48, a threat's line goes to the owner; S13-10, no safety message to the one who threatens; `record.ts` `lineRows`). The request names one business, the call's own: the business it runs under and whose owner it reads, so no call reads another business's owner. **Role and scope:** as `rosie_job` inside the call's transaction, with `rosie.business_id` set to the business and confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()` before the first read (STATE note 22; T-1094's note N2), since `person_handles` and `handles` are tables only the runtime side holds (0012: `rosie_app` holds no grant on them), as v1.3's items 35 and 36 run; `person_roles` is read with the business named too. STATE note 5 ("Tool API paths never run as rosie_job") holds for the six other reads, which need no runtime-only table. **Columns:** `person_roles` `id`, `person_id`, `role` and `ended_at`; `person_handles` `person_id` and `handle_id`; `handles` `id` and `kind`, **never `address`**: the answer names the handle by id, as T-307's outbox rows take it, never a number or an email address (`refuse-business-owner-returns-number`). **The owner** (default 58): the business's one live owner role (`role` owner, `ended_at` null); none answers `owner` null (`business-owner-none`), and **more than one answers `moreThanOneOwner` and nothing else, never one of them**. The sources give a business one account owner (S2-02, MASTER-FLOW-A line 1220: "One account owner"; S11-73 and S11-100: only the account owner closes or hands the business over, and the old owner's access ends at the hand-over), while K-04 v1 does not refuse a second live owner role (`person_roles_one_live_role` is per person). So two are a record the sources do not allow, such as a hand-over left half done, and picking either could send the business's safety notice to someone who no longer runs it (open gap 41). **The handle** (default 59): of the owner's handles (`person_handles`), the phone handles first, then the email handles, each in handle id order (compared, never parsed: CQ-09; the order T-307's `Reach` lists a person's mobiles and emails in), the first of them; none answers `owner` null, since nothing can reach them. Why the phone first: the owner's one mobile is their sign-in (FS row B12; K-22 v1 `accounts.mobile`), Rosie's lines to an owner are texts (the master's `san → owner · msg`), and a safety line goes by text first and every other way the person allows at its first failure (arch 02 line 125; v1.1 item 15, `safetyOtherWay`), so the email handle is the dispatcher's other way, not the line's first (`business-owner-phone-first`, with K-22's `seed-owner-id-upload`). Among several phone handles, which K-22's "one mobile" does not allow and K-04 does not refuse, the lowest id is taken: each is the owner's own, so the line reaches no one else (open gap 43). A null owner makes the service build no line to the owner and refuse the record (`safetyLineNotBuilt`): it fails closed.

43. **`readBusinessZone`** (`SafetyReader.businessZone`: K-04 `businesses.time_zone`), the IANA zone "that day" is read in for a harm report (K-34 `harm_reports.day_zone`; CQ-05's default, K-34 default 4; `record.ts` default 10; `business-zone-barbershop`). The request names one business, the call's own. **Role and scope:** as `rosie_app` on the call's own transaction under that business's scope, whose `businesses` policy shows `rosie_app` the scope's own row alone (0012). K-04 keeps every business's zone (`time_zone` not null), so a business the scope does not show answers `notFound`, never a null zone (`business-zone-no-business-row`, `refuse-business-zone-null`), and the service refuses the harm report rather than file it in a zone made up (`harmReportNeedsBusiness`).

### What v1.4 leaves for later minor versions

`arrivedAt` (open gap 40: T-1155 gives a text's K-06 `receivedAt` a K-04 home and a K-05 read; until then a release that needs the texts' arrival order answers `stillDanger`, so the hold stays: `release.ts` default 13); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), K-05 v1.8 (DL-344, DL-364, DL-391, DL-438); and the owner's sign-in mobile from K-22 (open gap 43).

### Consumers of v1.4

Each moves to v1.4 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.3 consumers keep working.

- **Action-layer engineer:** C-104 the safety and hand-offs service (T-318), whose `SafetyReader` the seven calls back, all but `arrivedAt`, through C-087's `runCore` (T-1140), bound in C-074 the Tool API turn face by T-1141.
- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1161 (`db/repo/safety-reads.ts`). v1.4 needs no migration.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.4` and its database side, new tasks. The fixture part may join `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, as v1.2's did (T-128, DL-124): it adds v1.4 to that file's versions and C-104 to its side list, and then moves this list into the section "Producers and consumers", whose every component that file's side list must hold.

What two consumers must hold to:

- **T-1141** binds each port method to its call on the call's own transaction and business, and hands each answer's value to the service as it is: `safetyOutcome`, `hold`, `written`, `owner` and `timeZone` are the port's shapes (default 60). It passes the call's own business in every request, never one the service names: a port call naming another business (`ownerOf` or `businessZone` with a business that is not the call's) is refused, never read. It maps `notFound`, `moreThanOneOwner` and `transient` to a thrown error that fails the call closed, never to null or false, which would read as none (default 61). Until T-1155 it wires `arrivedAt` to answer null (unknown), which a release reads as not later (`stillDanger`), never a time taken from `read_at` (T-318's security review).
- **T-1161** runs items 37 to 41 and 43 as `rosie_app` and item 42 as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()`; finds the business first; names the business in every read of a business-scoped table; and tests the `businessId` null path (no row with a business returned), each read under another business's scope, an owner whose email handle's id sorts before the phone's (the phone is still the answer), two live owner roles at one business (`moreThanOneOwner`, which no seed holds, so the test writes the second role itself) and an owner with no handle (`owner` null). It selects none of `safety_outcomes.sealed_words`, `handles.address`, `outbox_rows.plan` or `outbox_rows.final_parts` in these reads, the barrier item 37 rests on, since `rosie_app` holds `SELECT` on `safety_outcomes` and `outbox_rows` and `rosie_job` on `handles` (0012, 0016): its tests check that no statement of the seven names one.

## v1.5 (T-1184): a reader for a row to someone with no role yet, on the sign-up section

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1141's round 1 NEED, item 2 (`ops/cards/T-1141.md`), ruled by DL-364 and recorded as QUESTIONS.md CQ-179. Item 35's table maps a row's reader (`ComposeRow.role`, `FinalizeRow.reader`) as "the one of `recipient.roles` the message is for ... with none, refused". At the walking skeleton's turn 1 Sam holds no role yet (ADR-013 default 130; T-1148's seed, `db/seeds/skeleton.ts`), so turn 1's row, `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`, S1-02's question to him, was refused (`noReader`; T-1141's `READER_WAITING`), while K-09 v1's `compose-input-s1-02.json` and v1.6's `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` read him as the owner (`audience.role` owner). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.5.schema.json` holds every v1.4 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.4.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all five files as they are, and changes none. So every v1 to v1.4 exchange is a v1.5 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.5). **No database change:** no call is added or changed, and `readOutboxRow` reads as item 35 says, `recipient.roles` as stored; no column, grant or migration. What v1.5 adds is the consumers' mapping, item 44, stated as `rowReader` so its fixtures check it. **Not here:** the port's code (T-1141's round 2, `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/`); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-364, DL-391, DL-438), with T-1155's arrival read and T-1177's outcome per answered label after it.

### The rule v1.5 adds

44. **The sign-up reading** (item 35's table, row `role`; `FinalizeRow.reader`; CQ-179's default, DL-364). A row whose recipient holds a role at the row's business is read by that role, as v1.3 maps it: the one of `recipient.roles` the message is for (identity and access, T-308; S13-31), through `readerOf` (owner and admin read as the owner, staff and customer as themselves), and a role that reads as no one (`own_business`) is refused. A row whose recipient holds no role (`recipient.roles` empty: no live role at the row's business, or no business yet) is read by **the audience of the step line the row carries** when, and only when:
    - the recipient is a person (`recipient.personId` not null): a handle with no person holds no role and is no one's own sign-up (default 64);
    - the row's step is in **section 1 of its flow**, the sign-up section the sign-up door enters (ADR-013 section 3; K-02 v1.1 default 9): K-01's `step.section` is 1 in the flow item 35's flow row reads the step in (defaults 53 and 65);
    - the step line the row carries goes to a reader: the lane of the step's lines from Rosie (default 62) is `owner`, `staff` or `cust` (K-01 `lane`), read as K-03's `readerRole` owner, staff or customer (`READER_LANES`, `actions/src/compose-input/master-line.ts`).

    Then that audience is the reader (`FinalizeRow.reader`, K-03 `audience.role`), and `ComposeRow.role` is the role of the same name, which no `person_roles` row holds: it is how the row is read, never a role the person is given (default 63). **Every other row whose recipient holds no role is still refused** (`noReader`), as v1.3 refuses it: a step outside section 1 or a row with no person (`noRole`), and a section 1 line that goes to no reader or cannot be told without a role (`lineHasNoReader`). Turn 1's row reads so: Sam is a person with no role, S1-02 is in section 1 of flow A, and its one line from Rosie goes to the owner (K-01 `bundle-v0-skeleton.json`), so he reads it as the owner (`row-reader-s1-02-no-role`), as K-09's two compose-input fixtures read him.

**`rowReader`** is no database call: it is item 44 stated as an exchange (`call`, `given`, `request`, `outcome`, as every fixture here is), so its fixtures check the rule. Its request is what the mapping reads: from `readOutboxRow`'s answer, the row (`outboxRowId`), its bundle (`row.bundleId`), its line (never null: a null line is refused before a reader is looked for, item 35's table) and its recipient (`personId` and `roles`, as stored); and from the row's bundle, through the caller's own bundle port, the flow the step is read in (default 53), the step's section in that flow (K-01 `step.section`) and the lane of the step line the row carries (`lineTo`, default 62). Its outcome is the reader, by a role (`from` `roles`, with the role's id) or by the sign-up line (`from` `signUpLine`, no role id), or `noReader` with why. The schema holds item 44 whole (`rowReader.exchange`'s rules): a recipient who holds a role is never read by the line (`refuse-row-reader-role-read-by-line`), and one with no role is read by the line only on section 1 (`refuse-row-reader-no-role-outside-section-1`). Which of several roles the message is for (T-308) is the caller's, as v1.3 says, and is not checked here.

**Keywords beyond ADR-005's list:** `rowReader.exchange` holds item 44's rules in `dependentSchemas` on `outcome`, which every exchange has, each rule an `if` and `then` built with `allOf`, `not`, `minItems` and `maxItems`, as K-01 holds its lint checks (bundle README, "Keywords beyond ADR-005's list"). The generated `RowReaderExchange` type is wider than the schema there: it leaves the rules out, so a consumer proves its mapping on the `rowReader` fixtures, not by the type alone (ADR-005: "a contract that needs one says so in its README").

**What a person with no role can read** (for the security reviewer's review): only a row addressed to them, since `readOutboxRow` gives the row's own recipient and the mapping reads no other person's roles; only a row whose step is in the sign-up section, Rosie's lines to the person setting a business up; and on such a row only what limit 7 lets the line's audience see (arch 06 line 311). No role is written or held: `recipient.roles` stays as stored, no K-05 call writes or returns the sign-up reading, every other read of roles (`readTurnState`, `handleTargets`, `sendState`'s audience: default 31) reads them as stored, and once a role is written the row is read by it. Turn 1's S1-02 line carries no slot fact (K-09 v1.6 `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json`: its facts are the step card's terms).

### Consumers of v1.5

Each moves to v1.5 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.4 consumers keep working.

- **Action-layer engineer:** C-090 compose input (`ComposeRow.role`) and C-093 the finalize call (`FinalizeRow.reader`), whose row ports C-074 the Tool API turn face binds (T-1141's round 2, `rows.ts`), which maps the reader by item 44 and drops `READER_WAITING`.
- **Database engineer:** nothing to build: `readOutboxRow` (C-152, T-1138) reads as v1.3 says, and v1.5 needs no migration.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.5`, a new task (every v1.5 fixture against its schema, refused ones refused), which may join `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` as v1.2's did (T-128, DL-124). v1.5 has no database side.

What T-1141 must hold to: it maps the reader exactly as `rowReader` does, from `readOutboxRow`'s answer and the row's bundle: by the role the message is for when the recipient holds one; by the line's audience only for a person with no role on a section 1 step whose line goes to owner, staff or cust, the lane read as default 62 says; `noReader` otherwise. It tests turn 1's row with no role (the owner) and a step outside section 1 with no role (refused), reads no role from another person on the handle, and never writes, caches or passes on the sign-up reading as a role the person holds.

### What v1.5 leaves for later minor versions

`sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), K-05 v1.8 (DL-364, DL-391, DL-438: v1.6 is T-1196's sensitive switch, v1.7 T-1211's open chat), then T-1155's arrival read and T-1177's outcome per answered label, each in its own minor version after it, in that order; and open gaps 44 to 46.

## v1.6 (T-1196): the row's business's sensitive switch on readOutboxRow

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): DL-391, from T-1190's reads and open gap 30. The turn face's compose input and finalize fill `SlotSource.sensitive` (RULES 4.6: under the switch, texts show only the display name; S13-22), and they read only `readOutboxRow` and `readFixedLineFacts` (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts`, from item 35's table: "nothing else is read for them"). So until a K-05 read gives the switch they treat it as on for every row (DL-326 note b: fail closed), and turn 1's S1-02 row composes as gentle, while K-09 v1.6's `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` and T-1175's recording 4 have it routine (DL-373). The switch is a plug-in switch per kind of work and flow, stored in K-23 v1's `company_kinds.pf_switches_a` and `pf_switches_b` (K-26 v1's `plugin_switches` registry names it `sensitive`: migrations 0019 and 0023), and no K-05 call read `company_kinds` (open gap 30: "until a minor version reads it"). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.6.schema.json` holds every v1.5 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.5.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.4, v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all six files as they are; the changed ones, `readOutboxRow.response` and `readOutboxRow.exchange`, take one new optional field, `businessSensitive`, and `readOutboxRow.response` gives each of v1.3's fields by `$ref` to v1.3's own definition of it (v1.3 defines `row`, `answers` and `slotSource` inline, and v1.4 and v1.5 keep them unchanged). So every v1 to v1.5 exchange is a v1.6 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.6). New definitions: `businessSensitive` (the field's value, with its source and reason) and `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` (the answer a v1.6 call gives, the field required). **No database change:** the read reads K-23 v1's `company_kinds` as migrated (0019), with the grants it holds; no call, column, grant, index or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's read (T-1197, `db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts`); binding it in the turn face (T-1190, `rows.ts`); a booking's own switch through its service's kind of work (open gap 30: item 35's table keeps a row about a booking on); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-391, DL-438).

### The rule v1.6 adds

45. **`readOutboxRow`'s `businessSensitive`, the row's business's sensitive switch** (DL-391; RULES 4.6; S13-22; open gap 30). **The read:** in item 35's own read-only transaction (one moment), as `rosie_job` under the row's business with `rosie.business_id` confirmed by `requireBusinessScope()`, the call also reads K-23 v1's `company_kinds` whose `business_id` is the row's business (`company_kinds_business_idx`), the columns `pf_switches_a` and `pf_switches_b` alone. The read names the business beside the scope, as every read here does: `company_kinds` is business-scoped, and `rosie_job` holds `SELECT` on it behind its runtime policy (0019). **The answer:**
    - `true` when any of those rows lists `sensitive` in `pf_switches_a` or `pf_switches_b` (K-26 v1's `plugin_switches`, 0023, names the switch `sensitive`, with `grief` merged into it, and code writes only registry names into K-23's two columns, which the database checks for form alone: K-23 README; 0023's header), so no other name counts;
    - `false` when the business has `company_kinds` rows and none lists it;
    - `null` when it has none (a sign-up before S1-04 writes its kinds of work: `read-outbox-row-s1-02-no-kinds`), and for a row with no business (`businessId` null), for which no kinds of work are read (default 68).

    **The caller** (T-1190's readRow ports, C-074) fills `SlotSource.sensitive` from it for a row about no booking: `null` is on, never off, so it fails closed (DL-326 note b; DL-391), and an answer with no `businessSensitive` (a v1.3 to v1.5 answer) reads as `null` (default 67). A row about a booking stays on whatever the business's switch (item 35's table; open gap 30), until its booking's own switch is read through its service's kind of work. A read that fails (`transient`) is thrown, never read as off (T-1197, T-1190), as default 61 has every error fail the call closed.

    **Why one value for the business, in either flow:** the switch is per kind of work and per flow (K-23's two columns; SPEC-master 5b: a switch can differ by flow), and a row about no booking names neither: no booking names a service and so a kind of work, and the flow its step is read in is the caller's, from the row's bundle (default 53), not the database's. So the read cannot pick one kind of work or one flow, and it takes the stricter (default 66): under the switch, texts show only the display name (RULES 4.6), so a business any of whose kinds of work is sensitive, in either flow, is read as sensitive, and its rows about no booking are filled in their sensitive form (`read-outbox-row-practice-sensitive`: the practice's one kind of work lists it in flow A only). And where nothing is stored, off would fail open: a therapy practice's first texts, before its kinds of work are written, would go in their routine form, so `null` is never off (default 67).

    **What it never answers:** the switch's one value alone, never the business's kinds of work, their facts or trade names, or any other switch, and never per flow (`refuse-outbox-row-switch-per-flow`): no more than RULES 4.6 needs (default 69).

**`readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response`** is the answer a v1.6 call gives: every field of `readOutboxRow.response`, with `businessSensitive` required. `readOutboxRow.response` takes the field as optional only so that v1.3 to v1.5's answers, which have none, stay valid and every earlier exchange is a v1.6 exchange (a minor version only adds, as v1.2's optional `claimedRow.port` did); a v1.6 call always writes it, and its producer checks every answer it gives against `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` (`refuse-outbox-row-switch-left-out`: the practice's answer with the switch left out is refused). `readOutboxRow.exchange`'s outcome names both answers, `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` first; they overlap, which `anyOf` allows, so an answer with the field is taken by both and one without it by `readOutboxRow.response` alone. `npm run contracts:types` gives each its own type: `ReadOutboxRowV1Dot6Response`, with `businessSensitive` required, for the producer's answer, and `ReadOutboxRowResponse`, with it optional, for a consumer that also reads earlier answers.

### Consumers of v1.6

Each moves to v1.6 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.5 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1197 (`db/repo/turn-face-reads.ts` `readOutboxRow`): item 45's read inside item 35's transaction, under the row's business; v1.6 needs no migration.
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-074 the Tool API turn face, whose compose-input and finalize row ports (`rows.ts`; C-090's `ComposeRow` and C-093's `FinalizeRow`, each with its `source`) fill `SlotSource.sensitive` from `businessSensitive` (T-1190).
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.6`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts` as v1.5's did, adding v1.6 to `WRITTEN_FOR` with a mend for each refused fixture. Its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after T-1197, adding v1.6 to its versions, writing the practice's five rows before its exchange (section "Fixtures"), and taking `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` as a refused answer's definition (its twin `read-outbox-row-practice-sensitive`, its fault `businessSensitive`).

What two consumers must hold to:

- **T-1197** reads `company_kinds` inside `readOutboxRow`'s own transaction, under the row's business, naming the business (`business_id` equal to it), the two switch columns alone. It answers `null` for a business with no kinds of work and for a row with no business, and then reads no `company_kinds` row at all: a company kind whose `business_id` is null is a sign-up's not yet tied to a business, so a read that matched a null business (`is not distinct from`, or a `coalesce`) would answer from another signer's kinds of work (default 68). It always writes `businessSensitive`, types its answer as `ReadOutboxRowV1Dot6Response` and checks every answer against `readOutboxRow.v1dot6Response` in its tests, and throws a read's error, never answering `false` for it. It tests a business with two kinds of work, one listing the switch in flow B only (`true`), the yoga studio's scope over the barbershop's row (`notFound`, nothing of its kinds of work), and the `businessId` null path with a company kind of no business present (`null`).
- **T-1190** reads `null` and a missing `businessSensitive` as on (fail closed), keeps a row about a booking on (item 35's table), and tests turn 1's row three ways, `false` (routine), `null` (on) and `true` (on), and that a read that fails fails the call, never reading it as off.

### What v1.6 leaves for later minor versions

`sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-391, DL-438), then T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader with the business's live-owner state, each in its own minor version at the next free number (DL-391); a booking's own switch through its service's kind of work (open gap 30); and open gap 47.

## v1.7 (T-1211): a number's text opens its chat

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): join 11's handoff 1 (`ops/cards/T-562.md`), carded by DL-438 and recorded as QUESTIONS.md CQ-194. RULES 4.1b keeps one flag per mobile, "chat open, set when it first texts Rosie's number. STOP clears it; turning texts back on (7.5) sets it again" (K-04 v1 `phones.chat_open`, with `chat_open_at` and `chat_way`), and limit 8 holds every row to a number that is not stopped and has no open chat (arch 06 line 313; MC C9: Rosie never writes first). No K-05 call wrote the flag: `handleTargets`, `sendState`, `renewClaim`'s `recipient` and `readOutboxRow`'s `recipient.phone` read it (items 27, 28, 32, 35), and K-09's `recordStopOrStart` writes the stop alone. Test owner 1's seed holds no phone row on purpose (T-1148: JOIN is a first text), and turn 1's commit writes the thread alone (K-09 v1.5 `commit-s1-01.json`), so limit 8 held the JOIN's reply waiting for a chat (`waitingChat`: e2e row 11) and the first reply never went; join 12 (T-563) and D-017's human test wait on it. **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.7.schema.json` holds every v1.6 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.6.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.5, v1.4, v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all seven files as they are, and changes none; it adds the call `openChatOnText` (item 46) and the definitions its exchange uses. So every v1 to v1.6 exchange is a v1.7 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.7). **No schema change:** the call writes K-04 v1's `phones` and `event_log` as migrated (0004, 0011), with the grants `rosie_job` holds (0012: `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `phones`, `SELECT` on `handles`, `SELECT` and `INSERT` on `event_log`), and K-08 v1 already holds its entry's kind, `phone.chat_opened`; no column, grant, index or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's call and the gateway's wiring (two new tasks: "Consumers of v1.7"); a number's chat thread outside S1-01 (open gap 48); `sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-438).

### The call v1.7 adds

| Call             | Item                               | Scope                                                                                   | Repeating it                                                                                                                                                              | Time budget                                                            |
| ---------------- | ---------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `openChatOnText` | 46: a number's text opens its chat | runtime, as `rosie_job` with no business scope (`phones` and `handles` are scoped none) | Safe: the text's event id is the key; a repeat of a text that opened the chat says `repeated`, and nothing is written for a text on an open chat or from a stopped number | Inside the gateway's 1 s (arch 02 line 109), beside `handleSealingKey` |

It reads and writes PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, in one transaction, and can answer `transient`, as every K-05 call does.

### How it works (K-04 v1 and K-08 v1 as frozen)

46. **`openChatOnText`** (RULES 4.1b; MC C9; K-04 `phones`; CQ-194, its timing moved to receipt: below).

    **Who calls it, and when.** The gateway (C-044), at receipt, once for each text or photo a phone handle sends (K-06 v1 `provider` linq, `handle.kind` phone, `kind` text or photo, `fromRosie` false; default 71): after the scrub and `handleSealingKey` (item 29), whose answer gives the handle's K-04 id, and before it writes the sealed text to the handle's actor (arch 02 line 109). So the chat is open in K-04 before anything is written for the text: its safety record on arrival, the turn it joins, a STOP's or a START's record. It is never called for an email handle, a reaction, a vote, an edit or a receipt. A `transient` answer is a write that failed: the gateway answers 503, nothing is stored, and Linq retries the webhook (arch 02 line 109: "Write fails: 503, and the provider retries"), which the call takes as a repeat. `notFound` and `notAPhone` are a caller's fault, since the key call has just found or written a phone handle: the gateway stores the text all the same, so a refusal never keeps a text from being received and read for safety, and rows to the number wait for the chat, as limit 8 fails closed (default 77).

    **Why at receipt.** CQ-194's default had the call before the turn starts, after the safety read. It moves to receipt for reasons 1 and 2, and keeps the rest of that default: one runtime-side call per text from a phone handle, repeat-safe by the event id, and what it inserts, opens and never touches.
    1. **A first text in danger gets its checked line.** A text whose read holds danger or timed out is recorded on arrival, before batching, in its own arrival turn, whose commit writes the checked line as an outbox row of send class safety (K-09 v1.1 `actorOnArrival`, its default 36; arch 02 line 111). Limit 8 holds every row to a number that is not stopped and has no open chat, "a safety line too" (`runtime/src/dispatch/limit8/limit8.ts`), and the commit that answers the person can supersede every row that waits for their chat (item 19). Recorded after the safety read, the chat of a person whose first text is a crisis would still read closed when their 988 or 911 line is claimed: the line would wait, then be superseded, never sent, against arch 02 line 111 ("the outcome step's checked message goes first, at any hour, past any opt-out") and RULES 4.12.
    2. **A first text that is STOP is recorded.** K-09's `recordStopOrStart` answers `notFound` (table `phones`) for a number with no phone row (`actions/src/domain/flags/stop-start.ts`), and so does its replay for a STOP the actor took while PostgreSQL was down (`runtime/src/outage/db/outage.ts`, `replayStopOrStart`: refused, never written). At receipt, every stored text's number has its row before any STOP of it is recorded.
    3. **Linq's chat is open once a text arrives, whatever the turn then does:** a turn the gate refuses, one parked after its third lapse (whose "nothing changed" row waits for the chat too: v1 item 12), or a batch a later text restarts. In the commit, the chat would open only with a commit.
    4. **The gateway knows the handle then, and already writes for it.** `handleSealingKey` answers the handle's K-04 id at receipt, while the actor learns it only from a commit (`runtime/src/actor/handle-actor.ts`). The gateway already reads PostgreSQL for each text with words, and writes a first-seen handle's rows (item 29), so the call adds no dependency and no failure answer: the same 503, the same retry.
    5. **Every read of the turn sees the chat as Linq does:** the guard's targets (item 27), the frontier's turn state (item 26), the commit's text rows (T-307's `textHeldBack`, `actions/src/domain/messages-consent`, answers `chatNotOpen` for a phone with no row) and limit 8 at send (item 28). And the call moves a row's version only for a number's first text, before that text's turn reads the row, so it never fails a running turn's compare-and-set (default 76).

    **The transaction** (one, as `rosie_job` with no business scope; the entry has no business):
    1. Read the handle by id (`handles`: kind and address). None answers `notFound` (table `handles`); an email handle answers `notAPhone`. Nothing is written.
    2. No phone row for the handle (`phones_handle_key`): insert it, `number` the handle's address (read here, never sent), `handle_id`, `chat_open` true, `chat_open_at` the request's `receivedAt`, `chat_way` its `service`, the other columns at K-04's defaults (not stopped, version 1), `on conflict do nothing`, so a parallel first text's insert is waited for and found. Inserted: append the entry and answer `inserted`.
    3. Else lock the row `FOR UPDATE`, so a STOP that is committing is waited for and seen, then, in this order:
       - this text's own entry exists (`<event id>/chat-opened`, K-04 `event_log` by its primary key): answer `repeated`, with the row as it stands, writing nothing;
       - stopped: answer `stopped`, writing nothing;
       - chat open: answer `alreadyOpen`, writing nothing;
       - else, closed and not stopped (a mobile saved before it ever texted, such as a booking the business adds: MC C9): set `chat_open` true, `chat_open_at` the request's `receivedAt` where it is null (default 74), `chat_way` its `service`, the version up by one; append the entry; answer `opened`.
    4. Each answer names the handle and gives the row as the call leaves it (chat open, its time and way, stopped, version: `chatPhoneOpen`, `chatPhoneStopped`), never the number, with the entry it appended, or null.

    **The entry** (`chatOpenedEvent`; K-08 v1 `phone.chat_opened`; arch 09 lines 457, 492: every write appends who, what, old and new, when and why): id `<event id>/chat-opened`; no zone and no business; actor `provider`, Linq's report of the text, with no person (default 75); table `phones`, the row by its handle id (K-08 v1 default 3); old values null for an insert, else the closed chat's columns that change, and new values the open chat's (`chatClosedValues`, `chatOpenValues`: `chat_open`, `chat_open_at`, `chat_way`, nothing else); nothing sealed; `why` the provider event, Linq and the event id; no approval and no proofs. It is appended with no turn (none is written yet, and K-04's `event_log_turn_fk` names a written one), the event id as its job id (K-04 `event_log.job_id`: "For a clock firing, a webhook, ...: the job's id"), the text's trace id and no bundle. That its id, row and `why` are the request's event and handle is a code check, as a claim entry's id is (item 33).

    **What it never changes:** a stopped row, in any column, its version included; `stopped`, `stop_at`, `start_at`, `linq_contact_added_at`, `poll_voted_at` or `optout_line_delivered_at` on any row; `chat_open_at` once it is set; `chat_way` on an open chat (default 72); another handle's row (the request names the handle, and the number is its own address); the chat id, whose one home is K-04 `threads.linq_chat_id`, written by S1-01's commit (K-09 v1.5 default 135); a person, role, consent, thread, turn or outbox row. The number travels in no request (`refuse-open-chat-with-number`), answer or entry. `db/repo/phones.ts` `recordChatOpened` (T-183, a K-04 helper, which writes a stopped row's way and version too) is no K-05 call and does not stand in for this one.

    **The stop and the text** (for the security reviewer). A stopped number's chat never reopens without a START. The call reads `stopped` under the row's lock, so a STOP committing at the same moment is waited for and found, and a stopped row answers `stopped` with nothing written. Only START, through K-09 `recordStopOrStart` from that number's own START text (RULES 7.5; K-09 v1 README default 29), clears the stop and opens the chat, as `db/repo/phones.ts` `startPhone` does. K-04's `phones_stop_closes_chat` refuses an open chat on a stopped row, so a call could open one only by clearing the stop, which its entry would show: the schema refuses an entry that names `stopped` (`refuse-open-chat-stopped-number`). A repeat of an old text, which Linq may send for 30 minutes, never reopens a chat a STOP closed since: the row is stopped, and the text's own entry makes it `repeated`. A STOP after the call closes the chat as always. The call moves a row's version only for a number's first text, before that text's turn, so it never fails a STOP's compare-and-set (K-09 `recordStopOrStart` compares the phone's version).

**Keywords beyond ADR-005's list:** `allOf` narrows v1's `eventEntry` to the chat-opened entry (`chatOpenedEvent`), and `chatPhoneOpen` and the entry to what an `inserted` or `opened` answer holds, as v1.2's `claimEvent` narrows `eventEntry`. The generated types are intersections with an open index signature, wider than the schema, so a producer proves its answers on the v1.7 fixtures and against the schema, not by the type alone (ADR-005: "a contract that needs one says so in its README").

### Consumers of v1.7

Each moves to v1.7 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.6 consumers keep working.

- **Runtime engineer:** C-044 the gateway, the caller, at receipt, after `handleSealingKey` and before the actor (a new task, which turns e2e row 11's `waitingChat` into a claimed send); C-124 the outbox dispatcher and C-125 limit 8, whose chat check (`sendState`, `renewClaim`'s `recipient`) then finds the chat of a number that has texted Rosie open, with nothing of theirs to change.
- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, `openChatOnText` in db/repo (a new task), item 46's transaction as `rosie_job`; v1.7 needs no migration.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.7`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.7 to `WRITTEN_FOR` with a mend for each refused fixture (`refuse-open-chat-stopped-number`: twin `open-chat-ben-stopped`, its outcome; the other two: twin `open-chat-first-text-sam`, its request), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after the database engineer's call.

What the two new tasks must hold to:

- The database engineer's call runs item 46's transaction as written, in one transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope, and answers each v1.7 fixture's outcome. It reads the number only from K-04 `handles`, never from the request; it writes no column but `chat_open`, `chat_open_at`, `chat_way` and `version`, and no row but the phone row and its one entry; it never changes a stopped row. It tests parallel first texts of one new number (one row, one entry, the rest `alreadyOpen`), parallel repeats of one text (one entry, the rest `repeated`), a STOP after the call (closed) and a text after it (`stopped`, every column and the version unchanged), a START then the old text again (`repeated`), a later text by another service on an open chat (the way kept), and checks each entry against K-08 v1's `eventLogRow` once put in K-04's column names. It never binds `db/repo/phones.ts` `recordChatOpened` as this call.
- The gateway calls it once per text or photo from a phone handle, after `handleSealingKey` and before the actor's `accept`, with the handle's id from the key call's answer and, from the K-06 event, its id, service, `receivedAt` and trace; never for an email handle, a reaction, a vote, an edit or a receipt, and never with the number. It answers 503 for `transient` or no answer (nothing stored), and stores the text on `notFound` or `notAPhone`, the refusal its receive span's error code. No span or log line holds the number or the chat id.

### What v1.7 leaves for later minor versions

`sendState`'s consent fields (T-1102), now K-05 v1.8 (DL-438; CQ-194), then T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader with the business's live-owner state, each in its own minor version at the next free number (DL-391); and open gaps 48 to 51.

## v1.8 (T-1102): who gave a texts yes, and for which booking

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1099's handoff (`ops/cards/T-1099.md`; DL-130), carded as T-1102 and renumbered by DL-344, DL-364, DL-391, DL-438 and DL-450. Limit 8 (C-125; `runtime/src/dispatch/limit8/limit8.ts` `yesAfterStop`, T-1099) counts a texts yes newer than a STOP as turning texts back on only when it is the person's own word sent from the number that stopped: START, for every business, or a fresh yes on a new booking, for that booking (RULES 7.1, 7.5; S13-32, MASTER-FLOW-A lines 7214, 7215 and 7242). v1.2's `sendState.textsConsent` (item 28, default 35) gives the newest covering row's id, business, `revoked` and time, but not who gave it or for which booking, so limit 8 reads every yes as not known (`notKnown`): the stop stands for every row, the person's own fresh yes included. **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.8.schema.json` holds every v1.7 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.7.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.6, v1.5, v1.4, v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all eight files as they are; the changed ones, `sendState.response` and `sendState.exchange`, take two new optional fields in `textsConsent`, and `sendState.response` gives each of v1.2's other fields by `$ref` to v1.2's own definition of it (v1.2 defines them inline, and v1.3 to v1.7 keep them unchanged). So every v1 to v1.7 exchange is a v1.8 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.8). New definitions: `textsConsentRead` (the row with its two new fields, optional), `textsConsentV1dot8` (the same, both required) and `sendState.v1dot8Response` (the answer a v1.8 call gives). **No schema change:** K-29 v1's `consents` row holds both facts (item 47, "Why no K-29 minor version"), read with K-04 v1's `turns` as migrated (0009, 0013), with the grants `rosie_job` holds (`SELECT` on `consents` and `turns`: 0012, 0013); no K-29 minor version, column, grant, index or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's read (a new database task) and the send-state port's mapping (a new runtime task): "Consumers of v1.8"; whether a fresh yes's booking was made after the stop (open gap 52); whether a stopped number's own fresh yes clears the flag (QUESTIONS.md CQ-74: only START does, the default in use).

### The rule v1.8 adds

47. **`sendState`'s `textsConsent` says who gave the yes and for which booking** (T-1099's handoff; RULES 7.1, 7.5; S13-32 lines 7214, 7215 and 7242). **The read:** in item 28's own read-only transaction (one moment), as `rosie_job` with no business scope, the newest covering row is chosen as v1.2 chooses it (default 35, unchanged), and the call also reads that row's `channel`, `turn_id`, `handle_id` and `booking_id` (K-29 v1 `consents`) and, when `turn_id` is not null, that turn's `handle_id` (K-04 v1 `turns`, by its primary key). Then:
    - **`fromContact`** is true when, and only when, the row is a yes (`revoked` false) given by text (`channel` text), and either
      - a turn of the contact's own handle wrote it: `turn_id` names a turn whose `handle_id` is the row's `handle_id`. A turn batches one handle's texts (K-04 v1 `turns`: "a batch of a handle's texts"; arch 01 line 59), and an exact yes is bound to the read-back it answers in its turn (arch 01 line 89; K-29 `consents.turn_id`), so a yes given by text in that turn came from the number the row covers. A clock turn on the handle carries no text, and no frozen writer records a yes in one: a yes is the person's own word (RULES 7.1), and K-09's clock firing writes only a work phone's no answer, which withdraws (`recordStopOrStart`); or
      - it is a START recorded with no turn: `turn_id` and `business_id` both null. K-29 v1 lets only a STOP's or a START's row have no business (`consents_every_business_only_texts`; `business_id`: "Null only on a texts row from a STOP or a START"), a row that does not withdraw is a START, and K-09 v1 records START only from the phone's own START text, in its turn or written on return by its actor (`recordStopOrStart`; `start-recorded.json`; `refuse-start-from-page.json`), so a START with no turn is that number's own text, written on return.

      It is false for every other row: a yes the business entered (the owner's '1 Yes' by text is a turn of the owner's handle: `send-state-business-yes`; on the Today page it is channel page), a page's, an outside AI's or an email's yes, a yes by text from another handle (a parent's, a payer's: S13-32 line 7214), a yes no turn wrote that is not a START (default 79), an email contact's row (default 80), and every row that withdraws (default 82).

    - **`bookingId`** is the row's `booking_id` as stored (default 81): the booking a fresh yes covers (`send-state-own-yes-one-booking`), and null on START's row for every business (`send-state-own-start`) and on a STOP's (`send-state-stop-row`).

    **Who reads it, and how** (T-1099; C-125): limit 8 reads both only while the phone is stopped, and after `revoked`. A yes lifts the stop for a row only when `fromContact` is true and the yes is newer than `phone.stopAt`: for every row when it names no business (START), else only for a row about the booking it names (`otherBooking` otherwise); a missing field is not known and lifts nothing. A row the person's own yes lifts goes on to the chat check, where a stopped phone's closed chat holds it (K-04 `phones_stop_closes_chat`), so under CQ-74's default (only START clears the flag) nothing goes to a stopped number before START, but a safety line or a stop reply.

    **The stop and the yes** (for the security reviewer):
    - **`stopped` is the phone's flag, never derived from a consent row** (STATE note 10; T-1099; default 84): `phone.stopped` and `phone.stopAt` are K-04 `phones` as stored, written with the STOP's or the START's row in one commit (K-29 README); `fromContact` and `bookingId` describe the consent row alone.
    - **The business's word is never the person's own** (RULES 7.1; S13-32 line 7215): a yes the business entered, by text or on a page, reads false, and so does a yes by text from any handle but the one the row covers.
    - **A row that withdraws is never a yes:** its `fromContact` is false, and the schema refuses true (`refuse-send-state-stop-read-as-own-yes`), so no reader that skips `revoked` can take a STOP for a yes that lifts it.
    - **It fails closed where the row does not show its source:** a yes no turn wrote that is not a START reads false (default 79), even one that came from the contact, as K-29's `seed-chat-link.json` writes Ben's ready-text yes with no turn.
    - **A v1.8 answer always states both** (`sendState.v1dot8Response`; `refuse-send-state-yes-source-left-out`, `refuse-send-state-booking-left-out`), so a missing field means an answer of v1.2 to v1.7, which limit 8 reads as not known.
    - **Whether the booking is new is not read** (T-1099's security note a; default 83; open gap 52): RULES 7.5 and S13-32 line 7242 name "a fresh yes on a new booking", and K-04's own `phones.stopped` "a new booking's texts after its fresh yes from this phone", but no frozen part stores when a booking was made, so v1.8 gives the booking a yes names, not its age. An own yes for a booking made before the stop lifts that booking's rows at limit 8 only as far as the chat check, where they wait while the stop stands: nothing is sent before START.

    **Why no K-29 minor version** (the card: "if K-29's consent row cannot hold either fact, the freeze names the K-29 minor version and the migration task it needs instead of guessing"): `bookingId` is a K-29 column (`consents.booking_id`). `fromContact` is held by the row's own frozen columns read with the turn that wrote it: `channel` ("where it was given"), `turn_id` ("the turn that committed the row") and K-04's `turns.handle_id` ("the handle whose texts it batches") show who sent a yes given by text in a turn, and `business_id` null with `revoked` false shows a START, which K-09 v1 records only from that number's own text. Every frozen writer of a texts yes by text writes it in a turn (a staff member's ready text rides as a core action of its turn: K-09 v1 README; a read-back's yes is bound to its turn: arch 01 line 89; the owner's '1 Yes' by text is his turn's), or it is a START. So K-29 v1 holds both facts, and the read needs no new column (default 78). A writer that ever records a contact's own yes outside a turn, other than a START, is open gap 53, which names the K-29 minor version and migration it would need; until then such a row reads false, the safe side.

**Keywords beyond ADR-005's list:** `textsConsentRead` and `textsConsentV1dot8` hold default 82 as an `if` and `then` on `revoked`, as v1's `eventEntry` and `outboxRowNew` hold their rules. The generated types do not hold it, so a producer proves its answers on the v1.8 fixtures and against the schema, not by the type alone (ADR-005: "a contract that needs one says so in its README").

**`sendState.v1dot8Response`** is the answer a v1.8 call gives: every field of `sendState.response`, with `textsConsent`, when there is one, holding both fields (`textsConsentV1dot8`). `sendState.response` takes them as optional only so that v1.2 to v1.7's answers, which have neither, stay valid and every earlier exchange is a v1.8 exchange (a minor version only adds, as v1.6's `businessSensitive` did); a v1.8 call always writes them, and its producer checks every answer it gives against `sendState.v1dot8Response`. `sendState.exchange`'s outcome names both answers, `sendState.v1dot8Response` first; they overlap, which `anyOf` allows, so an answer with both fields is taken by both and one without them by `sendState.response` alone. `npm run contracts:types` gives each its own type: `SendStateV1Dot8Response`, both fields required, for the producer's answer, and `SendStateResponse`, both optional, for a consumer that also reads earlier answers.

### Consumers of v1.8

Each moves to v1.8 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.7 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, `sendState` in db/repo (a new task: `db/repo/send-state.ts` with `db/repo/consent.ts`'s `newestTextsConsent`), item 47's read inside item 28's transaction as `rosie_job`; v1.8 needs no migration.
- **Runtime engineer:** C-124 the outbox dispatcher, whose send-state port (`runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` `sendStateOf`, T-562) maps `fromContact` and `bookingId` across unchanged (a new task), and C-125 limit 8, which reads them as its `yesAfterStop` already does, with nothing of its own to change.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.8`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.8 to `WRITTEN_FOR` with a mend for each refused fixture (`refuse-send-state-yes-source-left-out`: twin `send-state-business-yes`, its outcome; `refuse-send-state-booking-left-out`: twin `send-state-own-yes-one-booking`, its outcome; `refuse-send-state-stop-read-as-own-yes`: twin `send-state-stop-row`, its outcome), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after the database engineer's read, its chains running v1's plan and claim steps as well as `commitTurn`.

What the two new tasks must hold to:

- The database engineer's read runs item 47 as written, inside `sendState`'s one read-only transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope: the newest covering row picked as v1.2 picks it (default 35), its `channel`, `turn_id`, `handle_id`, `booking_id`, `business_id` and `revoked`, and its turn's `handle_id` by the turn's id. It always writes both fields, types its answer as `SendStateV1Dot8Response` and checks every answer against `sendState.v1dot8Response` in its tests. It tests each v1.8 fixture's chain giving its outcome; each v1.2 send-state fixture giving its outcome with the two fields added (`send-state-stopped`: `fromContact` false, `bookingId` null; the other four have no texts row); a yes by text in a turn of another handle, a page's yes and an email contact's yes (each false); a START with no turn (true, no booking) beside a yes with no turn that is not a START (false); and a STOP (false). It reads no row's words.
- The runtime engineer's port maps `fromContact` and `bookingId` across unchanged when the answer holds them, and leaves them out when it does not, so limit 8 reads them as not known; it never derives `stopped`, or its end, from `textsConsent` (STATE note 10). It tests the v1.8 fixtures through the port and limit 8: `send-state-stop-row` and `send-state-own-yes-one-booking` drop their row (a STOP; another booking's yes), `send-state-business-yes` and `send-state-own-start` let it on to the other checks (not stopped).

### What v1.8 leaves for later minor versions

## v1.9 (T-1226): a repeated delivery receipt says what it repeats

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): join 13's round 1 Need 2 (`ops/cards/T-564.md`) and STATE note 38, from T-255's handoff, carded by DL-466 and recorded as QUESTIONS.md CQ-201. The question's clock row arms at its first delivery receipt (arch 02 line 127): `markPart` says `firstDelivery` on the mark that records the row's first delivery (item 4), and the dispatcher hands that answer to the clock arming (C-124's `handleDeliveryReceipt`, `runtime/src/dispatch/dispatcher.ts`; C-055's arming, T-255), whose `armClock` is safe to repeat (item 5). A crash between `markPart`'s commit and `armClock` leaves the part delivered and the clock row waiting, and the gateway never answers Linq, so Linq sends the receipt again (arch 02 line 109: "Write fails: 503, and the provider retries; Linq retries for 30 minutes"). That receipt is a repeat: nothing is written, the answer says `repeated` with `firstDelivery` false (`db/repo/outbox.ts` `markPartV12`), and nothing in it says whether the delivery it repeats was the row's first, so the dispatcher stops on it and the arming is lost for good: the delivered question takes the never-delivered path (arch 02 line 127; STATE note 37). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `data-access.v1.9.schema.json` holds every v1.8 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.8.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.7, v1.6, v1.5, v1.4, v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all nine files as they are; the changed ones, `markPart.response` and `markPart.exchange`, take one new optional field, `repeatedDelivery`, and `markPart.response` gives each of v1.1's fields by `$ref` to v1.1's own definition of it (v1.1 defines them inline, and v1.2 to v1.8 keep them unchanged). So every v1 to v1.8 exchange is a v1.9 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.9). New definitions: `repeatedDelivery` (the delivery a repeat repeats: whether it was the row's first, and when) and `markPart.v1dot9Response` (the answer a v1.9 call gives, the field required). **No schema change:** the call reads only K-04 v1's `outbox_parts.delivered_at` of the row's own part tries (0010), which item 15's transaction already reads under its locks; no column, grant, index or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's answer (T-1227) and the dispatcher's receipt handling (T-1228): "Consumers of v1.9"; the arming's question-facts read and a handle's actor name (open gap 25; QUESTIONS.md CQ-200).

### The rule v1.9 adds

48. **`markPart`'s answer to a repeated delivery receipt says what it repeats** (STATE note 38; CQ-201; arch 02 lines 109 and 127). A `delivered` mark whose part is already `delivered` is a repeat, as in v1 (item 4): nothing is written, and the answer says `repeated` true and `firstDelivery` false. From v1.9 it also says what it repeats, in **`repeatedDelivery`**, read in the mark's own transaction under the row's lock, from the row's own part tries (`outbox_parts` by `outbox_row_id`, which item 15's moves already read: db/repo locks the row, then its parts, `FOR UPDATE`), so no mark of another part of the row can come between:
    - **`firstOfRow`**: true when no other part try of the row, of either part and any try, holds a `delivered_at` earlier than this part try's own, a tie counting as first; false when one does (default 85). The delivery the repeat names was the row's first (`mark-a7-03-delivered-repeat`), or a later part's (`mark-a7-03-poll-delivered-repeat`).
