    - **`deliveredAt`**: this part try's `delivered_at` as stored, the `at` the recorded mark wrote, never the repeat's own `at` (default 86).

    `repeatedDelivery` is null on every other answer: a `delivered` mark that records a delivery, whose `firstDelivery` says whether it is the row's first, as v1 says (`mark-a7-03-poll-delivered`), and every `attempt`, `sent` and `failed` mark, a repeat or not, since only a delivery receipt arms a clock (arch 02 line 127; default 87). `firstDelivery` keeps v1's meaning: only the mark that records the row's first delivery says it, and a repeat never does (default 88).

    **Who reads it, and how** (C-124 with C-055): the dispatcher hands a delivered mark to the clock arming when `firstDelivery` is true, as now, and from v1.9 also when it is `repeated` and `repeatedDelivery.firstOfRow` is true: then as a first delivery whose time is `repeatedDelivery.deliveredAt`, with the row, part, try and question the answer names. `armClock` is safe to repeat (item 5): a clock row already armed answers `repeated` with its first arming, and a fired or cancelled one `clockClosed`, so a repeat whose first arming went through arms nothing twice, and a repeat whose arming the crash lost arms the clock as that arming would have, the same question from the same delivery time (`mark-a7-03-delivered-repeat`, then `arm-clock-a7-03-after-4006`'s request). A repeat of a later part's delivery (`firstOfRow` false), an answer with `repeatedDelivery` null, and one without it (v1 to v1.8, read as not known) hand nothing on, as today (default 89).

    **Why a new field, not `firstDelivery` kept true on a repeat of the first delivery** (the card's question):
    1. **`firstDelivery` keeps its meaning.** From v1 it is said by the mark that records the row's first delivery ("records `delivered_at` once and says `firstDelivery` when no part of the row was delivered before": item 4), and every answer v1 to v1.8 give to a repeat says false (`markPartV12`; join 13's `hop13.worker.test.ts` and e2e row 13 assert it). True on a repeat would change what a frozen field means in an answer v1.8 already gives: a changed meaning, which is a breaking change (build prompt 6.4; `contracts/README.md` "Versions"), a major version with the CTO's OK and a migration task per consumer.
    2. **Its readers would take a repeat for a second first delivery.** The arming acts only on `firstDelivery` (`runtime/src/clocks/arming.ts`: `notFirst` otherwise), the receipt's span records it (`got.firstDelivery`), and `ReceiptResult` returns it. A field of its own says only what a repeat repeats, so every reader of `firstDelivery` is unchanged.
    3. **It fails closed.** An answer without the field is one of v1 to v1.8, read as not known: nothing is handed on, as today. The schema refuses a repeat that says `firstDelivery` (`refuse-mark-repeat-says-first-delivery`), a delivered repeat answered null (`refuse-mark-delivered-repeat-null`) and a v1.9 answer that leaves the field out (`refuse-mark-repeat-delivery-left-out`).

    **Why the record's times** (default 85): K-04 v1 stores when each part try was delivered (`outbox_parts.delivered_at`, the receipt's `at`: item 4), not which receipt was recorded first, so a repeat reads "the row's first delivery" from the times the record holds. It names the delivery that said `firstDelivery` whenever the receipts were recorded in the order of their own times: a row of one part, a retried receipt, and a second part sent once the words' receipt is in (K-07 rule 4). It can name another where a receipt was recorded after a later-timed one (the poll sent after its 10 s wait while the words' receipt was late, K-07 rule 4; a late receipt of an earlier try, K-07 rule 2). Then the earlier-timed delivery's repeat hands on, and `armClock` answers `repeated` if the clock is armed, or arms it from that earlier delivery; the repeat of the delivery that said `firstDelivery` does not, so if the crash lost its arming, that arming stays lost, as before v1.9 (open gap 54). A tie counts as first for each, so each hands on and `armClock` keeps the first arming.

    **Why the stored time** (default 86): the clock's due time and `armed_at` count from the delivery (arch 02 line 127; T-255's due rule; item 5), and a repeat need not carry the time the first receipt did. A retry of the webhook is the same event (K-06 v1 `duplicate-event.json`), but on SMS the receipt is Linq's `message.sent` or the SMS provider's receipt (arch 02 line 127), so one part can be reported delivered twice, at two times. With the stored time, the arming a repeat hands on is the lost one exactly, and no second report moves a clock's time.

    **The repeat and the clock** (for the security reviewer):
    - **A repeat arms nothing a first delivery would not have.** The clock is named by the answer's `openQuestionId`, the row's own, never by anything in the receipt (as T-564's security review found for a first delivery), and its time is the record's, never the receipt's. `armClock` keeps a clock row's first arming and never opens a fired or cancelled one (item 5: `repeated`, `clockClosed`), so a repeat cannot move a deadline, arm a clock twice or reopen a question a cancel withdrew.
    - **It reads and returns no more than it must.** v1.9 reads, under the locks item 15 already takes, the `delivered_at` of the row's own part tries, and answers a boolean and a time: no message id, key, address, number or words, and nothing of another row.
    - **It writes nothing**, as a repeat writes nothing in every version: no row, part, claim or entry changes, and its `row` is the row as it stands.
    - **Receipts reach `markPart` only through the gateway's verified webhook** (arch 02 line 109; C-044), which v1.9 does not change.

**Keywords beyond ADR-005's list:** `markPart.response` and `markPart.v1dot9Response` hold default 88 (an answer that names what it repeats is a repeat, and never says `firstDelivery`) as an `if` and `then` on `repeatedDelivery`, and `markPart.exchange` holds its tie to the request (a delivered mark answered `repeated` states an object; a mark that is no delivery states null) as an `if`, `then` and `else` on the request's `outcome`, as v1.8's `textsConsentRead` holds default 82. The generated types do not hold them, so a producer proves its answers on the v1.9 fixtures and against the schema, not by the type alone (ADR-005: "a contract that needs one says so in its README").

**`markPart.v1dot9Response`** is the answer a v1.9 call gives: every field of `markPart.response`, with `repeatedDelivery` and `row` (which every call from v1.1 writes: item 15) required. `markPart.response` takes them as optional only so that v1 to v1.8's answers, which have no `repeatedDelivery`, and v1's, which have no `row`, stay valid and every earlier exchange is a v1.9 exchange (a minor version only adds, as v1.8's `sendState.v1dot8Response` did); a v1.9 call always writes both, and its producer checks every answer it gives against `markPart.v1dot9Response`. `markPart.exchange`'s outcome names both answers, `markPart.v1dot9Response` first; they overlap, which `anyOf` allows, so an answer with the field is taken by both and one without it by `markPart.response` alone. `npm run contracts:types` gives each its own type: `MarkPartV1Dot9Response`, `repeatedDelivery` required, for the producer's answer, and `MarkPartResponse`, with it optional, for a consumer that also reads earlier answers.

### Consumers of v1.9

Each moves to v1.9 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.8 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, T-1227 (`db/repo/outbox.ts`, the dispatcher's `markPartV12`): item 48 inside item 15's transaction, under the locks it already takes; v1.9 needs no migration.
- **Runtime engineer:** C-124 the outbox dispatcher, whose receipt handling (`runtime/src/dispatch/dispatcher.ts` `handleDeliveryReceipt`, over the outbox port `runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` `realOutbox`) hands a repeat of a row's first delivery to C-055 the clock arming (T-1228), which takes it as a first delivery.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.9`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.9 to `WRITTEN_FOR` with a mend for each refused fixture (`refuse-mark-repeat-delivery-left-out`: twin `mark-a7-03-delivered-repeat`, its outcome; `refuse-mark-repeat-says-first-delivery`: the same twin, its outcome; `refuse-mark-delivered-repeat-null`: twin `mark-a7-03-poll-delivered-repeat`, its outcome), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after T-1227, its chains running v1's `commitTurn` and v1.2's plan, claim and marks.

What the two new tasks must hold to:

- The database engineer's answer (T-1227) runs item 48 as written, in `markPart`'s one transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope, from the part tries it has locked: on a `delivered` mark whose part is `delivered` it writes nothing and answers `repeated` true, `firstDelivery` false and `repeatedDelivery` with `firstOfRow` (no other part try of the row holds an earlier `delivered_at`; a tie is first) and `deliveredAt` (the part try's stored `delivered_at`, never the request's `at`); on every other mark it answers `repeatedDelivery` null. It always writes the field, types its answer as `MarkPartV1Dot9Response` and checks every answer against `markPart.v1dot9Response` in its tests. It tests each v1.9 fixture's chain giving its outcome; each earlier `markPart` fixture's chain giving its outcome with `repeatedDelivery` null added (none of them repeats a delivery); a repeat of a delivery with no other part delivered (true), of a later part's (false), of two parts delivered at one time (each true), of a part whose receipt was recorded after a later-timed one and of a late receipt of an earlier try after a 4006 (each by the times: default 85), and a repeated `sent` and `failed` mark (null). It reads no message id, key or words it does not read now.
- The runtime engineer's receipt handling (T-1228) hands a delivered mark to the clock arming when `firstDelivery` is true, as now, or when it is `repeated` and `repeatedDelivery.firstOfRow` is true, as a first delivery delivered at `repeatedDelivery.deliveredAt`, named by the answer's row, part, try and `openQuestionId`, never by the receipt; and nothing otherwise: a repeat of a later part's delivery, `repeatedDelivery` null, or an answer without it (an earlier version's), so a missing field is never read as a first delivery. The outbox port (`realOutbox`, T-562) builds the dispatcher's answer field by field, so it maps `repeatedDelivery` across unchanged, as it maps `row`, and the K-05 doubles the receipt tests run on answer it as v1.9 does. It tests a repeat of the first delivery after a crash before `armClock` (the clock armed once, at the stored time: `mark-a7-03-delivered-repeat`, then `arm-clock-a7-03-after-4006`'s request), the same repeat when the clock is already armed (`armClock` answers `repeated`: nothing armed twice, and no cancel and no sync, as T-255 builds a repeat), a repeat of a later part's delivery (`mark-a7-03-poll-delivered-repeat`: nothing handed on) and an answer without the field (nothing handed on), and keeps join 13's proof that a second delivered event arms nothing more.

### What v1.9 leaves for later minor versions

T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader with the business's live-owner state, each in its own minor version at the next free number from v1.14 (DL-391, DL-450, DL-466; v1.10 is T-1240's, DL-492, v1.11 T-1249's, DL-499, v1.12 T-1248's, DL-498, and v1.13 T-1277's, DL-526); the arming's question-facts read and a handle's actor name, which join 13 hands in beside `armClock` (open gap 25; CQ-200), now v1.10's (items 49 and 50); and the order receipts were recorded in (open gap 54).

## v1.10 (T-1240): the receipt's and the clock arming's reads

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the D-017 path audit's ordered list, its line F1 and rulings R3 and R4 (`ops/reviews/D-017-path-audit/`), carded by DL-492, with QUESTIONS.md CQ-200 and CQ-61. The first staging turn's delivery receipt and its clock arming reach PostgreSQL through reads no K-05 call named. The clock arming's question facts and a handle's actor name are handed in beside `armClock` and `cancelClock`, as T-255 built them (`runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` `ArmingCalls`; open gap 25), and CQ-200's default says "a later K-05 minor version would name both reads". The Linq verify's chat lookup is handed in too (`adapters/src/linq/webhook/parse.ts` `ParseLookups.chatHandle`), and without it a receipt cannot be parsed and the gateway answers 503 (CQ-61: "the gateway wiring passes chatHandle"). DL-468 binds the arming's calls in the same change as the outbox calls, since a receipt whose arming fails is answered 200 and never retried (the audit's R4), so the reads are named first. The card's fourth read, the handle's party for a turn (`runtime/src/turn/types.ts` `TurnDirectory.partyOf`), is not named here: the business it gives is a rule the sources leave to two readings (open gap 55), and the card's lead note has the others freeze without it. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.10, the next free number (DL-492; no other K-05 freeze was running). `data-access.v1.10.schema.json` holds every v1.9 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.9.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.8, v1.7, v1.6, v1.5, v1.4, v1.3, v1.2, v1.1 and v1), all ten files as they are, and changes none; it adds three calls and the definitions their exchanges use. So every v1 to v1.9 exchange is a v1.10 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.10). **No schema change:** the reads read K-04 v1's `open_questions`, `businesses`, `bookings`, `reservations`, `clock_rows`, `handles` and `threads` as migrated (0002, 0004, 0005, 0007 to 0009), with the grants `rosie_job` holds (0012), and write nothing; no column, grant, index or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's three reads and the runtime's bindings ("Consumers of v1.10"); how the deployed Worker takes the role each read names (T-1239's design record); and what "What v1.10 does not name" lists.

### The calls v1.10 adds

| Call                | Item | Port it serves                                                                                                                             | Scope and role                                                                                                         | Repeating it | Time budget                                                                                   |
| ------------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readQuestionFacts` | 49   | the clock arming's `facts.questionFacts` (`runtime/src/clocks/types.ts` `QuestionFactsReader`; C-055)                                      | runtime, as `rosie_job` with no business scope; each read of a business-scoped table names the question's own business | Safe: a read | Inside the gateway's 1 s for the receipt (arch 02 line 109), beside `markPart` and `armClock` |
| `readHandle`        | 50   | the clock directory's `actorNameOf` (`runtime/src/clocks/types.ts` `ClockDirectory`; the arming, C-055, and the sweep and Fire now, C-054) | runtime, as `rosie_job` with no business scope (`handles`: scope none)                                                 | Safe: a read | As item 49 for a receipt; none in the sources for the sweep                                   |
| `readChatHandle`    | 51   | the Linq verify's `chatHandle` lookup (`adapters/src/linq/webhook/parse.ts` `ParseLookups`; C-044)                                         | runtime, as `rosie_job` with no business scope (`threads`, `handles`: scope none)                                      | Safe: a read | Inside the gateway's 1 s (arch 02 line 109)                                                   |

Each reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, in one read-only transaction (one moment), writes nothing, and can answer `transient`, as every K-05 call does. **Why the runtime side, as `rosie_job`:** each is a read of the runtime's own hops, the receipt path and the clock arming, which run across businesses by handle (K-04 v1 rule 4: "the jobs that work across businesses by handle (the actor's guard, the dispatcher, the clock sweep)"; K-05 "Scope": `armClock`, `cancelClock` and `markPart` run there), and `handles` and `threads` are tables only `rosie_job` reads (0012). None is a Tool API read, and `rosie_app`, the Tool API's role, holds no grant on `handles` or `threads` (0012: "none on a table that spans businesses"), so none runs as `rosie_app`. No read uses a definer function or a table owner's rights: each reads through `rosie_job`'s own grants and runtime policies (0012). Ids are compared byte by byte, never parsed (CQ-09).

### How each v1.10 call works (K-04 v1 as migrated)

49. **`readQuestionFacts`** (the facts T-255's due rule reads about one open question, beyond its receipt: `runtime/src/clocks/types.ts` `QuestionFacts`; `runtime/src/clocks/due.ts`; arch 02 line 127; closes open gap 25 for the clock arming). **Role and scope:** as `rosie_job` with no business scope, the side `armClock` and `cancelClock` run on (item 5), since the arming is a runtime job on the receipt path (C-055) and `open_questions`, `businesses`, `bookings`, `reservations` and `clock_rows` are business-scoped tables `rosie_job` reads behind its runtime policies (0012). It reads the question first (`open_questions` by its primary key); none answers `notFound` (table `open_questions`). Every later read names the question's own business beside its key, so the answer holds nothing of another business even under a role row-level security does not bind: the business (`businesses` by `open_questions.business_id`), the booking (`bookings` by `open_questions.booking_id` at that business) and the booking's reservations (`reservations` by `booking_id` at that business: `reservations_booking_idx`); a question with no business reads none of the three. It answers, in the port's own names:
    - **`clockLine`**: `open_questions.clock` as stored, null when the step has none (step 9's default: arch 02 line 127);
    - **`businessZone`**: `businesses.time_zone` of the question's business; null for a question with no business (a sign-up's, before S1-09 writes one);
    - **`visit`**: the booking's `flow` and its start, the earliest `lower(during)` among the booking's reservations whose state is `held` or `booked` (default 90); null for a question about no booking and for a booking with no such reservation, which has no time; never an address or an address's zone (default 91);
    - **`neverDelivered`**: the question's clock row of kind `never_delivered` (`clock_rows` by `open_question_id`) with its version, chosen as default 92 says; null when it has none, as every question has until a commit makes one (STATE note 37; turn 1's commit makes none).

    The question's own clock row (kind `question`) is not read: `armClock`, named by the question, finds it in its own transaction (item 5). Nothing of the question's options, read-back, versions read, handle, person or status is read or answered. **The caller** (C-055, through `ArmingCalls.questionFacts`) reads it once per first delivery it is handed, before `armClock`, naming the question by markPart's `openQuestionId`, never by anything in the receipt. It maps the answer to `QuestionFacts` field by field and gives the port's `visit.addressZone` and `visit.address` null (default 91), so a flow B visit's zone falls to the business's, as join 13's default already has it (geo null; CQ-05, CQ-08). `notFound` arms nothing and the arming reports `questionNotFound`. What the arming does when the read fails is its own (`ARMING_TRIES`; DL-494's owed follow-up card 4 would answer a failed arming 503, so that Linq's retry, a repeat v1.9 hands on, arms it).

50. **`readHandle`** (a K-04 handle by its id, for the actor the arming, the minute sweep and Fire now reach a clock row's handle by: `runtime/src/clocks/actors.ts` `actorOf`; `ArmingCalls.actorNameOf`; CQ-200). **Role and scope:** as `rosie_job` with no business scope: `handles` is scoped none, and only the runtime side reads it (0012). It reads `handles` by its primary key, the columns `kind` and `address` alone, and answers them in K-06 v1's `handle` shape, with `handleId` the request's; `handle` is null when no row has the id (default 95). **The actor name** (default 93): the consumer builds it from the answer with the gateway's own function, `runtime/src/gateway/seal.ts` `actorNameOf` (`<kind>:<address>`), the one rule the gateway names a handle's actor by when it hands the sealed text over (`runtime/src/gateway/hand-off.ts`) and the turn by when it asks for its party (`runtime/src/turn/turn.ts`), never a copy of that rule, so the arming syncs the very actor that holds the handle's texts and alarm. No K-05 answer holds an actor name: the rule that builds one is the runtime's (default 52: what K-04 stores is returned as stored, and the rules over it stay their owners'). **The address in the answer** (for the security reviewer): a phone number or an email address, which the actor's name must hold, since it is the Durable Object's name (`idFromName`). It is read on the runtime side alone, and the consumer uses it only to name the actor, never logging it or putting it in a span, an error or an entry, as the gateway keeps the actor name (T-243). Nothing else of the handle, its people, its phone, its threads or its keys is read.

51. **`readChatHandle`** (the phone handle a Linq chat id names, the number a receipt belongs to: CQ-61; `adapters/src/linq/webhook/parse.ts` `receiptHandle`). **Role and scope:** as `rosie_job` with no business scope: `threads` and `handles` are scoped none (0012). It reads `threads` by `linq_chat_id` (`threads_linq_chat_key`: one row at most), the columns `kind` and `handle_id`, then that thread's handle (`handles` by its primary key: `kind` and `address`). It answers the handle, `handleId` and K-06 v1's `handle` with kind `phone` and the number in E.164 form, when the thread is of kind `chat` and its handle of kind `phone`; else both fields null (default 94): no thread holds the chat id (only S1-01's commit writes a number's chat thread: open gap 48), a thread of kind `email` holds it (K-04's `threads_chat_or_email` leaves `linq_chat_id` free), or the thread's handle is an email handle. **The caller** (C-044, the gateway's Linq verify): a receipt (`message.sent`, `message.delivered`, `message.failed`), Rosie's own reaction and a failed poll carry the chat, not the number, so the adapter asks `chatHandle` for the chat's handle and builds the K-06 event with its number; a null answer, or a read that fails, makes the adapter throw `receiptHandleUnknown`, and the gateway answers 503, so Linq retries (CQ-61's default, unchanged). The binding gives the adapter `handle.address`, or null. **For the security reviewer:** the request carries the chat id alone, and the answer the handle's id and number, nothing of the thread's booking, reply address or version, and never the chat id. The number is the one the receipt's K-06 event carries as its handle (K-06 v1: "that Rosie's part went to for a receipt"), and neither it nor the chat id is logged or put in a span or an entry (K-09 v1.5 default 135). A receipt is placed only on the handle whose chat the thread records, never a guessed one, and what the receipt marks is still found by the part's message id and key (items 4 and 31), never by this handle.

### What v1.10 does not name

- **The handle's party for a turn** (`TurnDirectory.partyOf`: the handle's K-04 id, the business the turn's calls run under, the person, and the zone its texts' dates resolve in). Its business is a rule two readings fit (open gap 55), for the lead's ruling; until a later minor version names the read, the turn's directory stays closed on staging (`runtime/src/wiring/closed.ts`), and no turn runs there.
- **Where the arrival safety read's result is kept until the commit** (`TurnSafety.resultsOf`): T-1241's design record.
- **`idsOf` and `isStopped`** for the danger and outage paths (`runtime/src/safety/types.ts` `HandleDirectory.idsOf` and the safety deps' `isStopped`; the audit's part a, item 4): neither the first staging turn nor its receipt reaches them.
- **The test-phone page's reads** (a session's standing, the roster, a phone's state by number) and **the copy deps' two sources** (DL-427): a later minor version, if they are ruled K-05's (the audit's F2; QUESTIONS.md CQ-206, CQ-214).
- **How the deployed Worker takes the role** each read names: T-1239's design record (CQ-208).

### Consumers of v1.10

Each moves to v1.10 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.9 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, the three reads in db/repo (new tasks: the audit's D1b, `readQuestionFacts` and `readHandle`, beside `db/repo/clocks.ts`; D1c, `readChatHandle`, in `db/repo/threads.ts`), each as `rosie_job` on the runtime side; v1.10 needs no migration.
- **Runtime engineer:** C-055 the clock arming, whose `ArmingCalls.questionFacts` and `ArmingCalls.actorNameOf` (`runtime/src/wiring/real.ts`) bind `readQuestionFacts` and `readHandle` in the change that binds the outbox calls (DL-468; the audit's P5); C-054 the minute sweep and staging's Fire now, whose clock directory names a row's actor through `readHandle` the same way once their binding is carded (the audit's part c); and C-044 the gateway, whose Linq verify takes `chatHandle` over `readChatHandle` (the audit's CHAT card, after D1c).
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.10`, a new task (the audit's Q1). Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.10 to `WRITTEN_FOR` with a mend for each refused fixture (`refuse-question-facts-caller-business`: twin `read-question-facts-s1-02`, its request; `refuse-question-facts-visit-address`: twin `read-question-facts-a7-03`, its outcome; `refuse-read-handle-phone-not-e164`: twin `read-handle-sam`, its outcome; `refuse-chat-handle-email`: twin `read-chat-handle-alex`, its outcome), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after the database engineer's reads, writing `read-chat-handle-sam`'s one noted row, S1-01's thread, before its exchange.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **The database engineer's reads** run items 49 to 51 as written, each in one read-only transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope, writing nothing, and answer each v1.10 fixture's outcome; each answer is checked against its v1.10 definition in their tests. `readQuestionFacts` names the question's own business in each read of `businesses`, `bookings` and `reservations` and reads none of them for a question with no business; it is tested with a booking whose held or booked reservations start at two times (the earlier), a released and a waiting reservation of the booking (neither read), a booking whose reservations are all released (`visit` null), a question with no business (`businessZone` and `visit` null), two never-delivered rows (default 92) and a fired one alone (still given). `readHandle` selects `kind` and `address` alone. `readChatHandle` is tested with a thread of kind `email` that holds a chat id and with a chat thread of an email handle (each: both fields null). None selects a visit's address, a message's words or any column of `sealing_keys`, and their tests check that no statement names one.
- **The runtime engineer's bindings** map each answer across as items 49 to 51 say: `questionFacts` field by field to `QuestionFacts`, with `visit.addressZone` and `visit.address` null, `notFound` to the port's `NotFoundError` and `transient` to its `TransientError`; `actorNameOf` as null when `handle` is null, else the gateway's own `actorNameOf(handle)`, imported, never written again; `chatHandle` as `handle.address` when found, else null, a thrown error or `transient` also null (the adapter's 503). None logs a number, an address or a chat id, or puts one in a span. They test hop 13 with answers shaped as `read-question-facts-s1-02`, `read-handle-sam` and `read-chat-handle-sam` (S1-02's clock armed once, due 8pm the day after delivery, the actor `phone:+15555550100` synced, the receipt placed on Sam's number), `read-chat-handle-unknown` (503, nothing marked) and `read-handle-unknown` (no actor told; the arming still armed).

## v1.11 (T-1249): the handle's party for a turn

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1240's NEED, README open gap 55, carded by DL-499 as a rule about who may act for a business, the architect's to rule with the security reviewer (QUESTIONS.md CQ-220). A turn's first step is its directory's party read (`runtime/src/turn/types.ts` `TurnDirectory.partyOf` and `TurnParty`; `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts`, before the texts are opened; `runtime/src/turn/park.ts`, at a park): the handle's K-04 id, the business the turn's calls run under (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`), the person (`caller.personId`) and the zone its texts' dates resolve in (K-02 v1 `text.sentAt.zone`). No K-05 call named it, so on staging the directory stays closed (`runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` `StateReadInputs.turnDirectory` null) and no turn runs (the D-017 path audit's R3). The lead's note binds the ruling: whatever cannot be decided from the rows fails closed, the turn parked or refused, and never resolves to a guess. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.11, the next free number (DL-499; T-1248, the next K-05 freeze, had not started). `data-access.v1.11.schema.json` holds every v1.10 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.10.schema.json` (which refers on to v1.9 and through it to every earlier version), all eleven files as they are, and changes none; it adds one call and the definitions its exchange uses. So every v1 to v1.10 exchange is a v1.11 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.11). **No schema change:** the read reads K-04 v1's `handles`, `person_handles`, `person_roles` and `businesses` and K-23 v1's `companies` as migrated (0002, 0004, 0012, 0019), with the grants `rosie_job` holds, and writes nothing; no column, grant, index or migration is added. **No other contract changes:** test owner 1's turn runs under `biz-lees-barbershop` by item 52's rule from T-1148's seed as it stands (`read-turn-party-test-owner-1`), so ADR-013 default 130, K-09 v1.5 default 130 and its turn 1 fixtures (`build-frontier-s1-01.json`, `commit-s1-01.json`, `compose-input-s1-02.json`) and the seed hold unchanged. K-21 v1's test roster, the staging-only source gap 55 named (CQ-206), is not read: the rows give test owner 1's business alike on every environment, and a turn's business never comes from a list the record does not hold. **Not here:** db/repo's read and the runtime's binding ("Consumers of v1.11"); the safety results store (T-1241); what "What v1.11 does not name" lists.

### The call v1.11 adds

| Call            | Item | Port it serves                                                                                                                       | Scope and role                                                                                                                                                                        | Repeating it | Time budget                                                                                  |
| --------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readTurnParty` | 52   | the turn's directory, `TurnDirectory.partyOf` (`runtime/src/turn/types.ts`), which a run of the handle actor's turn and its park ask | runtime, as `rosie_job` with no business scope (`handles`, `person_handles`: scope none; every business's `person_roles`, `businesses` and `companies` behind their runtime policies) | Safe: a read | None in the sources; once per run of a turn, before its texts are opened, and once at a park |

It reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**, in one read-only transaction (one moment), writes nothing, and can answer `transient`, as every K-05 call does. **Why the runtime side, as `rosie_job`:** the read decides which business a turn runs under, so it runs before there is a business scope, and it must see every business at which the handle's people hold a role or own a company to know whether they name one (K-04 v1 rule 4: "the jobs that work across businesses by handle (the actor's guard, the dispatcher, the clock sweep)"; `handleTargets`, the guard's read of the same handle, runs there: item 27). `handles` and `person_handles` are tables only `rosie_job` reads, and `rosie_app`, the Tool API's role, holds no grant on them (0012); `person_roles` and `businesses` (0012) and `companies` (0019) are business-scoped tables `rosie_job` reads behind their runtime policies, which with no scope show every row, a company with no business yet included (K-23 README rule 5). No definer function and no table owner's rights: each read goes through `rosie_job`'s own grants and policies. Ids are compared byte by byte, never parsed (CQ-09).

### How the v1.11 call works (K-04 v1 and K-23 v1 as migrated)

52. **`readTurnParty`** (the handle's party for a turn: `runtime/src/turn/types.ts` `TurnParty`; closes open gap 55). **Role and scope:** as `rosie_job` with no business scope (above). In one read-only transaction it reads:
    1. **The handle**, by the request's address (`handles_address_key`): its `id` and `kind`. None, or one of another kind, answers `unknown`, every field null (default 96).
    2. **Its people**, `person_handles` by `handle_id` (`person_handles_handle_idx`), in `person_id` order: `personId` is the one person when there is exactly one, else null (default 97).
    3. **Their roles**, `person_roles` by `person_id` (`person_roles_person_idx`): each row's `business_id`, `role`, and whether its `ended_at` is null (live) or set (ended).
    4. **Their companies**, `companies` by `owner_person_id` (`companies_owner_idx`): each one's `business_id`, null for a company with no business yet.
    5. **For each business a company of theirs names**, its live owner roles (`person_roles` by `business_id` with role `owner` and `ended_at` null: `person_roles_business_role_idx`), and whose they are.
    6. **The zone** of the one business it answers: `businesses.time_zone` by `id`.

    The scopes the rows name are the business of each live role of the handle's people and the business of each company they own, a company with no business yet naming "no business yet". It answers by this rule, the first case that applies (default 100):
    - **`anotherOwner`** (`businessUndecided`): a company one of them owns names a business whose live owner role is held by a person who does not write from the handle. The business has one account owner (S2-02; RULES 6.8), and it is someone else, so the company row is left from before a hand-over (S11-100: "Sam's access ends at the handover time") or by a hand-over left half done (default 58), and the rows do not say the business is theirs to act for (default 99).
    - **`roleEnded`** (`businessUndecided`): a company one of them owns names a business at which none of them holds the live owner role and one of them holds a role that ended: they left it or were removed (S11-100; RULES 8.7: "Someone leaves: their last day is set. Sign-in, texts, day-list links and code views end"), so the company naming them is not enough (default 99).
    - **`severalBusinesses`** (`businessUndecided`): the scopes are more than one: live roles or companies at two businesses or more, or a business beside a company with no business yet (default 98).
    - **`atBusiness`**: the scopes are one business: the turn runs under it, with its zone.
    - **`noBusiness`**: there is no scope (no live role and no company), or the only one is "no business yet": the turn runs with no business, on the runtime side, and its zone is null (defaults 98 and 101).

    Case by case, as the card lists them. **A person with one live role** (`read-turn-party-one-role`): its business. **Live roles at several businesses** (`read-turn-party-several-businesses`, Sam's three owner roles in K-04's seed): `severalBusinesses`. **No live role and one owned company** (`read-turn-party-test-owner-1`, test owner 1): the company's business, while no other person holds its live owner role and none of the handle's people left it, which is how a sign-up stands, since journey 1 writes the company (S1-04) and the business (S1-09) and no owner role (open gap 58); with no business yet, no business (`read-turn-party-sign-up-before-business`). **Several owned companies** (`read-turn-party-several-companies`): `severalBusinesses`, unless none has a business yet. **An ex-owner after S11-100** (`read-turn-party-handed-over`, `read-turn-party-owner-role-ended`): `anotherOwner` or `roleEnded`, never the business, and never no business in its place. **A handle that is unknown** (`read-turn-party-unknown`): `unknown`. **The zone when there is no business** (`read-turn-party-first-text`): null, which the consumer reads as UTC (default 101).

    Nothing of a role, a company, another business or another person is answered, nor the businesses found: a party is the handle's id, one business or none, the person or none, and that business's zone or none; an undecided business is its reason, the handle's id and the person (default 102). The address is the request's, read on the runtime side alone, and never answered. **The caller** (C-046, through `TurnDirectory.partyOf`, for a run of the turn and for its park) maps the answer as "Consumers of v1.11" says, and no answer becomes a party it does not state: never a business picked from several, and never no business in place of one the rows cannot give.

### Who may act for a business, as the sources give it

- **The account owner, admins and staff act for the business, each by their role; anyone may ask, see prices and book at any door.** S13-31, the home step: "The business side: one account owner, admins like an office manager, and staff"; RULES 6.8: "Money, the team and settings are the owner's, or an admin the owner lets"; S13-27 rule 34: "Only the account owner, or an admin the owner lets, decides fees, refunds, the team and settings"; RULES 6.3: "Ask, see prices and times, book a new visit: anyone, at any door. Nothing on file is used or shown first." So a turn runs under the one business its sender is on record for (a live role, or the company of a business they are signing up), with no business when they are on record for none, and not at all when the rows name several or contradict each other; what the sender may do there is the gate's (limit 1), never this read's.
- **A hand-over ends the old owner's access.** S11-100: "Sam sells Lee's Barbershop to Hugo P. ... Sam's access ends at the handover time. Records stay with the business. Everything the old owner held ends at the handover"; "Rosie's team hands it over only on papers. Never: hand over on a text alone." RULES 6.9: "the new owner confirms by a code to their own phone, passes the ID check and links their own Stripe; later charges go only there". Someone who leaves: RULES 8.7, "Sign-in, texts, day-list links and code views end". So a company row still naming the old owner gives them nothing (default 99).
- **A login may own several companies, and the master asks which.** K-23: "one company is one business, a second company a second business on the same login" (S1-04); S11-15: "Pause which business?"; S13-27 rule 29: "Read the role from what the message is about. When two roles fit, ask which one." So several fail closed until a turn can ask (default 98; open gap 56).
- **A phone is not a person.** S13-27: "A phone is not a person" (rule 28: "One phone can be several people, and one person can hold several roles"); RULES 6.5: "Before a phone links to an old record, nothing on file is used or hinted at"; RULES 12.1: "Whoever holds the customer's phone can act as them." So the person is named only when the handle has one (default 97), and an erased person's link is gap 28's and gap 60's.
- **The Master Corrections:** C1 makes one Rosie number serve every business ("owner sign-up and support use it too"), so one handle can act for several businesses and the read must look across all of them; C9 and C10 (first contact by the person's own text; the owner's ID by upload) govern how sign-up proves the owner, not which business a turn runs under; and by MC section 2 the home steps quoted here (S2-02, S11-100, S13-27, S13-31) win over RULES' summary of them.

### What v1.11 does not name

- **`idsOf`** for the safety read's danger and outage paths (`runtime/src/safety/types.ts` `HandleDirectory.idsOf`): still a later version's ("What v1.10 does not name"). When it is named, its business follows item 52's rule, so a danger text's call and the turn's run name one business for one handle, or none.
- **A turn across several businesses** (open gap 56) and **the runtime's path for an undecided business** (open gap 57): cards of their own.
- **Who writes the owner's role and moves the company** at sign-up, hand-over and removal (open gaps 58 and 59): journey 1's and journey 11's lanes.
- **The test-phone page's reads and the copy deps' two sources**: T-1248, at the next free number (DL-498), now v1.12's (items 53 to 57).
- **How the deployed Worker takes `rosie_job`**: ADR-014 (T-1239), its job login behind `HYPERDRIVE_JOB`.

### Consumers of v1.11

Each moves to v1.11 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.10 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, `readTurnParty` in db/repo (a new task, beside `db/repo/targets.ts`, whose `handleTargets` reads the same handle's people and roles), as `rosie_job` on the runtime side; v1.11 needs no migration.
- **Runtime engineer:** C-046 the actor, whose turn's directory (`TurnDirectory.partyOf`, through the seam `runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` holds for it, `StateReadInputs.turnDirectory`) binds `readTurnParty` (a new task, after the database engineer's read and ADR-014's handle), and C-180 the park (`runtime/src/turn/park.ts`), which asks the same directory; and open gap 57's card for a business the rows leave undecided (C-046, C-051 the guard, C-180).
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.11`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.11 to `WRITTEN_FOR` after v1.10's task (the audit's Q1), with a mend for each refused fixture (`refuse-turn-party-caller-business`: twin `read-turn-party-test-owner-1`, its request; `refuse-turn-party-undecided-names-businesses`: twin `read-turn-party-several-businesses`, its outcome; `refuse-turn-party-business-zone-null`: twin `read-turn-party-test-owner-1`, its outcome; `refuse-turn-party-no-business-zone-utc`: twin `read-turn-party-first-text`, its outcome), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after the database engineer's read, writing the rows each fixture's note lists before its exchange.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **The database engineer's read** runs item 52 as written, in one read-only transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope, writing nothing, and answers each v1.11 fixture's outcome, each answer checked against its v1.11 definition in their tests. It selects `handles.id` and `kind` (never the address back), `person_handles.person_id`, `person_roles.business_id`, `person_id`, `role` and `ended_at`, `companies.business_id` and `businesses.time_zone`, and no other column; its tests check that no statement names a column of `sealing_keys`, `phones`, `threads`, `messages`, `bookings` or `consents`. Beyond the fixtures it is tested with: two people on one handle whose live roles name one business (that business, `personId` null) and two (`severalBusinesses`); a live role at one business beside a company with no business yet (`severalBusinesses`); a live customer role and a company at a business whose live owner role another person holds (`anotherOwner`); a company at a business where one of the handle's people holds the live owner role beside an ended staff role (that business); a role whose `ended_at` is after now (ended); two reasons at once (default 100's order); and a request whose kind is not the stored handle's (`unknown`).
- **The runtime engineer's binding** maps the answer to `TurnParty` field by field: `atBusiness` as it stands; `noBusiness` with the zone UTC (default 101); `unknown` as the port's null; `businessUndecided` as the port's null, so no turn runs, until open gap 57's card, and never a party with a business picked or with business null; `transient` as a thrown error (the run ends `noDirectory`, nothing opened, read or written). The park asks the same directory and maps it the same way. The binding never logs the address, the actor's name or the request, or puts them in a span. It tests a turn's first run with answers shaped as `read-turn-party-test-owner-1` (the JOIN turn under `biz-lees-barbershop`, whose sign-up door's target is Lee's Barbershop, as `build-frontier-s1-01.json` has it), `read-turn-party-first-text` (no business, the zone UTC), `read-turn-party-unknown` and each `businessUndecided` fixture (nothing opened, decided or written; the park refused the same way).

## v1.12 (T-1248): the test-phone page's reads and the actor's copy sources

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the D-017 path audit's line 26 ("F2": the ruling, and a K-05 minor version if ruled K-05's, for the test-phone page's reads and the copy deps' two sources), its ruling R5 and its owners report G10, G11, G16 and G17, carded by DL-498 with QUESTIONS.md CQ-206 and CQ-214. Staging's test-phone page (C-205; K-21 v1 `testPhoneSend` and `testPhoneParts`) answers 503 until three ports have real readers (`actions/src/page-calls/staging/test-phone/types.ts` `TesterAccess.standing`, `TestPhoneRoster.phone` and `PhoneStates.state`; `runtime/src/wiring/test-phone/pages.ts` fails each closed while unbound), and the handle actor's copies (C-053) say of their two sources, `SafetyTimerSource.liveTimers` and `StoppedFlagSource.stoppedFlag`, "No frozen data-access call reads them yet" (`runtime/src/actor/copies/types.ts`). The lead's note binds the card to the five reads: whatever one of them cannot be ruled from the sources is reported open, never guessed (open gap 62). **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.12, the next free number (DL-498, DL-525; v1.11 is T-1249's). `data-access.v1.12.schema.json` holds every v1.11 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.11.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all twelve files as they are, and changes none; it adds five reads and the definitions their exchanges use. So every v1 to v1.11 exchange is a v1.12 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.12). **No schema change:** the reads read K-04 v1's `handles`, `person_handles` and `phones`, K-22 v1's `sessions` through `session_for_token`, K-29 v1's `chat_links`, K-34 v1's `safety_timers` and K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients` as migrated (0004, 0012, 0013, 0016, 0017, 0020), with the grants `rosie_job` holds, and write nothing; no column, grant, index, table or migration is added. **Not here:** db/repo's reads, the seeds, the roster's list and the runtime bindings ("Consumers of v1.12"); sign-in's server side (T-1250, ADR-016; CQ-214 (b)); what "What v1.12 does not name" lists.

### Where each read lives, and who builds and binds it

| Read (its port)                                            | Lives in                                                                                                                    | K-05 call                      | db/repo call: built by                                                                             | Bound in runtime/src/wiring by                                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1. The tester's standing (`TesterAccess.standing`)         | K-22 `sessions`, through `session_for_token`; K-36 `team_page_recipients`                                                   | `readTesterStanding` (item 53) | the database engineer, a new task (the audit's D2)                                                 | the runtime engineer, `RootOptions.testPhone.access` (the audit's P6b, a part of T-1154)                                         |
| 2. The roster (`TestPhoneRoster.phone`)                    | the list: staging's roster config, the seed's own (default 106); the number and the email: K-04 `handles`, `person_handles` | `readRosterPhone` (item 54)    | the list: the database engineer's seed card (the audit's D3); the read: the database engineer (D2) | the runtime engineer, `RootOptions.testPhone.roster` (P6b)                                                                       |
| 3. A phone's state by number (`PhoneStates.state`)         | K-04 `phones`; K-29 `chat_links`                                                                                            | `readPhoneState` (item 55)     | the database engineer (D2)                                                                         | the runtime engineer, `RootOptions.testPhone.phones` (P6b), with the chat link's address from Rosie's number (default 109)       |
| 4. The live safety timers (`SafetyTimerSource.liveTimers`) | K-34 `safety_timers`                                                                                                        | `readLiveTimers` (item 56)     | the database engineer, a new task (the audit's D4)                                                 | the runtime engineer, `setCopyDeps` for staging in `install()` (`runtime/src/wiring/root.ts`; the audit's P6c, a part of T-1154) |
| 5. The stopped flag (`StoppedFlagSource.stoppedFlag`)      | K-04 `phones`                                                                                                               | `readStoppedFlag` (item 57)    | the database engineer (D4)                                                                         | the runtime engineer, `setCopyDeps` (P6c)                                                                                        |

Each read runs on the runtime side as `rosie_job` with no business scope, on the primary, uncached, in one read-only transaction, writes nothing, and can answer `transient`, as every K-05 call does. **Why `rosie_job`:** `phones`, `handles` and `person_handles` are tables only `rosie_job` reads (0012: "the only role that reads phones, handles and sealing keys"); `team_page_recipients` grants `rosie_app` nothing (0017; K-36 rule 2), and the team console's reads run on the runtime side, unscoped (K-21 v1 "The team console"); and the copies serve a handle's actor, which works across businesses by handle (K-04 v1 rule 4). Under ADR-014 the Worker reaches that role through its job handle (`HYPERDRIVE_JOB`). **Why no business scope:** a session, a team row, a handle and a phone have none (K-04 rule 4: scope none), a test phone's chat links may be any business's, and the actor's copy needs every business's timers of its handle, which a read under one business's scope would hide (default 110). **Why the primary, uncached** (arch 02 line 117; arch 10 line 504): a session that ended and a team row removed end the standing at once (RULES 8.7: "Sign-in, texts, day-list links and code views end"); the stopped flag's re-read ends the lost commits' marks noted before it was sent, so a read from a cache or a replica could end a mark on a flag it never saw (T-1206's security note a; DL-427); and the page shows the state the next text from the phone will meet.

### How the v1.12 calls work (as migrated)

53. **`readTesterStanding`** (staging's test tools: `TesterAccess.standing`; K-21 v1 README "Staging only": "a signed-in roster tester or a person on Rosie's team"). The request carries the session token's sha256 (lower-case hex: K-22 `sessions_token_hash_form`) and the time, never the token: the page entry hashes the cookie's token, and the token never leaves the handler (`actions/src/page-calls/staging/test-phone/handlers.ts`, step 3; K-21 "Never in a span or a log"). In one read-only transaction:
    1. **The session:** K-22 v1's `session_for_token(token_sha256, at)`, which `rosie_job` may execute (0020): the session with that hash that has not ended and has not expired at `at`, with its person, else no row, never a hash. No row answers `noSession`, whether the hash is unknown, the session ended (signed out, contact changed, left, handed over) or it expired (default 104).
    2. **The team row:** K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients` of that person with `removed_at` null (`team_page_recipients_one_live_per_person`). One answers `tester`; none answers `notTester` (default 103).

    It answers the standing alone: never the session, the person, a hash or whether a person or a team row exists, so a call that is no tester's learns `notTester` and nothing more, and a call with no live session `noSession` and nothing more (K-21 `notTester`, `signInNeeded`). It writes nothing: a session's `last_seen_at` is sign-in's server side's (T-1250), not this read's. **Who is a tester on staging** (default 103): the person of a live session who holds a live team row. K-21 v1 counts a person on Rosie's team (K-21 README "The team console": "a call counts as Rosie's team when its session's person holds a live K-36 v1 `team_page_recipients` row"; K-21 default 14), and on staging its team rows are "the roster's testers and the test console" (K-21 default 2), reached only at roster test phones or the test console (K-36 README, "The recipient list"; build prompt 9.4: "every alert to Rosie's team go to test phones or the test console, so no real person is alerted"). A roster tester who holds no team row answers `notTester` under v1.12: whether such testers exist, and where they are listed, is open gap 62. **The check tester** (CQ-214 (a)) holds a team row as the test console's member (default 105), so the automated check and the 20-text run do not wait on gap 62.

54. **`readRosterPhone`** (the page's roster: `TestPhoneRoster.phone`). The roster's list, which roster id is which K-04 handle, is staging's config (K-21 default 6: "the test-phone roster ... loaded into staging's config"), kept as the seed's own list (default 106), so the binding finds the roster id's handle id there and asks this read for that handle. The request names the handle alone, never a roster id or a number. In one read-only transaction:
    1. **The phone:** K-04 `handles` by `id`, of kind `phone`; its `address` is the number, in E.164 form (`handles_address_form`). No handle, or one of kind `email`, answers `phone` null, which the binding gives the page as no roster phone by that id (`notTestPhone`), so nothing is signed or posted (default 107).
    2. **The email on file:** the handle's people (`person_handles` by `handle_id`); when it has exactly one person, that person's handles of kind `email` (`person_handles` by `person_id`, to `handles`); exactly one gives its `address`, and anything else null (default 107).

    It answers the number and the email alone, never the person, a name or another handle.

55. **`readPhoneState`** (the page's phone state: `PhoneStates.state`). The request names the roster phone's number (K-04 `phones.number`, the row's key), which the binding has from item 54. In one read-only transaction:
    1. **The phone row:** K-04 `phones` by `number`: `chat_open`, `stopped` and `stop_at`. `stoppedAt` is `stop_at` while `stopped` is true, else null: after a START the row keeps the last STOP's time (DL-193), which is no STOP in force (`PhoneState.stoppedAt`: "When the STOP in force came, or null"); a stopped phone's chat is closed (`phones_stop_closes_chat`), and the answer holds that too. No row answers `phone` null: a number whose first text has not come (v1.7's `openChatOnText` writes the row at receipt: item 46), which the binding gives the page as a chat closed and not stopped (`PhoneState.chatOpen`: "false while the phone has no row yet") (default 108).
    2. **The chat link:** K-29 `chat_links` by `phone_number` (`chat_links_phone_idx`), the newest by `issued_at`, a tie to the lower `id`, whatever its purpose, business or use: its `short_code` and `ready_text` as stored. None gives null, and a number with no phone row has none (`chat_links_phone_fk`) (default 109).

    It answers no number, and of the link nothing but its code and its ready text.

56. **`readLiveTimers`** (the copies' reload: `SafetyTimerSource.liveTimers`; K-34 v1 `safety_timers_worker_live_idx`: "the actor reloads its copies"). The request names the actor's handle. In one read-only transaction it reads K-34 `safety_timers` whose `worker_handle_id` is that handle and whose `state` is `running` or `missed` (a missed check stays open until SAFE: S13-10), at every business, earliest `due_at` first, a tie to the lower `id`, and answers each with the thirteen columns the copy keeps (`runtime/src/actor/copies/types.ts` `SafetyTimerRow`), each named in K-05's form for its column: `id`, `businessId`, `bookingId`, `workerHandleId`, `emergencyContactPersonId`, `emergencyContactHandleId`, `dueAt`, `dueZone`, `address`, `addressForPersonId`, `clockRowId`, `state` and `version`. None is an empty list, an answer. **The address is sealed** (K-34 rule 6: bytea under `address_for_person_id`'s key): the read answers its bytes as stored, in PostgreSQL's hex text form (a backslash, `x`, and two lower-case hex digits a byte, as K-34's `seed-safety-timer.json` writes them), never opened, decoded or put in plain words. It is the one K-05 answer that carries a value sealed under a person's key, because the actor's copy must hold "its due time, address and emergency contact" to act while PostgreSQL is down (arch 11 line 566; K-34's table comment), and the copy keeps it sealed (`copies.ts`: "never opened here") (default 110).

57. **`readStoppedFlag`** (the copies' re-read: `StoppedFlagSource.stoppedFlag`; T-1206). The request names the actor's handle. In one read-only transaction, begun after the call is sent, it reads K-04 `phones` by `handle_id` (`phones_handle_key`): `stopped` and `version`, by which the copy orders its writes. No row answers `notFound` (table `phones`): the port has no answer for none, so the copy reads it as a failed re-read and keeps the stricter reading, a lost commit's mark included (`copies.ts` `readStoppedAgain`: "keeps the copy as it was, the stricter reading included"). It reads the primary, never a replica or Hyperdrive's cache (T-1206's security note a; DL-427: a read the copy takes ends the lost commits' marks noted before it was sent) (default 111).

### What v1.12 does not name

- **Where the roster's testers who hold no team row are kept** (open gap 62): the lead's ruling first, then a later minor version if the ruling names a new source.
- **The chat link's address** (`ChatLink.link`): K-09 v1 `issueChatLink`'s `sms:` form from Rosie's number, a config value (Q-51), which the binding builds (default 109; open gap 63).
- **Sign-in's server side** (T-1250, ADR-016): the sessions item 53 finds are written there, and for the check tester by its seed.
- **The other points carded for "K-05's next minor version":** CQ-211 (`markPart`'s throw on a delivered part with no time), CQ-234 (default 91's and `questionVisit`'s wording on K-30, and whether `readQuestionFacts` reads `booking_addresses.zone`) and CQ-235's note a (two live owner roles at a company's business). DL-498's lead note keeps v1.12 to the card's five reads, so they wait for v1.14 (v1.13 is T-1277's sign-in version, DL-526, which keeps to its card).
- **T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader**: each at the next free number from v1.14.

### Consumers of v1.12

Each moves to v1.12 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.11 consumers keep working.

- **Database engineer**, the producer: C-152, `readTesterStanding`, `readRosterPhone` and `readPhoneState` in db/repo (a new task, the audit's D2), and `readLiveTimers` and `readStoppedFlag` (a new task, the audit's D4), each as `rosie_job` on the runtime side; v1.12 needs no migration. C-202, the seed card (the audit's D3): the roster's list (default 106) and the check tester's rows (default 105), staging only.
- **Release engineer:** seed:staging runs D3's set and hands it the check tester's token hash by name, never shown (the audit's SEED card: `infra/scripts/seed-staging.sh`); and Rosie's number as a staging config value (STATE note 18's task), for the chat link (open gap 63).
- **Runtime engineer:** C-040 and C-205 (`runtime/src/wiring/test-phone/pages.ts`), `RootOptions.testPhone` over items 53 to 55 and the roster's list (the audit's P6b, a part of T-1154), through the job handle of ADR-014; C-046 the actor and C-053 its copies, `setCopyDeps` for staging over items 56 and 57 (P6c, a part of T-1154).
- **Action-layer engineer:** C-205's ports (`TesterAccess`, `TestPhoneRoster`, `PhoneStates`) stay as they are; T-565's handlers already answer as items 53 to 55 need.
- **QA:** the contract test `data-access.v1.12`, a new task. Its fixture part joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, adding v1.12 to `WRITTEN_FOR` after v1.11's task, with a mend for each refused fixture (each twin is named in section "Fixtures"), and its database side joins `tests/contract/data-access.v1.db-side.test.ts` after the database engineer's reads, writing the rows each fixture's note lists before its exchange.

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **The database engineer's reads** run items 53 to 57 as written, each in one read-only transaction as `rosie_job` with no business scope (the scope setting set empty for the transaction, as the seeds set it), writing nothing, and answer each v1.12 fixture's outcome, each answer checked against its v1.12 definition in their tests. `readTesterStanding` calls `session_for_token` and reads `team_page_recipients.person_id` and `removed_at` alone; its tests check that no statement names `token_sha256`, `device_sha256` or `code_hash`, and that no answer, error or log line holds the request's hash, and it is tested beyond the fixtures with a session expiring one second after `at` (live), a person with a removed team row and a live one (`tester`) and a person with two sessions. `readRosterPhone` reads `handles.id`, `kind` and `address` and `person_handles`' two ids alone, and is tested with a phone handle of two people (the number, email null), a person with two email handles (email null) and a phone handle with no person (email null). `readPhoneState` reads `phones.chat_open`, `stopped` and `stop_at` and `chat_links.id`, `short_code`, `ready_text` and `issued_at` alone, and is tested with two links of one phone at two times (the later) and at one time (the lower id), and with links of two businesses. `readLiveTimers` reads item 56's thirteen columns, `state` included, and answers the address in its hex text form, and is tested with timers at two businesses of one handle (both, in due order), a `safe` timer (left out), a `missed` one (listed) and another handle's (left out). `readStoppedFlag` reads `phones.stopped` and `version` alone. None reads a column of `sealing_keys`, `messages`, `consents` or `proof_codes`, and their tests check that no statement names one.
- **The seed card** (the audit's D3): the roster's list as default 106 says, `db/seeds/staging-core.ts` and `db/seeds/skeleton.ts` taking their roster ids and handle ids from it, and the check tester's rows as default 105 says, staging only, with its token's sha256 handed in by name; the skeleton seed's refusal ("load this set ... never on top of" other rows for tp-01) is kept, since the check tester shares no handle with any roster phone.
- **The runtime engineer's bindings:** `access` maps `standing` across unchanged, and `transient` or a thrown error throws (the page entry answers 503); `roster` finds the roster id's handle id in the list (none: null), asks item 54 and answers `{ testPhoneId, number, email }`, or null for `phone` null; `phones` maps `phone` null to a chat closed and not stopped, maps a row field by field, and gives `chatLink` from the answer's code and ready text with the address built from Rosie's number in K-09's form, or null while staging holds no such config value (default 109); `setCopyDeps` gets `timers`, which answers `{ result: "timers", timers }` with each row mapped field by field to K-34's column names (`id` as `id`, `businessId` as `business_id`, and so on, the address as given), and `phones`, which answers `{ result: "phone", stopped, phoneVersion }` and gives `notFound` and `transient` as `{ error }`. None logs a token, a hash, a number, an address (sealed or not), an email or a ready text, or puts one in a span. They test the page's two calls with answers shaped as the v1.12 fixtures (a tester, a non-tester, no session; tp-01's number and no email; no phone row, an open chat, a stopped phone, a START after a STOP, a chat link) and the copies' reload and re-read with `read-live-timers-sam`, `read-live-timers-none`, `read-stopped-flag-alex-stopped`, `read-stopped-flag-alex-started` and `read-stopped-flag-no-phone` (the stricter reading kept).

## v1.13 (T-1277): sign-in by code, and the guards for an outbox row with no turn

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): ADR-016 (sign-in by code on staging; T-1250, DL-519, DL-526) section 7's card V3, carded by DL-526 as T-1277, with its lead notes (DL-526, from T-1250's round 2 security note b; DL-546, from T-322's, T-1274's and T-1275's reviews). K-21 v1's sign-in calls (`signInStart`, `signInCheck`, `signOut`) answer 503 on staging (DL-501) because no K-05 call reads or writes a sign-in (open gap 16), and K-04 v1.1 (T-1273) now lets work with no turn write an outbox row, whose readers ADR-016 section 1 rules on. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.13, the next free number after T-1248's v1.12 and the only K-05 freeze in flight (DL-506). `data-access.v1.13.schema.json` holds every v1.12 definition under its own name, by `$ref` to `data-access.v1.12.schema.json` (which refers on to every earlier version), all thirteen files as they are; it restates nine of them to take what v1.13 adds, each taking only a new optional field or a new error shape, the rule of section "v1.1"'s **Kind**: `claimedRow` (two optional fields) with `claimOutbox.response` and `claimOutbox.exchange` (to refer to it), and `markPart.errors`, `dispatcherGivesUp.errors` and `writeAllSet.errors` (a new error, `noTurn`) with their three exchanges; and it adds six calls. So every v1 to v1.12 exchange is a v1.13 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks each against v1.13). **No schema change:** the calls read and write K-04 v1.1's `outbox_rows` (`turn_id` nullable, `job_id`, `email_purpose`, `not_after`) and K-22 v1.2's `sign_in_attempts` with its three calls, as T-1278 migrated them (0032 to 0034), K-22 v1's `accounts`, `proof_codes`, `sessions` and its four calls (0020), K-04 v1's `handles`, `person_roles` and `event_log`, and K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients`, with the grants each role holds, and write K-08 v1.1's and v1.2's kinds; no column, grant, index, table or migration is added. **Breaking for one reader by ADR-016's own rule** (K-04 v1.1's open point, CQ-248): v1.13 builds the default ADR-016 section 1 rules, `readOutboxRow`'s `notFound` for a row with no turn (item 65), so no v1.3 exchange changes. **Not here:** db/repo's calls (R0 T-1279, R1 T-1280, R2 T-1284); the page calls' server side (B1 T-1282, B2 T-1285); the dispatcher's email branch and limit 8 (E1 T-1283, E2 T-1286); staging's wiring (F T-1288, D T-1289); the contract test (T-1292); the widened tester standing (T-1295).

### The calls v1.13 adds or changes

| Call                                           | Item | Role and scope (ADR-016 section 6; ADR-014)      | `rosie.person_id` (K-22 rule 4)                                                                | Repeating it                                                       | Time budget                                             |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `startSignIn`                                  | 58   | `rosie_job`, the runtime side, no business scope | unset: the call acts for no one                                                                | Safe: the request's key (rule 1) answers the first attempt         | None in the sources; before the page's answer           |
| `sendSignInCode`                               | 59   | `rosie_job`, the runtime side, no business scope | unset                                                                                          | Safe: keyed by the attempt; its first send answered                | None in the sources; after the answer                   |
| `proveSignIn`                                  | 60   | `rosie_app`, the Tool API's, no business scope   | unset until `check_proof_code` answers `right`; then the attempt's person, in that transaction | Keyed by the attempt, the device and `requestId`: the first result | None in the sources; 10 minutes and 5 tries (RULES 6.2) |
| `endSession`                                   | 61   | `rosie_app`, no business scope                   | the person `session_for_token` answers, set before the update                                  | Safe: an ended, expired or unknown session answers `repeated`      | None in the sources                                     |
| `personStanding`                               | 62   | `rosie_job`, the runtime side, read-only         | unset                                                                                          | Safe: a read                                                       | None in the sources; once per `signedIn`                |
| `signInEntries`                                | 63   | `rosie_job`, the runtime side                    | unset                                                                                          | Safe: each entry's id is fixed by rule                             | None in the sources; after the answer                   |
| `claimOutbox`                                  | 64   | as v1.2                                          | unset                                                                                          | as v1.2                                                            | as v1.2                                                 |
| `readOutboxRow`                                | 65   | as v1.3                                          | unset                                                                                          | as v1.3                                                            | as v1.3                                                 |
| `dispatcherGivesUp`, `writeAllSet`, `markPart` | 66   | as v1.1                                          | unset                                                                                          | as v1.1                                                            | as v1.1                                                 |

Each call is one transaction on PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached** (a sign-in is a decision and a write: arch 02 line 117; arch 10 line 504), sets `rosie.business_id` empty for the transaction (a sign-in has no business: K-22 `proof_codes` and `sessions` are scoped none), and can answer `transient`. Under ADR-014 the runtime side's calls run as `rosie_job` through `HYPERDRIVE_JOB`'s login and the Tool API's as `rosie_app` through `HYPERDRIVE_APP`'s, and no transaction spans both: the code and its email row commit together as `rosie_job`, and the check, `device_known` and the session insert together as `rosie_app`, where K-22 rule 5's session lock binds them (ADR-016 section 6). **`rosie.person_id` per call** (K-22 README rule 4: "K-05 states it per call"): only `proveSignIn`, once its code is right, and `endSession`, once `session_for_token` answers, set it, each with `set_config('rosie.person_id', <persons.id>, true)` as `db/repo/scope.ts` sets `rosie.business_id`, so PostgreSQL drops it with the transaction; every other call leaves it unset, which on the Tool API's side reads no person's code or session and on the runtime side binds nothing, the runtime policies on `proof_codes`, `sessions` and `sign_in_attempts` being `using (true)`. **Times:** every time a sign-in call reads or writes is its own transaction's `now()`, the database's clock, never one from the page or the request, which carry none (default 112).

**The order around the page's answers** (ADR-016 sections 1, 2, 4 and 6): `signInStart` runs `startSignIn`, answers the page, and only then, for a start that names a person and is not a repeat, runs `sendSignInCode` and the dispatch of section 2 in the request's `waitUntil`, so the answer waits on no write the two paths do not share. `signInCheck` runs `proveSignIn`, on `signedIn` reads `personStanding`, answers the page, and then runs `signInEntries` for the check's entries. `signOut` runs `endSession`, answers the page, and then runs `signInEntries` for `session.ended`.

### How the v1.13 calls work (K-04 v1.1, K-22 v1.2 and K-08 v1.2 as migrated)

58. **`startSignIn`** (K-21 v1 `signInStart`'s server side; ADR-016 section 4, rules 0 to 5; K-22 v1.2 rule 9). The request carries the attempt's two ids, made by the page call from at least 128 bits of a cryptographically secure generator each (`id`, the server's, and `attemptId`, the page's: DL-546; K-21 default 18), the start's and the device's sha256, the contact as typed and normalized (an email in lower case, a mobile in E.164, or `link`), the contact's keyed hash and the secret's name, and way `email`. In one transaction, by the database's clock:

    0. **The hour's bound**, read before any lock and any account: with `SIGN_IN_STARTS_PER_HOUR` (100) attempts of any contacts started after one hour before now, `paused` until K-22 rule 9's time, `by` `hourBound`, nothing written.
    1. **The contact's lock:** `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('sign_in_attempts:' || contactHash))`, the blocking form, taken before rules 1 to 5 read anything, so two starts of one contact run one after the other and never both pass the pause or the cap (DL-546; T-322's security note b). Then **the repeat**: an attempt with the request's key answers `started`, `repeated`, with its own id, attempt id and expiry, `personId` null, and nothing written: a repeat sends no second code (K-21 v1 schema line 688).
    2. **The contact's pause:** the contact's latest `paused_until` after now gives `paused` until it, `by` `contactPause`, nothing written.
    3. **The day's cap:** with `SIGN_IN_STARTS_PER_DAY` (5) attempts of the contact started after 24 hours before now, `paused` until K-22 rule 9's time (the earliest of five plus 24 hours), `by` `dayCap`, nothing written; so a start past the cap never counts toward it (`refuse-start-sign-in-past-cap-wrote`).
    4. **The account,** in one statement and only for an email or a mobile: `accounts` whose `email` is the address with `email_proven_at` set (way email never reaches an email not yet proven: K-21 v1 schema line 688), or whose `mobile` is the number, with whether its person holds a live owner or admin role (K-04 `person_roles`, `ended_at` null). Exactly one account names its person; none, two or more (`accounts.email` is not unique: ADR-016 open point 14) name none. A `link` start reads no account (K-22 default 15; CQ-244).
    5. **The insert:** the attempt (`id`, `attempt_id`, `request_sha256`, `device_sha256`, `contact_hash`, `hash_key_name`, `way`, `person_id` and `owner_or_admin`, else null and false; `started_at` its default `now()`, `expires_at` 10 minutes on), answered `started` with its ids and expiry, and `personId`, for the server alone, never the page or a span.

    Rules 0 to 3 read no account and rules 4 and 5 run the same statements whether or not one matches, so a pause, a cap and a start answer alike for any typed contact (K-21 v1 schema line 780). An id or a key already taken answers `constraint` and writes nothing.

59. **`sendSignInCode`** (ADR-016 section 1; K-22 v1.2 rule 9's "given its code"). The request carries the attempt's id, the page call's trace, the bundle active at the call (`readActiveBundle`; K-08 default 1), the code's id, its keyed hash and the secret's name, the email row's id, and the row's code-built plan and final parts (`codeRowPlan`, `codeFinalParts`: below); never a person, a handle, an address, the code alone or a time. In one transaction, by the database's clock:
    1. **The contact's lock:** the attempt's `contact_hash` is read and K-22 rule 9's lock is taken on it, the same lock as item 58's, before the attempt is locked and before the account or the day's codes are read: one send per contact at a time, so two sends at once cannot both pass the cap (DL-546; T-322's security note b).
    2. **The attempt,** locked `FOR UPDATE`: none is `notFound` (table `sign_in_attempts`); one that names a code answers that send again, `repeated` (its code, its email row found by the attempt's id as its job id, K-04 v1.1 `outbox_rows_job_idx`, and its end), writing nothing and making no second code; one with no person answers `notSent`, `noPerson`; one whose `expires_at` is before now `notSent`, `attemptExpired`.
    3. **The address,** read for the attempt's person, never taken from the caller (K-22 rule 4): the person's `accounts.email` with `email_proven_at` set and its `handles` row of kind `email`; none answers `notSent`, `noProvenEmail`.
    4. **The day's codes:** `proof_code_limits(<that handle>, 'sign_in', now() - interval '24 hours', now())`: 5 codes or more to the handle in the window, every person and purpose, answer `notSent`, `dayCap`; a sign-in code of it paused answers `notSent`, `codePaused` (ADR-016 section 4, "after the answer").
