    5. **The writes:** the `proof_codes` row (`sign_in`, the attempt's person, no business, that handle, channel `email`, the keyed hash and its secret's name, `sent_at` now, `expires_at` 10 minutes on); the email row with no turn (`job_id` the attempt's id, `turn_id` null, recipient that handle and the person, no business, booking, question or copy, channel `email`, `quick_reply`, `email_purpose` `code`, `not_after` the code's `expires_at`, `versions_read` empty, the request's bundle and trace), inserted `planned` at version 1, then its plan and final parts written and moved to `checked` at version 2, K-07's two moves (README lines 40 to 41 of K-07), so no row ever stands without final parts to stop its recipient's later rows (item 3); the attempt's `proof_code_id`; and the three entries below. The answer names the code, the row, its commit order, its version, the code's end and the three entry ids.

    **The entries** (ADR-016 section 1, "The entries"; K-08 v1, v1.1), written by the call itself from what it wrote, never handed in: each with the attempt's id as its job id, no turn, no bundle and no business, the request's trace, actor Rosie, zone null (UTC), nothing sealed, `why` `{"step": "S11-52"}`:

    | Entry                  | Kind                 | Old → new                                                                                                                                        |
    | ---------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    | `log-<row id>-created` | `outbox_row.created` | none → `state` planned, `channel` email, `send_class` quick_reply                                                                                |
    | `log-<row id>-planned` | `outbox_row.planned` | `state` planned → checked                                                                                                                        |
    | `log-<code id>-sent`   | `proof_code.sent`    | none → `person_id`, `purpose` sign_in, `sent_to_handle_id`, `channel` email, `sent_at`, `expires_at`, under K-22 v1's column names (default 123) |

    None holds the plan, the final parts, the code, its hash or the address (tool-api schema line 4065; K-08 README lines 179, 182). **The K-03 shape** (ADR-016's "Not measured" row): K-03 v1's `rowPlan` and `finalParts` hold a code row as they are, so K-03 needs no minor version: the plan is code-built, as a fixed line's row's is (CQ-177 item 128; `write-plan-alex-safety.json`), with channel email, no copy, formats `text` alone and no lines, question, page type or thread, since K-03 v1 has no slot for a code (K-09 open gap 25); the final parts are one `words` send holding the code's line, "Code NNNNNN: type it on the sign-in page. It works for 10 minutes." (K-12 `send-code.json`'s form; the stand-in for Jay's words, ADR-016 open point 5), with no calendar file, option, link, fixed line or slot (`codeRowPlan`, `codeFinalParts`; T-322's `actions/src/domain/proof-codes/words.ts`; default 116). The email port adds the subject, the automated-assistant line and the sender, as for every email row (K-12 README line 29).

60. **`proveSignIn`** (K-21 v1 `signInCheck`'s server side; ADR-016 section 4, "The check"; K-22 v1.2 rule 9's check). The request carries the page's attempt id, the device's sha256, the check's `requestId`, the typed code's keyed hash (made for every check, with or without an account) and the session a right code would start (its id and its token's sha256, the token 32 bytes from a cryptographically secure generator: card A); never the device cookie, the code, the token or a time. As `rosie_app`, in one transaction, by the database's clock, in K-22 rule 9's order:
    1. **The binding:** `sign_in_attempt(attemptId, deviceSha256, now())` locks the attempt; no row, whether the id is unknown, the device another or an argument null, answers `attemptNotFound` alone, the same for all, telling nothing of any attempt and writing nothing (K-21 README line 117; `refuse-prove-sign-in-other-device-told`).
    2. **The check's key and the look:** the call makes the key, sha256 of the attempt's `id`, the device's sha256 and `requestId`, a line feed between (K-22 rule 9, `last_check_sha256`), from the `id` the binding answered, which the page never learns; `sign_in_attempt_tried(id, key, NULL, now())` writes nothing, and a repeat answers its first result, `repeated`: a `signedIn` one as `alreadySignedIn`, with no token (K-21 README line 115).
    3. **The attempt's own state:** signed in answers `alreadySignedIn`; paused answers `paused` until its `paused_until`; past its `expires_at` answers `expired`; each recorded by `sign_in_attempt_tried` with that label, no try counted, before any code is checked. So a sixth check after five wrong ones answers `paused` from the server, cookie or none (STATE note 74).
    4. **The code:** `check_proof_code(proof_code_id, typedHash, now())`; a null `proof_code_id` (no account, or no code yet) answers `unknown` before any write.
    5. **`right`:** `rosie.person_id` is set to the attempt's person; `device_known(deviceSha256)`; an attempt whose person held a live owner or admin role at its start (`owner_or_admin`), who has had a session, live or ended (rule 5's select policy now shows the person's own), on a device `device_known` does not know, answers `secondProofNeeded`, recorded, and starts no session (K-21 README line 110); else the session is inserted under rule 5's insert policy (its id, the person, the token's sha256, the device's sha256, `proven_via` `code`, the attempt's code, `started_at` and `last_seen_at` now, `expires_at` 30 days on: ADR-016 open point 2), the check recorded `signedIn`, and `sign_in_attempt_signed_in(id, <session id>)`: `signedIn`, with the person, the session, its end and the code. **`used`** (the attempt's code used, no session naming it) answers `secondProofNeeded` again, recorded. **`wrong`, `paused`, `expired` and `unknown`:** one wrong try, `sign_in_attempt_tried(id, key, 'wrong', now())`, and the answer is the attempt's after it: `wrong` with 5 less its `wrong_tries` left, or at the fifth `paused` until its `paused_until`; the call then writes nothing more and commits, so the try counts whatever fails after (K-22 README line 212).

    **The null id's path departs from K-21 README line 116** (DL-526, from T-1250's round 2 security note b). K-21's line maps `check_proof_code`'s `unknown` to `attemptNotFound`; here a null code id's `unknown`, and DL-526's `expired` before a code's `sent_at`, are one wrong try of the attempt, answered from the attempt (`wrong` with its tries left, then `paused`), never `attemptNotFound`, so the five answers of `sign-in-wrong-five.json` are the same with or without an account (ADR-016 section 4; K-22 rule 9's check, step 5). In a sign-in check `attemptNotFound` comes from the binding alone (step 1). B2's builder (T-1285) follows this item, not K-21's line 116, for a sign-in check; that line stays as it is for a K-09 `proveCode` by a code id the server kept.

    The answer is K-21's label with what the server needs and the page never sees: the attempt's id (the job id of the check's entries), the person (for `personStanding`), the session, and the attempt's code (for `signInEntries`), never a hash. A session start with no person is refused (`refuse-prove-sign-in-session-no-person`). A session the database refuses (an id or a token hash taken, 23505) answers `constraint`, and nothing, the code's use included, was written.

61. **`endSession`** (K-21 v1 `signOut`'s server side; ADR-016 section 6). The request carries the session token's sha256 alone (`refuse-end-session-token-not-hash`). As `rosie_app`, in one transaction: `session_for_token(tokenSha256, now())`; no row (unknown, ended or expired) answers `signedOut`, `repeated`, naming nothing and changing nothing; else `rosie.person_id` is set to the person it answers, and `ended_at` = now, `ended_why` `signed_out` and the version raised are written on that session, which rule 5's update policy admits only as that person's own live session (K-22 rule 5), answering the session and the person for the `session.ended` entry. Ending another person's sessions (`left`, `handed_over`, `contact_changed`) is not this call's: the commit writes them on the runtime side (K-22 rule 5).

62. **`personStanding`** (K-21 v1 `signedIn.person`; ADR-016 section 6, "The answer's standing"). The request names the person `proveSignIn` answered. As `rosie_job`, in one read-only transaction that writes nothing: the person's live roles (K-04 `person_roles`, `ended_at` null, as `db/repo/people.ts` reads them), every business, in role id order, in K-21's names (`own_business` as `ownBusiness`), a customer role left out, since customers have no sign-in (FS line 701; `refuse-person-standing-customer-role`); `team`, a live K-36 `team_page_recipients` row (`removed_at` null); `tester`, by the rows v1.12's `readTesterStanding` reads (item 53, default 103), read by person, until T-1295's version widens both (open gap 62). The page call gives `tester` false outside staging (K-21 v1 schema line 853), and on `transient` answers `signedIn` with roles empty and both flags false, failing closed; each console's own call checks standing again (ADR-016 section 6).

63. **`signInEntries`** (ADR-016 section 6; K-08 v1.1 `proof_code.checked`, K-08 v1.2 `session.started` and `session.ended`). The request carries the job id, the page call's trace and what to log, a code or a session by its id, never a value: the call reads every value from the row's plain columns, so no caller can put a code, a hash, a token or an address in an entry (`refuse-sign-in-entries-caller-values`). As `rosie_job`, in one transaction, each entry `insert ... on conflict (id) do nothing`, so a repeat writes nothing and the call can run after the answer, and again after a lost one:

    | Asked                        | Entries the row calls for                                                                   | Id                                              | Old → new (K-22 v1's column names)                                                                        | Actor                                                                                           | `why`                       |
    | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
    | `proof_code.checked`, a code | one per change of the row not yet logged: each wrong try k, 1 to `wrong_tries`, and its use | `log-<code id>-wrong-<k>`; `log-<code id>-used` | `wrong_tries` k − 1 → k, and at k = 5 also `paused_until` null → its time; `used_at` null → its time      | Rosie for a wrong try (who typed it is not known); the code's person for its use, their own act | `{"step": "S11-52"}`        |
    | `session.started`, a session | its start                                                                                   | `log-<session id>-started`                      | none → `person_id`, `proven_via`, `proof_code_id`, `started_at`, `last_seen_at`, `expires_at` (K-08 v1.2) | the session's person                                                                            | `{"step": "S11-52"}`        |
    | `session.ended`, a session   | its end, once it has one                                                                    | `log-<session id>-ended`                        | `ended_at`, `ended_why` null → their values                                                               | the person for `signed_out`; Rosie for any other end                                            | null (K-08 v1.2 default 25) |

    Each entry has the request's job id and trace id, no turn, no bundle and no business (team only: K-08 defaults 16 and 22), zone null (UTC), nothing sealed, and its `at` the time it is written. **The job id** (CQ-258 (a)): the attempt's id for a check's entries, as for its code's (default 117); for a sign-out's `session.ended`, the sign-out's own K-21 `page.requestId`, since no attempt is part of it. **`secondProofNeeded`** (CQ-258 (b)): its code was used, so it writes the code's `proof_code.checked` (`log-<code id>-used`) and no session entry (K-08 v1.2 default 24); a later check answered `secondProofNeeded` again changes nothing and logs nothing new (default 118). Attempts get no entry: their own row is their record (ADR-016 section 6). An asked code or session that does not exist is `notFound`, and nothing is written.

64. **`claimOutbox`** (v1.2 item 30, with K-04 v1.1): each claimed row also carries **`emailPurpose`** and **`notAfter`**, `outbox_rows.email_purpose` and `not_after` as stored, null where the column is null, always written by a v1.13 claim; a `claimedRow` with neither is a v1 to v1.12 answer. As K-04 v1.1's CHECKs hold them, a purpose is only on an email row and a `code` row always has its end (`refuse-claimed-code-row-no-not-after`). Limit 8 withdraws a row at or after its `notAfter`, at every try (ADR-016 section 3; E1, T-1283), and the email port takes the row's K-12 purpose from `emailPurpose` (E2 and F, T-1286 and T-1288). The claimed row still carries no turn id and no job id (ADR-016 section 1's reader table). R0 (T-1279) builds it.

65. **`readOutboxRow`** (v1.3 item 35): **a row with no turn** (`turn_id` null, a job row) **answers `notFound`** (table `outbox_rows`) before any turn is read, as a safety row answers `fixedLineRow` first, so the read never reads its turn and never meets `answersOf`'s `BrokenRecordError` for it (ADR-016 section 1's reader table and open point 12). No v1.3 exchange changes: v1.3 never held such a row. R0 (T-1279) builds it, and D (T-1289), the first card that lets a job row be written outside a test, depends on R0, so no reader as deployed meets a job row unguarded. If the CTO rules K-04 v1.1's change major (CQ-248), this rule moves with it and nothing else does.

66. **`noTurn`** (ADR-016 section 3, "How a code row ends"): a code row is never given up, never `failed` and has no follow-up; it ends `sent`, `delivered`, `droppedStopped` or `withdrawn` at its `not_after`. `dispatcherGivesUp` (item 17: checked once the row is found and locked, before its claim, version or time), `writeAllSet` (item 25: as each listed row is locked, before its state) and `markPart`'s `failed` mark that carries `followUps` (item 15: before any part or row moves) write their rows and entries under the failed row's turn, which a row with no turn has none of, so each answers **`noTurn`** with the row and writes nothing, the failure included (`refuse-give-up-no-turn-wrote`). A `failed` mark with no `followUps` on such a row is marked as for any row, and its retry passes limit 8's `notAfter` first. None of the three is built yet (`db/repo/outbox.ts` names no give-up, "all set" or follow-up), so the refusal goes with the card that builds each.

### What v1.13 does not name

- **Way text and a link's sign-in that sends a code** (CQ-244; ADR-016 section 5): not served. `startSignIn` takes way `email` alone (`refuse-start-sign-in-text-way`), and a link start writes an attempt with no person and sends nothing.
- **The second proof's calls** (a `new_device` code to the owner's email, or the ID re-check: K-21 open gap 2): the attempt keeps the used code and its person; no tester can reach it (ADR-016 section 6).
- **CQ-44's general commit with no turn**: open; any call that carries it writes outbox rows in K-04 v1.1's shape. **A clock firing with no turn that writes an outbox row** stays refused (`refuse-fire-without-turn-writes-outbox`; CQ-28): v1.13 restates no `fireClock` definition.
- **T-1295's widened tester standing**, **T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader**, and **CQ-211's note on `markPart`, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a and CQ-263** (item 56's role check, which T-1301 built): each at the next free number from v1.14 (DL-506), since v1.13 keeps to its card (DL-526).
- **A delete job for attempts** (CQ-246; ADR-016 open point 10).

### Consumers of v1.13

Each moves to v1.13 in its own task that needs it; v1 to v1.12 consumers keep working.

| Role                  | Card                   | Components                                    | What it builds on v1.13                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| database engineer     | R0, T-1279             | C-152                                         | `claimOutbox`'s `emailPurpose` and `notAfter`; `readOutboxRow`'s `notFound` for a row with no turn (items 64, 65)                                                                |
| database engineer     | R1, T-1280             | C-152, C-193                                  | `startSignIn` and `sendSignInCode` in `db/repo/sign-in-send.ts` (items 58, 59)                                                                                                   |
| database engineer     | R2, T-1284             | C-152, C-193                                  | `proveSignIn`, `endSession` and `personStanding` in `db/repo/sign-in-check.ts`, with `db/repo/scope.ts`'s person setter (items 60 to 62); `signInEntries` (item 63: open gap 68) |
| action-layer engineer | B1, T-1282             | C-193, C-095                                  | `signInStart`'s server side: the ids and hashes, `startSignIn`, the answer, then `sendSignInCode` with T-322's code, plan and final parts                                        |
| action-layer engineer | B2, T-1285             | C-193, C-095                                  | `signInCheck` and `signOut`'s server side: `proveSignIn`, `personStanding`, the answer and its token, then `signInEntries`; `endSession`; the null id's path of item 60          |
| runtime engineer      | E1, T-1283; E2, T-1286 | C-124, C-129                                  | limit 8's `notAfter` withdrawal; the email branch, which never asks a give-up, an "all set" or a follow-up of a row with no turn                                                 |
| runtime engineer      | F, T-1288; D, T-1289   | the composition root, for C-129, C-193, C-213 | the claim's two fields mapped through; the calls bound on each side's client, the hashing port, section 2's dispatch after `sendSignInCode`                                      |
| QA                    | T-1292                 | the contract tests, for C-152                 | the v1.13 fixtures against v1.13, each refused one mended into its twin, every earlier fixture against v1.13, and each exchange through db/repo's calls                          |

What the new tasks must hold to:

- **db/repo's calls** run items 58 to 66 as written, each in one transaction in its role, setting `rosie.business_id` empty and `rosie.person_id` only where the table above says, passing `now()` as every `at`, and answer each v1.13 fixture's outcome, each answer checked against its v1.13 definition in their tests. No statement returns `token_sha256`, `device_sha256`, `code_hash`, `contact_hash`, `request_sha256` or `last_check_sha256`, and no answer, error, span or log line holds a request's hash, the contact or the code's words; their tests check both. R1's tests hold the card's own cases (ADR-016 card R1) and two parallel sends of one contact at its cap giving one code; R2's hold card R2's, DL-526's race and a sixth check answered `paused` with and without an account. R0 adds `read-outbox-row-sign-in-code`'s noted rows to `db/repo/turn-face-reads.int.test.ts` (its `NOTED_ROWS`), so the read meets the job row itself: until then that test lists the fixture (T-1277, under DL-455's grant) and reads a row it has not written, which answers the same `notFound`.
- **The page calls** never send the device cookie, the code, the token or a time to K-05, make the attempt's two ids from at least 128 random bits each, map item 60's answers to K-21's labels with the token added only to `signedIn`, give the page no `personId`, `id` or code id, and follow item 60 for the null id's `unknown`.
- **The dispatcher and the wiring** map `emailPurpose` and `notAfter` field by field, read a missing field as a v1 to v1.12 claim (no purpose, no end), and never ask `dispatcherGivesUp`, `writeAllSet` or a follow-up of a row with no turn; a `noTurn` answer is a bug to log by the row's id alone.

## Fixtures (`fixtures/`, listed in `fixtures/index.json`)

Each accepted fixture is one exchange (`<call>.exchange`): `call`, `given`, `request` and `outcome` (the response or one of the errors). `given` says what the exchange assumes, so QA's contract test can build it: `k04Seeds` (K-04's seed fixtures, each loaded with its own `after` chain), `after` (exchanges of this folder that run first, in order, each with its own `given`), `now` (the fake clock) and a `note`. `commitSeq` values assume a fresh database, so the test compares their order, not their numbers. Ids the call makes are fixed by rule: a reservation's saved-time clock row is `<reservation id>/saved-time`.

Accepted (38): `commit-first`, `commit-repeat-same-turn`, `commit-version-changed`, `commit-reservation-lost`, `commit-opens-question` (the tour's A7-03), `write-plan`, `write-plan-repeat`, `write-plan-owner`, `unplanned-after-crash`, `claim-in-order`, `mark-sent`, `mark-delivered`, `reserve-10-min`, `reserve-ai-door-15-min`, `reserve-overlap-refused`, `reserve-next-in-line`, `reserve-third-refused`, `release-expired-promotes`, `extend-once`, `extend-ai-door-refused`, `arm-clock`, `cancel-clock`, `due-for-handle`, `due-for-sweep`, `fire-once`, `fire-repeat`, the evening chain behind `claim-held-lets-later-pass` (`commit-evening-notice`, `write-plan-evening-notice`, `claim-evening-notice`, `mark-held-for-text-hours`, `commit-sam-quick-reply`, `write-plan-sam-quick-reply`, `claim-held-lets-later-pass`), `publish-bundle-copy`, `set-active-bundle`, `active-bundle`, `bundle-value-on-miss`, `park-turn`. That is 02-contracts K-05's 22 v1 fixtures, with `due-for-handle` and `due-for-sweep` split by caller as listed, plus the repeats, the bundle writes NQ-PB-5 adds and the chains they need. Refused (7, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each against its `<call>.request` and refused for one reason): an outbox row with a plan at the commit (arch 01 lines 39 to 41), a state write to `reservations` (only confirm, release and reserve change them), a fourth try (arch 11 line 576), a document's saved time over 24 hours (A4-21), the AI door asking for more than its 15 minutes (MC C3), a turnless firing that writes an outbox row (K-04 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null), a row held with no time (arch 06 line 313).

**v1.1** (listed with `"version": "v1.1"`; every v1 fixture also passes v1.1). Accepted (38): the effect chain on Alex's calendar write, `commit-writes-effect` (commit-first's turn in its v1.1 form, run instead of it), `claim-effects`, `effect-result`, and instead of it `effect-result-network-error`, `claim-effects-lapsed`, `effect-try-2`, `effect-try-3`, `effect-try-4`, `effect-fifth-try-outage`, then `service-back` or instead `commit-lets-go-outage-effect`; `provider-event-recorded`, `provider-event-duplicate`; `reconciliation-refund`; the claim chain on Alex's confirmation after v1's `claim-in-order`, `renew-claim-first-send`, `mark-failed-retry-after`, `claim-lapsed-reclaimed`, then `renew-claim-not-yet`, `renew-claim-lost`, `dispatcher-gives-up-too-soon`, `dispatcher-gives-up` (the next way, an email row), then `write-all-set` (texts work again), and `write-all-set-not-failed` after `claim-lapsed-reclaimed`; the waiting chain on Dee W.'s booking notice (K-07's `outbox-waiting-chat-*` rows), `commit-adds-dee-booking`, `write-plan-dee`, `claim-dee`, `mark-waiting-chat`, then `mark-row-wrong-state`, `commit-supersedes-waiting` or instead `commit-withdraws-waiting`, and after `commit-supersedes-waiting` the answer that never goes, `write-plan-dee-answer`, `claim-dee-answer`, `renew-claim-dee-answer`, `dispatcher-gives-up-owner-number` (Dee gave no email, so Sam gets her number); the safety chain on Alex's emergency line (K-07's `outbox-safety-retry`), `commit-alex-safety`, `write-plan-alex-safety`, `claim-alex-safety`, `mark-safety-failed-other-way`. Each effect result (`effect-result*`, `effect-try-2` to `4`, `effect-fifth-try-outage`) carries its `effect.result` entry. 02-contracts K-05's three v1.1 names are `claim-effects`, `effect-result` and `provider-event-duplicate`; the rest are added so every v1.1 call, outcome and K-07 move appears. Refused (10, in `fixtures/invalid/`, each for one reason, each accepted once that one fault is fixed: checked by a throwaway probe, 2026-11-07, with 26 more wrong cases refused, and for round 2 with 13 more around the follow-ups, the "all set" and the result's entries): a waiting row with a held time, the reconciliation writing a charge, a calendar effect with no visit, an ok try with an error code, a Retry-After on a failure with no next try, effects of an SMS port (DL-012), a Linq event as a provider event, an effect result with no event-log entry, a safety line's other-way row with no entry, an "all set" by email. Dee W. is the master's sample cast (S12-26), as K-07 names her; her mobile is +15555550107 and her rows are written by the chain, not seeded.

**v1.2** (listed with `"version": "v1.2"`; every v1 and v1.1 fixture also passes v1.2). `given.partSeeds` names other parts' seeds as `<part>/<file>` (K-29's here). Accepted (37): the gateway's key, `handle-key-existing` (Alex), `handle-key-person-no-key` (Sam, his key made now), `handle-key-first-sight` (a number never seen: handle, person and key written), `handle-key-first-sight-repeat` (nothing written twice), `handle-key-erased` (Ben T.'s number after his key was erased: a fresh person with no name and a new key); the sign-out, `end-session-sam` and `end-session-repeat`; the standing, `person-standing-sam` (four owner roles, no team row), `person-standing-alex` (customer roles only: none) and `person-standing-check-tester` (v1.12's check tester, its rows written by the database side as v1.12's `read-tester-standing-check-tester` lists them: team and tester); the entries, `sign-in-entries-signed-in` (the code's use and the session's start), `sign-in-entries-wrong-try` (the code's first wrong try) and `sign-in-entries-sign-out` (the session's end); and the readers of a row with no turn, `claim-sam-sign-in-code` (its purpose and `notAfter`), `read-outbox-row-sign-in-code` (`notFound`; the code row written by the database side as its note lists, since the read's own tests run only `commitTurn` steps), `give-up-sign-in-code-no-turn`, `write-all-set-no-turn` and `mark-sign-in-code-failed-no-turn` (`noTurn`). The codes in the final parts (482913 is S11-52's example), every hash (stand-ins in hash form, the contact hashes K-22's own), every id, trace and request id and the time of Nov 2 are examples; the check tester's rows and address are v1.12's.

Names and values come from fixture set v1's demo shops (barber, yoga studio, movers and, from v1.6, therapist: `fixtures/v1/test-kit/shops/`) and the master's sample cast (Sam, Lee's Barbershop, Alex M., Riley, Avery, Maya, Tomás; MASTER-FLOW-A A7-03), the same rows K-04's seeds hold, never a real person (rule 9). Phone numbers are in the 555-01xx range; the email domain is example.com. Agent tokens, Linq ids, trace ids, event ids and job ids are examples. The bundle id is K-01's `bundle-v0-skeleton.json`, and its manifest value is K-04's seed stand-in, whose bytes and sha256 match.

## Producers and consumers (02-contracts K-05)

- **Producer:** database engineer: C-152 data-access calls, with C-135 double-booking guard.
- **Consumers** (each at the newest frozen minor version): action-layer engineer: C-087 one commit per turn, C-075 frontier builder, C-100 reservations, C-134 saved-time rules, C-109 effects runner and C-112 reconciliation job (at least v1.1); runtime engineer: C-050 bundle pin, C-054 clock rows, alarm and sweep, C-055 clock arming, C-124 outbox dispatcher, C-125 limit 8, C-126 channel fallback, C-127 two-part send, C-180 crash in the middle of a turn; release engineer: C-020 deploy script (`publishBundleCopy`, `setActiveBundle`: BP 5.4; NQ-PB-5); frontend engineer: C-203 fake clock "Fire now" (through K-21 and the Tool API).
- **v1.1's consumers** (each moves to v1.1 in its own task that needs it; v1 consumers keep working): action-layer engineer: C-087 (effects and waiting rows in the commit), C-109 effects runner (`claimEffects`, `recordEffectResult`, `serviceBack`), C-112 reconciliation job (`reconciliationRefund`); runtime engineer: C-044 gateway (`recordProviderEvent`), C-054 sweep (a firing's effects), C-124 outbox dispatcher (the claim, `renewClaim`, `markPart`, `dispatcherGivesUp`), C-125 limit 8 (`markRow` `waitingChat`), C-126 channel fallback (`dispatcherGivesUp` `followUps`: the next way and the owner's number; `markPart` failed `followUps`: a safety line's other ways; `writeAllSet`: the "all set"), C-174 to C-176 outage paths (`serviceBack`). **Producer:** database engineer, C-152, with C-151 migrating K-07's schema addition.
- **v1.2's consumers** (each moves to v1.2 in its own task that needs it; v1 and v1.1 consumers keep working): database engineer: C-152, T-219 (`readTurnState`), T-220 (`handleTargets`, `sendState`, `handleSealingKey`), T-667 (the setup-part facts `readTurnState` reads, over K-22 to K-35, in M4), and the tasks that build `claimOutbox`'s port, `markPart` by key, `renewClaim`'s phone read and the claim entries; action-layer engineer: C-074 Tool API turn face and C-075 frontier (`readTurnState`: T-315), C-109 effects runner (the claim entries); runtime engineer: C-044 gateway (`handleSealingKey`: T-553), C-051 guard (`handleTargets`: T-556), C-046 actor and C-050 bundle pin with the frontier from the Tool API (T-557), C-124 outbox dispatcher (the port, `renewClaim`'s `recipient`, `markPart` delivered by key: T-562) and C-125 limit 8 (`sendState`, `renewClaim`'s `recipient`: T-562); QA: T-128 (the fixture contract test `data-access.v1.2`) and T-478 (its database side). **Producer:** database engineer, C-152 (T-219, T-220); no migration is needed. What three consumers must hold to (the security reviewer's notes on round 1): **T-562** treats a phone row's renewal with no `recipient` (optional in the schema, so a v1.1 answer still passes) as unreadable and sends nothing, never as not stopped; **T-553 and the actor** (C-046, which opens the sealed texts at its turn: `runtime/src/actor/inbox.ts`) take the data key as the non-exportable key object item 29 hands over, never as bytes (T-243's key-store port gives 32 raw bytes today); **T-219 and T-478** test the `businessId` null path of `readTurnState`: a read with no business returns no row that has one, a booking, a role, a pause, an open question or a consent row of a business included, since that path runs on the runtime side, where no row-level security leaves them out. And two from the security reviewer's notes on round 2: **T-553** makes `newHandleId` and `newPersonId` new on every call, never built from the address (the fixtures' `handle-gw-*` and `person-gw-*` ids are examples), since an id built from the address would meet 23505 on every retry once a number's keys were erased a second time, a 503 for good again; **T-478** seeds a private booking, a deceased booking and a paused row, which no v1.2 fixture sets, so `sendState`'s record flags are tested (its pauses are the recipient's only: default 42).
- **v1.3's consumers** (T-1137): the action-layer engineer's C-087 one commit per turn, and C-074 the Tool API turn face, which binds the three reads in turn order (T-1141); built by the database engineer's C-152 (T-1138), with no migration. Section "v1.3", "Consumers of v1.3", lists every consumer port with what each must hold to; that list joins this section with QA's v1.3 contract test task, which adds its sides to the contract test's side list.

Section "v1.4", "Consumers of v1.4", lists every consumer port with what each must hold to; that list joins this section with QA's v1.4 contract test task, which adds its sides to the contract test's side list.
