it reads K-04 v1's `handles`, `person_handles`, `person_roles` and `businesses` and K-23 v1's `companies`, K-06 v1's `handle` and K-08 v1's `handleRef`, and in its fixtures K-04's `seed-business-head.json`, `seed-person-roles.json` and `seed-sealing-key.json` and K-23's `seed-company-two-kinds.json` for its rows' values (frozen), with T-1148's `db/seeds/skeleton.ts` for test owner 1's. **v1.12 frozen before it:** K-05 v1.11 (T-1249, merged), K-04 v1 (T-013), K-22 v1 (T-031), K-29 v1 (T-038), K-34 v1 (T-043) and K-36 v1 (T-045); it reads K-04 v1's `handles`, `person_handles` and `phones`, K-22 v1's `sessions` and `session_for_token`, K-29 v1's `chat_links`, K-34 v1's `safety_timers` and K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients`, K-08 v1's `handleRef`, and in its fixtures K-04's `seed-person-roles.json`, `seed-phone-chat-open.json` and `seed-business-head.json`, K-22's `seed-session.json` and `seed-owner-id-upload.json`, K-29's `seed-chat-link.json` and K-34's `seed-safety-timer.json` (frozen), with T-1148's `db/seeds/skeleton.ts` and T-202's `db/seeds/staging-core.ts` for the roster's handles. **v1.13 frozen before it:** K-05 v1.12 (T-1248, merged), K-04 v1.1 (T-1273, merged), K-22 v1.2 (T-1274, merged), K-08 v1.2 (T-1275, merged), with their migration (T-1278, merged: 0032 to 0034); it reads K-04 v1.1's `outbox_rows` and `outbox_parts`, K-22 v1's `accounts`, `proof_codes`, `sessions` and its four calls and K-22 v1.2's `sign_in_attempts` and its three calls, K-04 v1's `handles` and `person_roles`, K-36 v1's `team_page_recipients`, K-03 v1's `rowPlan` and `finalParts`, K-12 v1's `purpose`, and K-08 v1.1's and v1.2's kinds, and in its fixtures K-22's `seed-sign-in-attempt.json`, K-04's `seed-bundle-registry.json` and `seed-person-roles.json`, and v1.12's check tester (frozen). **Next:** the lead's ruling on open gap 62 and T-1295's widened tester standing; T-1155's arrival read, T-1177's outcome per answered label and T-1187's row reader with the business's live-owner state, each its own minor version at the next free number from v1.14 (DL-391, DL-450, DL-466, DL-492, DL-499, DL-498, DL-506); and the points carded for "K-05's next minor version" (CQ-211, CQ-234, CQ-235's note a), at v1.14. **Next after that:** further minor versions as open gap 16 lists (w2-m3 new question 7's default: K-05's minor version freezes are T-072 to T-079, each with a contract test).

## Defaults used (T-014)

1. **Q-12:** one extension of 10 more minutes, offered before the end; a plan check up to 1 hour, a document up to 24 hours; never for the AI door, whose 15 minutes are fixed (MC C3). **MC C3** also gives the owner's OK up to 30 minutes (A5-08, B5-06): the `ownerOk` door.
2. **Q-15:** a quick reply is a reply in the same turn (the `quick_reply` send class, K-04).
3. **Q-21:** local PostgreSQL with PostGIS meanwhile; the calls need nothing beyond K-04's `btree_gist`.
4. **CQ-05:** a saved-time clock row is due in the business's time zone (no visit zone is in core).
5. **NQ-PB-5:** item 7 carries `publishBundleCopy` and `setActiveBundle`; the second is called only by the deploy script's step 3.
6. **NQ-RT-3:** the turn row is written once, as committed or parked. **NQ-RT-4:** a clock's firing is a turn whose only input is the clock row (`fireClock`).
7. **Scope:** the commit's writes run per write under their own business; reserve, release and extend under the reservation's business; the rest on the runtime side (K-04 rule 4's proposal, `rosie.business_id`).
8. **An open question is `waiting` until its first delivery and `open` after** (`armClock` moves it); K-04 names both statuses, and no source says when one becomes the other.
9. **A clock turn's one event id is the clock row's id**, since K-04 needs at least one (`turns_has_events`) and the firing has no text.
10. **The promoted next in line holds for 10 minutes**, the standard saved time (S12-01), since K-04 keeps no door on a waiting row.

## Defaults used (T-071, v1.1)

11. **`renewClaim` is the "part attempt"** that renews an outbox claim by 60 s (K-07 default 2), sets `first_try_at` once and clears `next_try_not_before` (K-07 names the moments, not the call); a lapsed claim nobody has taken again is still its holder's.
12. **An effect claim lapses 60 s after it is taken, and after a network error 60 s after the runner's planned next try** (`nextTryAt`), so a wait between tries longer than 60 s (the tries spread over 10 minutes: arch 10 line 542) does not hand the row to another runner mid-wait. K-07 default 2 gives 60 s; no source gives the rest.
13. **The call decides the outage** on the run's 5th try (K-07 default 3: "5 tries in all"), so the runner only reports each try.
14. **`markPart` raises the row's version whenever it changes a part**, the row's state changing or not (the database engineer's note on K-07).
15. **The commit finds the waiting rows it ends** (every one to the mobile, or about the booking or question) and writes one `outbox_row.state_changed` entry per row with the id `<row id>/superseded` or `<row id>/withdrawn` (K-08 v1's kind; K-07 defaults 9 and 10).
16. **A booking let go or cancelled while its effect waits in outage lets the effect go** (`letGo`): K-07 names the move for a time let go with no yes (arch 11 line 582; S12-17); `commit-lets-go-outage-effect` uses it for a cancellation, so no calendar event is written for a visit that will not happen.
17. **`recordProviderEvent` runs unscoped on the runtime side** (the database engineer's note on K-07; arch 02 line 109: the gateway stores it before it knows more).
18. **The reconciliation refund's repeat key is its effect id**, so the daily job run again over overlapping 48 hours refunds a charge once (arch 10 line 550).
19. **`attempts` at a re-claim counts the claims that handed the key out** (K-05 v1 item 3), so a try written by a failure and first handed out by a re-claim shows 2 (`claim-lapsed-reclaimed`) where K-07's `outbox-retry-after-reclaimed` shows 1 for the same moment; K-07's rule does not check `attempts`. **No timing and no repeat counts from `attempts`:** the 15-minute give-up counts from the claim's `firstTryAt` (item 17; K-07 rule 5), and a failed safety line's 10 s steps from its part tries (each try's `failed_at`, and the next try's key: K-07 rule 3). Whether a send is a repeat is decided by its idempotency key (`<row>/<part>/<try>`, sent to the provider with every send under it), never by `attempts`, so a re-claim that shows 2 sends nothing twice.
20. **A `sending` row with no claim row** (claimed by v1 code before the migration) counts as lapsed, so v1.1 can claim it.
21. **A follow-up row (the next way, the owner's number, a safety line's other way, the "all set") is written under the failed row's turn id, bundle and trace id**, since K-04 needs a turn for every outbox row and writes turns only for texts; the event-log entries carry the same turn id (K-04 `event_log_turn_or_job`), and the link to the failed row is the entry's `why` (K-07 open gap 3).
22. **The give-up and its follow-ups are one transaction, and so are a safety line's first failure and its other ways**, so a row never ends failed, nor a safety failure is recorded, without the rows that follow it (arch 02 line 125 gives the order, not the transaction).
23. **The "all set" row's id is `<first failed row id>/all-set`**, so one failure gets one "all set" (S12-10: "a short 'all set'"), and it is a text row (it goes "once texts work").
24. **The effects runner's entries are actor `job`**, carrying the effect's turn id or job id; K-08 v1 names `job` for "a sweep or reconciliation" and the runner is one.

## Defaults used (T-072, v1.2)

25. **Each call states its scope** (the card: "each primary and uncached, under the business scope"; arch 10 line 546: "Row-level security per business on every Tool API call"), read with K-04 v1 rule 4: `readTurnState`, a Tool API read, runs under the request's business, one business per call, and with no business on the runtime side returning only rows with no business (K-05 v1's scope for writes with no business); `handleTargets` and `sendState` run on the runtime side, which rule 4 names for the guard and the dispatcher (and K-29 rule 4 for their consent reads), and name each row's business; `handleSealingKey` touches only tables scoped none.
26. **A handle's sealing key is one of its people's keys** (NQ-RT-1's default on K-04 v1, whose `sealing_keys` is per person): the oldest held key among them (`created_at`, a tie to the lower person id), else a key made for the first of them with none, else, at first sight or when every one of them has had their key erased, a person with no name made on the handle and linked by `person_handles` ("linked to the person once known"), with a new key (open gap 18's proposal, moved from the erasure job into the call: round 2). No schema change: T-220's "a schema change the key call turns out to need" is not needed.
27. **The key leaves `handleSealingKey` as a key object that cannot be exported** (T-182's form), never as bytes in an exchange (rule 7). T-243's key-store port gives 32 raw bytes, so T-553 binds the port to a key object (the gateway's seal takes a WebCrypto key) rather than to bytes.
28. **A parallel first sight is one key:** the call takes a transaction-level advisory lock keyed on the handle's address before it reads or writes (T-220: 50 parallel calls, one key).
29. **A handle's bookings are those its people booked or are served by** (K-04's two indexes "The guard lists a handle's bookings"), in every stage; a payer or a worker who is neither is not listed (open gap 23).
30. **A fact names its home**, and the call reads only a table and column a frozen table list gives, of a table whose rows carry a version, never a column of `sealing_keys`, never a secret's hash (K-22's `proof_codes.code_hash`, `sessions.token_sha256` and `sessions.device_sha256`) and never a column of a table only Rosie's team reads (K-34's `harm_reports`, K-36's `team_pages` and `team_page_recipients`, K-37's `parked_turns`, `dead_letters`, `gap_cases`, `review_items` and `live_turn_labels`) (`unknownFact` otherwise; the schema refuses each in a request and in an answer: item 26), and answers `notFound` for a home row the scope hides or that does not exist, as for a requested booking: a read fails closed rather than give the frontier half its facts.
31. **The audience for text hours** (RULES 4.3: the owner and team from 8am): `owner` for a live owner role at the row's business; `team` for admin, staff or own_business (FS T01: "staff, office manager = admin, or someone with their own business"); `customer` otherwise, a row with no business or no person included, the later 9am being the stricter. The highest wins for a person with two roles.
32. **A booking is closed when done or cancelled** (no message about its future can still be right: arch 06 line 313, "withdrawn if its booking or question has closed"; K-07 `withdrawn`); a question when answered, defaulted or withdrawn (K-04 `closed_at`). `sendState` also returns the stage and the status, so C-125 may narrow it.
33. **The staging override is the release flag `staging.text-hours-override` at scope `phone:<number>`, enabled** (K-04's `seed-flag` writes it so; QUESTIONS.md M3-M3b.4: "a staging-only release-flag row per test phone"); false for an email handle.
34. **`visitZone` is null until K-30 freezes:** no frozen part stores a visit's zone, so CQ-05 falls to the business's zone (K-05 v1 default 4); `zone` is the one to read hours in, null for a row with no business.
35. **The texts consent row that covers a contact** is its newest texts row whose business is the row's or none, by `at`, a tie to the lower id (K-29: "the newest row (by at, then the lower id) decides").
36. **Consent rows are read without their exact words**, which stay in the row (audience never; the hash names them: arch 09 line 492), as K-08 default 19 keeps the ready text in its row.
37. **The claim entry's id is `<effect id>/claimed/<version after the claim>`, its `why` null (`old_values.state` names the state it was claimed from; K-08 v1's `why` allows nothing a claim could name), its zone the effect's business's** (K-08 v1.1 fixes the kind and the values, not these).
38. **`renewClaim` reports the phone and refuses nothing for a stop** (T-259's limit 8 decides what a stop allows; CQ-55 (2), a stop reply's marker, stays open), reading it `FOR SHARE` so a STOP being committed is waited for.
39. **`markPart` delivered finds a part by its key only when no part holds the receipt's message id and the part under the key holds none** (K-11 README line 50: the receipt is matched "by partKey when it holds no message id for that part").
40. **Lists with no time column are in id order** (open questions, persons, roles, pauses, bookings in the guard's list), ids compared, never parsed (CQ-09).
41. **An open question's send time and poll id are its newest send's to its own handle** (K-02 v1 `openQuestion` names one of each, for a question "sent to this handle"; K-04 keeps them on the parts of the outbox rows that ask it, not on the question): only rows to the question's handle count, so an email copy to another handle never moves them, and only parts that have gone, `sent` or `delivered`, never `pending` or `failed` (K-07 v1); `sentAt` is the latest time such a part went, its `sent_at`, or its `delivered_at` when no acceptance was recorded (a 4001 or 4006 that delivered anyway), and `pollId` the `linq_message_id` of the latest such part of kind `poll`, so a question asked again (`ask_count` 2 or more) gives its newest send and poll, a poll delivered after a 4001 or 4006 gives the id its receipt stored (item 31), and a poll that failed, even after Linq accepted it, gives none (its options go again as plain text); each null until a part has gone, and `pollId` null for a question that went out as text only or by email.
42. **The record flags `sendState` returns are the row's** (arch 03 line 175 names the flags, not the read): its booking's `private` and `deceased`, with the person served to check `private` against, and the open pauses at the row's business on its recipient person or the recipient's phone, since a pause names the business whose owner clears it (K-04 `pauses.business_id`, not null); a row with no business has none. Address pauses stay unread (open gap 17), and who the person served added (A3-50) is in no table v1.2 reads (open gap 19).

## Defaults used (T-1137, v1.3)

43. **The commit-time hold read is per business** (K-34 v1 rule 4: a hold's `business_id` is "the business whose routine actions wait", and no business scope sees another business's row; arch 10 line 546; the card: "read inside the call's business scope"). It returns the call's business's open holds, or with no business those with none. Holds of other businesses on the handle, and those with no business once there is a business, reach the commit through the runtime's unscoped read (`LeasedTurn.holds`: CQ-65, DL-119), which T-251 joins with this read; no business's hold is shown to another (RULES 4.5).
44. **A text is unread at commit time when no safety row the scope shows names it** (CQ-65's DL-127: "no safety row at commit time"), among the ids the caller names: the turn's texts whose outcome does not ride in this commit's core actions, and the runtime's unread ids (DL-133). A row the scope does not show counts as missing, so the read fails closed and the commit holds every part but a protective one.
45. **Open means not released** (`released_at` null: K-34 `safety_holds_open_handle_idx`). A released at-once hold whose plug-in stop words still block booking (A3-26) is not open and is not returned (open gap 31).
46. **Holds come in `held_at` order, a tie to the lower id; a reader's open questions in id order** (CQ-09: ids compared, never parsed).
47. **The words a row answers are handed in by the runtime caller, never opened by a Tool API path** (item 35's reasons): the read gives the texts' event ids and the handle they came from, and their safety results; the actor, which opens the turn's texts under the handle's key (C-046), hands their words in through the turn face.
48. **A row's step and line come from the question it asks** (`open_questions.step`, "The step that asks"; line 0, CQ-151 (a)'s default 2) until K-04 stores them (open gap 29). A row that asks no question answers `line` null, which compose input and finalize refuse, never guess.
49. **A row of send class safety is a fixed-line row** (arch 07 line 347: "Safety lines stay fixed text"; K-09 v1 `recordSafetyOutcome` writes the checked line as a safety row): the read answers `fixedLineRow` and gives no compose or finalize view of it (STATE note 17).
50. **"Rosie has texted this handle about this business before" is K-04's opener mark naming another row** (`openers_sent`: "Whether Rosie's opener for a business has gone to a handle"; arch 07 line 361). The row the mark names is the first text itself, so for it the answer is false and it carries the opener whenever it is finalized again. With no business, it is an earlier row to the handle with no business that carries the opener in its final parts (arch 07 line 361: "S1-02's opener before there is a business"), since `openers_sent` cannot hold a mark with no business.
51. **The questions still open with a reader** are the live questions sent to its handle at the row's business, other than the row's own, that have gone (item 26's rule, default 41), each with the words part of the newest asking row's final parts (arch 07 line 389: "its words as sent").
52. **What K-04 stores is returned as stored, and the rules over it stay their owners'**: the stored name, the stored language, every live role at the business and the phone's flags; which role the message is for (T-308), how Rosie addresses the reader and the message's language (C-090; arch 06), and what this phone allows (C-128) are the callers' rules, not this read's.
53. **The flow a row's step is read in comes from the row and its bundle, never a guess** (`ComposeRow.flow`, in which `readMasterLine` reads a shared step's line first, and the flow graph finalize's checks, fallback text and links are read from; T-1137 round 2, DL-326): an A or B step's own flow (K-01 v1 `stepId`); a shared step's, the booking's for a row about a booking (`slotSource.booking.flow`: K-04 `bookings.flow`), else the first of A, then B, that the manifest of the row's bundle (`row.bundleId`) lists and whose flow graph holds the step; a step no listed graph holds is refused (`stepMissing`, as `readMasterLine` refuses it). The caller reads it through its own bundle port (`ComposeBundles`, the frontier's: KV, or PostgreSQL's copy on a miss), the reads `readMasterLine` makes for the line, so this read returns no flow. **Why:** K-04 v1 stores no flow but a booking's (`turns` and `outbox_rows` hold none), and K-09's `composeInput` and `finalizeMessage` requests carry none, so the turn's own flow (K-02 `context.flow`) cannot reach the two calls (the action-layer engineer's FAIL on round 1). The shared steps are the same nodes in both flows (SPEC-master section 1: sections 1, 2 and 11 to 13 "are SHARED: the same nodes in both flows"; K-01 v1 README default 4), so any graph that holds a shared step gives the line, links and checks the turn's own flow gives, and a shared step that happens in one flow only (SPEC-master section 4, `only`) is found in whichever graph holds it. A before B is the frontier's own default for a turn whose open questions name no A or B step (`actions/src/frontier/assemble/frame.ts` `contextFlowOf`: the first flow the pinned bundle lists, A before B). The database is not where it is found: a bundle's step graph is read from KV, and from K-04's copy (`bundle_values`, each value up to 25 MB) only on a miss (arch 10 line 536), and K-04's seed copy of the skeleton's bundle holds a stand-in for its manifest alone (`seed-bundle-registry.json`), so no exchange here could show a flow the database found.
54. **A row whose question has no clock row is refused by finalize's port, never given `FinalizeRow.question` null**, which means a row that asks no question. The passed-on path withdraws the row's question with its clock row in one `cancelClock` (K-05 v1 item 5; arch 02 line 123: "the question is withdrawn, so no clock arms (RULES 5.2)"), the one withdrawal a finalize call makes (`actions/src/finalize/rewrite/passed-on.ts` `withdrawRequest`), so a null would leave the question open after its message was passed on, with no clock to default it: it would fail open. The commit makes every asked question's clock row with it (C-087, `actions/src/commit/build.ts`: `clock-<question id>`; `commit-s1-01-sam`), so a question with none is a broken record, not a row to finalize, and it is refused as a null `line` is (default 48).

## Defaults used (T-1160, v1.4)

55. **The safety service's reads are the call's own** (`ports.ts`: "on the call's own transaction under its business scope"; arch 10 line 546; K-04 v1 rule 4): each request states the call's business (the Tool API caller's `businessId`), never one the service picks, and runs under it: as `rosie_app` where every table it reads is business-scoped and granted to that role (items 37 to 41 and 43: 0012, 0016; STATE note 5), as `rosie_job` after `requireBusinessScope()` where it needs a table only the runtime side holds (item 42: STATE note 22, as v1.3's items 35 and 36), and with no business on the runtime side, rows with no business only (default 25).
56. **With a business, a read first finds the business in its scope** and answers `notFound` (table `businesses`) otherwise, so null or false always means none in this business, never no such business (as item 34 never answers "nothing open" under a business that is not there).
57. **A row of another business, or one with no business under a business scope, is not seen**, and answers as no row does: null or false. A text whose record another scope holds is then recorded again, and its commit is refused by `safety_outcomes_event_key` (`constraint`): the call fails closed rather than read another business's record (open gap 42).
58. **The business's owner is its one live owner role** (S2-02: "One account owner"; S11-73, S11-100): none is null, and more than one is `moreThanOneOwner`, never one of them picked (open gap 41).
59. **The handle the owner writes from is their first phone handle, else their first email handle, in handle id order** (FS row B12: the owner's one mobile is their sign-in; arch 02 line 125: a safety line goes by text first, every other way at its first failure; T-307's `Reach` order; CQ-09); none is null (open gap 43).
60. **Each value is the port's own shape** (`ports.ts` `StoredOutcome`, `StoredHold`, `OwnerContact`, a zone, and a boolean for `outboxRowWritten`), so the wiring hands it to the service unchanged: a stored outcome is `safety_outcomes` without `sealed_words`, and without `tries` and `danger_word_lists`, which the port does not read; a stored hold is every column of `safety_holds`, none of which is sealed.
61. **An error is never read as none:** `notFound`, `moreThanOneOwner` and `transient` fail the call closed (T-1141 maps each to a thrown error), never null or false, which the service would take as no record, no hold, no row or no owner.

## Defaults used (T-1184, v1.5)

62. **The lane of the step line the row carries, with no role to tell it by** (item 44; `rowLine`: "the line's index among the step's lines from Rosie to this reader"): the step's `say` messages from Rosie (`from` `san`: MC R1), in say order. When they all go to one lane, that lane, with the row's `line.index` counted among them; when they go to two or more lanes, null, since the index counts the lines to the row's reader, which only a role names, so the line is never told by a guess; null too when the step has no line from Rosie at that index. S1-02 has one line from Rosie, to the owner.
63. **The role the sign-up reading gives `ComposeRow.role` is the reader's own name** (owner, staff or customer, each a K-04 role: v1.2 `role`), which `readerOf` reads as itself, so the composer's `audience.role` and limit 7's reader are the line's audience. It is never written, cached or returned by a K-05 call as a role the person holds, so the gate, the frontier, the guard and the dispatcher, which read stored roles, never see it.
64. **A row with no recipient person is refused** (`noReader`, `noRole`), in section 1 or not: roles are a person's (K-04 `person_roles.person_id`), the card's security line reads "a person with no role reads only their own sign-up rows", and the sign-up door's writer is always a person, made at first sight (v1.2 item 29; T-1148's seed), so a bare handle, such as a listing's phone (S1-35's notice, out of scope under C-253), is never read as a line's audience.
65. **A step's section is its own** (K-01 `step.section`), read in the flow item 35's flow row gives (default 53); a shared step is the same node in both flows (SPEC-master section 1: sections 1, 2 and 11 to 13 are shared), so the flow never changes the answer for a section 1 step, and an A or B step's own flow is the only one that holds it.

## Defaults used (T-1196, v1.6)

66. **The switch is the business's one value, in either flow** (item 45; DL-391): true when any of its kinds of work lists `sensitive` in `pf_switches_a` or `pf_switches_b`. A row about no booking names no kind of work, and the flow its step is read in is the caller's (default 53), so the read cannot narrow it to one kind or one flow, and the stricter is the safe side of RULES 4.6.
67. **Nothing stored is never off** (DL-326 note b; DL-391): a business with no kinds of work answers `null`, which the caller reads as on, and an answer with no `businessSensitive` (one of v1.3 to v1.5) reads as `null`; only `false` is off.
68. **A row with no business reads no kinds of work** and answers `null`. A `company_kinds` row with no business is a sign-up's kind of work not yet tied to a business (K-23 README rule 5: "whose business is null is seen by no business scope; sign-up's turns read and write it on the runtime side"), never the row's; on the runtime side, where no scope keeps it out, a read that matched a null business would answer from another signer's kinds of work.
69. **The answer is the switch's one value alone**, as defaults 47 and 60 keep each answer to what its port reads: never a kind of work, its facts or trade name, another switch or a value per flow. A booking's own switch is not read here (open gap 30), so the caller keeps a row about a booking on.

## Defaults used (T-1211, v1.7)

70. **A number's text opens its chat at receipt**, by the gateway, after the scrub and `handleSealingKey` and before the text reaches the actor (item 46). CQ-194's default (one runtime-side call per text from a phone handle, repeat-safe by the event id) is kept, with its "before the turn starts (after the safety read)" moved to receipt, so a first text's checked safety line and its STOP meet an open chat and a phone row (item 46, reasons 1 and 2).
71. **Which texts open a chat:** a text or photo from a phone handle through Linq (K-06 v1 kinds text and photo, `fromRosie` false), the person's own message to Rosie's number (RULES 4.1b: "set when it first texts Rosie's number"). A reaction, a vote or an edit happens in a chat that is already open; a receipt is Rosie's own part's; an email, or an Apple ID email by iMessage, opens no mobile's chat (RULES 4.1b; S13-45).
72. **A later text changes nothing on an open chat**, its way included. K-04's `chat_open_at` is "when the mobile first texted Rosie's number", and the stored way is the opening text's: a reply's channel is the service of the text it answers (K-09 v1.5 default 132; arch 07 line 365), and a row that answers no text takes Linq's capability check (arch 07 line 365), so a later text's service is read where it is needed (open gap 49), and no version moves under a turn that read the row.
73. **`chat_open_at` is the gateway's receipt of the text** (K-06 v1 `receivedAt`, the clock the actor orders texts by: K-06 default 2), not the sender's `sentAt`, which is the phone's own clock.
74. **A closed chat that opens keeps a `chat_open_at` it already holds**, the first text's (K-04), as `db/repo/phones.ts` `startPhone` keeps it. In practice none holds one: only STOP closes an open chat, and STOP stops it.
75. **The entry's actor is the provider**, Linq, whose event reports the text, with no person: several people can write from one handle (arch 01 line 55), and a person actor needs one person (K-08 v1: a person's own act is the person's). Its job id is the text's event id (K-04 `event_log.job_id`, for a webhook), since no turn is written yet.
76. **A repeat is known by the text's own entry**, found under the row's lock: a text that opened the chat answers `repeated`, whatever the row's state now (a STOP since, or a START), and writes nothing; any other text answers by the row's state. The call moves a version only for a number's first text (an insert, or a closed chat that opens), which runs before that text's turn, so no turn's compare-and-set meets a version it moved.
77. **A refusal never stops a text.** The gateway answers 503 for `transient` alone: nothing is stored, and Linq's retry is a repeat. On `notFound` or `notAPhone` it stores the text, which goes on to its safety read and its turn, and rows to the number wait for the chat (limit 8 fails closed), with the refusal as its receive span's error code.

## Defaults used (T-1102, v1.8)

78. **`fromContact` is read from K-29 v1's own row and the turn that wrote it, never stored apart** (item 47; T-1099's handoff: "K-29 channel text and the writing turn's handle equals the consent's handle, or recorded on return from that number's own text"): true only for a yes (`revoked` false) given by text (`channel` text) that a turn of the row's own handle wrote (`turn_id`, then K-04 `turns.handle_id` equal to `handle_id`), or a START with no turn (`turn_id` and `business_id` null: only a STOP or a START has no business, and K-09 v1 records START only from that number's own text). "That number" is the handle the row covers, whoever on it sent the yes, as RULES 7.5 counts it ("from that number"), and as a STOP from a shared number stops it for every person behind it (K-09 v1 `recordStopOrStart`).
79. **A yes no turn wrote, other than a START, is never the person's own:** the row does not show where it came from, so it reads false (fails closed), even where it came from the contact, as K-29's `seed-chat-link.json` writes Ben's ready-text yes with no turn. No frozen writer records a contact's own yes outside a turn but START on return (open gap 53).
80. **An email contact's row reads false:** the field names a number's own text ("from the number that stopped": T-1099's handoff), START never comes by email (K-09 v1 `recordStopOrStart`), and only a phone carries the stopped flag, so limit 8 never reads it for an email contact.
81. **`bookingId` is `consents.booking_id` as stored, on every row:** the booking a yes was given for, and null on START's and STOP's rows for every business (K-29's writers give them none) and on a yes given for the business rather than one booking.
82. **A row that withdraws reads `fromContact` false:** it is no yes (the card: "true only when the yes is the person's own"), so a reader that skips `revoked` can never take a STOP for a yes that lifts it, and the schema refuses true on it (`refuse-send-state-stop-read-as-own-yes`).
83. **Whether a fresh yes's booking is new is not read** (T-1099's security note a): no frozen part stores when a booking was made (K-04 `bookings` and K-30 hold no creation time), so the answer names the booking, not its age (open gap 52). It fails closed at send all the same: limit 8 lifts a stop only for rows about the booking named, and a lifted row waits for the chat, which K-04 keeps closed on a stopped phone (`phones_stop_closes_chat`), until START (QUESTIONS.md CQ-74's default).
84. **`stopped` is the phone's flag, never derived from a consent row** (STATE note 10; T-1099): `phone.stopped` and `phone.stopAt` are K-04 `phones` as stored; `fromContact` and `bookingId` describe the newest covering consent row alone, and no reader derives a stop, or its end, from it.

## Defaults used (T-1226, v1.9)

85. **A repeat reads "the row's first delivery" from the times the record holds** (item 48): `firstOfRow` is true when no other part try of the row holds a `delivered_at` earlier than the repeated part try's own, a tie counting as first. K-04 v1 stores each part try's delivery time (`outbox_parts.delivered_at`), not the order the receipts were recorded in, so this is the one reading the record gives with no migration. It names the delivery that said `firstDelivery` whenever receipts were recorded in the order of their own times; where one was recorded after a later-timed one, it names the earlier-timed delivery (open gap 54). A tie hands each repeat on, and `armClock` keeps the first arming (item 5).
86. **`deliveredAt` is the stored `delivered_at`, never the repeat's own `at`**: the arming a repeat hands on counts from the delivery the record holds (arch 02 line 127; T-255's due rule; `armClock`'s `deliveredAt` and K-04 `clock_rows.armed_at`), so it is the arming the crash lost, and no second report of one part (on SMS, Linq's `message.sent` or the SMS provider's receipt: arch 02 line 127) moves a clock's time.
87. **Only a repeated delivery receipt says what it repeats**: `repeatedDelivery` is null on a `delivered` mark that records a delivery and on every `attempt`, `sent` and `failed` mark, a repeat or not, since only a delivery receipt arms a clock (arch 02 line 127; on SMS the receipt is marked `delivered`: T-255's handoff).
88. **`firstDelivery` keeps v1's meaning** (item 4): only the mark that records the row's first delivery says it; a repeat records nothing and says false, as every v1 to v1.8 answer does, and the schema refuses a repeat that says it (`refuse-mark-repeat-says-first-delivery`). Kept true on a repeat, it would be a changed meaning, a breaking change (build prompt 6.4).
89. **A missing or null field hands nothing on**: an answer without `repeatedDelivery` is one of v1 to v1.8, read as not known, and the dispatcher hands such a repeat to no one, as today (CQ-201's default until T-1227 and T-1228 merge); a v1.9 answer to a repeated delivery is never null (`refuse-mark-delivered-repeat-null`).

## Defaults used (T-1240, v1.10)

90. **A question's visit is its own booking's, and it starts at the earliest of the booking's held or booked times** (item 49; the port's "K-04 reservations.during's lower bound for the booking": `runtime/src/clocks/types.ts`). The booking is `open_questions.booking_id`'s, read at the question's business, its `flow` as stored. Its start is the earliest `lower(during)` among its reservations in state `held` or `booked`, the rows that hold a time (K-04's EXCLUDE constraints: "held or booked time"). A `released` row no longer holds the visit's time, and a `waiting` row waits for a time the booking does not hold yet (A4-18), so neither is read. The earliest, because a booking's held or booked rows can start at different times (a worker and a room for one visit, or the parts of a longer visit), and no clock may run past the first of them (S13-35: "No clock runs past the visit"; "Every deadline ends before the visit"; arch 02 line 127). A booking with no such row has no time, so `visit` is null, as the port has it ("a booking with no time"), and step 9's no-visit default applies.
91. **No visit address and no address zone are read or answered.** K-30 v1 holds a visit's address and its zone, and is frozen, but its tables are not migrated (0001 to 0031 hold none), so v1.10 reads neither, as v1.2's default 34 reads `visitZone` null until K-30. The binding gives the port's `visit.addressZone` and `visit.address` null, and the arming then takes the business's zone for a flow B visit (CQ-05, CQ-08), as join 13's default already does with no geo port. An address is a person's private detail (rule 7), so a later minor version that reads K-30 adds the zone first, as a new field, and an address only if the geo port's caller needs one (the security reviewer's to judge then). `refuse-question-facts-visit-address` holds the line.
92. **The never-delivered row, of several, is the waiting or armed one with the lowest id, else the one with the lowest id** (item 49), the rule by which `armClock` finds a question's own row (db/repo `clocks.ts` default 1), with ids compared, never parsed (CQ-09). A fired or cancelled row is given too, when it is the question's only one: the arming's cancel then answers `alreadyFired` or a repeated cancel, and its report says so, rather than "none". No commit writes the row yet (STATE note 37; `runtime/src/clocks/arming.ts`: "the action layer's join").
93. **The actor name is the gateway's rule over the stored handle** (item 50). K-05 answers the handle's kind and address as K-04 stores them (default 52: what K-04 stores is returned as stored, and the rules over it stay their owners'), and the consumer builds the name with `runtime/src/gateway/seal.ts` `actorNameOf` (`<kind>:<address>`), the one rule the gateway names every handle's actor by, never a second copy of it in SQL or elsewhere. So the arming, the sweep and Fire now reach the Durable Object the gateway writes the handle's texts to, and a change to the rule changes one function.
94. **A chat id names a handle only through a chat thread of a phone handle** (item 51): `threads` by `linq_chat_id`, of kind `chat`, and its handle, of kind `phone` (K-06 v1: "A Linq event comes from a phone"). Anything else is unknown, both fields null, which the adapter answers `receiptHandleUnknown` and the gateway 503 (CQ-61's default), so a receipt is never placed on a guessed handle.
95. **A miss is an answer for the two handle reads, and an error for the question's facts**, as each port reads it: `readHandle` and `readChatHandle` answer null for a handle or chat K-04 does not hold (`actorNameOf` null; `chatHandle` null), and only `transient` is their error; `readQuestionFacts` answers `notFound` (table `open_questions`), which the arming's port takes as `NotFoundError` (`questionNotFound`).

## Defaults used (T-1249, v1.11)

96. **The handle is the request's address and kind, and a miss is `unknown`** (item 52, step 1). The turn's handle is its texts' K-06 event handle, written as K-04's `handles.address` (K-06 v1 `handle`: "so the actor finds the handle row by it"; `handles_address_key`); a row of another kind cannot hold the same address (`handles_address_form`), so a kind that differs is a request no handle answers. `unknown` is the port's null ("not known, so no turn runs"): `handleSealingKey` writes a handle at its first sight, before its text reaches the actor (item 29), so a miss is a record that lost the handle, and no turn runs on a handle the record does not hold.
97. **The person is the handle's one person** (step 2). A handle can be several people (arch 01 line 55; S13-27 rule 28: "A phone reaches someone; it doesn't prove who they are. One phone can be several people"), and no row says which of them wrote, so with none or several `personId` is null (K-09 v1 `caller.personId`: "The person the caller acts for, when known"). The business is still read from all of them, as the guard lists all of a handle's people (arch 02 line 115; item 27), since whoever holds a phone can act as the people on it (RULES 12.1).
98. **The business is the one scope the rows name** (item 52's rule). A live role names its business: live is `ended_at` null (K-04 `person_roles.ended_at`: "null while it holds"; the live role of `person_roles_one_live_role` and of items 27 and 42); a role whose `ended_at` is set, whatever its time, is not live, as that index reads it, so a person's access to a business ends no later than the row says (RULES 8.7: "their last day is set"). A company names its business, or no business while it has none (K-23 README rule 5: sign-up's turns run on the runtime side). One scope gives the party; none, or only "no business yet", gives no business (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`: "Null only where no business exists yet"; RULES 6.3); more than one gives `severalBusinesses`. **Why a company counts:** K-09's journeys 1 and 2 write `companies` at S1-04 (`owner_person_id`) and `businesses` at S1-09, and no `person_roles` row for the owner who signs up (their one `person_roles` write is S2-02's staff: gap 58), so the company is the frozen record of whose business it is (K-23: "one company is one business, a second company a second business on the same login"; FS row B10). It is the reading that keeps test owner 1's turn under `biz-lees-barbershop` from T-1148's seed as it stands (ADR-013 default 130; K-09 v1.5 default 130), and a newly signed-up owner's turns under their business. **Why several fail closed:** the master reads which business a text is about from the message and asks when two fit (S13-27 rule 29; arch 02 line 115: "Several targets fit: the model picks by content (S13-27 rule 29); a tie gets one lettered 'which?' (S13-12)"; S11-15: "Pause which business?"), which no row holds before the turn reads the text, while every Tool API call of the turn runs under one business (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`; arch 10 line 546). One picked would act under a business the text may not be about and hide the others' rows from the frontier; no business would run an owner's or a customer's turn as a stranger's. Neither is a rule the sources give (QUESTIONS.md CQ-220's lead note), so the turn fails closed (gap 56).
99. **A company's business is theirs only while the rows agree** (`anotherOwner`, `roleEnded`). The account owner is the business's live owner role (S2-02: "One account owner"; RULES 6.8; S13-27 rule 34: "Only the account owner, or an admin the owner lets, decides fees, refunds, the team and settings"; default 58), and a hand-over ends the old owner's access (S11-100: "Sam's access ends at the handover time. Records stay with the business"; RULES 6.9). A company naming one of the handle's people while a person who does not write from the handle holds the business's live owner role, or while one of them held a role there that ended and none of them holds its live owner role, is a record a hand-over or a removal left behind (gap 59): the read does not settle it, by taking the company's owner or by dropping the business (default 58: "never one of them picked"), but answers why. A company whose business's live owner role one of the handle's people holds agrees with that role and is not checked further; what each person may do there is the gate's (limit 1: arch 06 line 299), never this read's.
100.  **The reasons in order:** `anotherOwner`, then `roleEnded`, then `severalBusinesses`; the first that applies is named. A company the rows contradict comes before the count, since it names a record Rosie's team must see; each fails closed alike (default 102), so the order changes the label and nothing else.
101.  **The zone** (step 6): the business's `time_zone` (S13-03: "a visit at the business uses the business's time zone"; CQ-05; item 43's `readBusinessZone` reads the same column). With no business no row stores a zone (K-04 keeps no person's zone), so the answer gives null, as `sendState` gives `zone` null for a row with no business (default 34) and `readQuestionFacts` gives `businessZone` null (item 49); the consumer gives the port, whose `TurnParty.zone` is a string, UTC, as K-08 v1 reads a null zone (`eventLogRow.zone`: "null means UTC") and T-255's due rule takes UTC with neither zone (v1.10 `readQuestionFacts.response`, `businessZone`: "with neither the due rule takes UTC"). A turn with no business has no visit and no business whose zone S13-03 would use, and any time it would change something by is read back first (RULES 4.2: "A change of money, time or person is read back first"). `refuse-turn-party-no-business-zone-utc` keeps UTC out of the answer, so no consumer takes it for a stored zone (gap 61).
102.  **No business is picked for an undecided party** (`businessUndecided`). It is an error, never an answer (as `moreThanOneOwner` is: default 61, "An error is never read as none"), so no decided path of the turn runs under a business picked from several, or under no business in place of one the rows cannot give: the binding gives the turn no party. The error carries the handle's id and the person, as a party would, and nothing else, so the runtime's own card can let a STOP, START or HELP through, which needs no business (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`: "or for a call that covers every business (a STOP: RULES 7.2)"; arch 02 line 115: code alone acts on a text that is only START, HELP or an opt-out word), and park every other run for Rosie's team with no business (arch 11 line 576), with no further K-05 change (gap 57).

## Defaults used (T-1248, v1.12)

103. **A tester is a live session's person who holds a live K-36 team row** (item 53). K-21 v1 lets staging's tools be used by "a signed-in roster tester or a person on Rosie's team" (K-21 README "Staging only"), Rosie's team is a live `team_page_recipients` row (K-21 README "The team console"; K-21 default 14), and on staging those rows are "the roster's testers and the test console" (K-21 default 2), reached only at roster test phones or the test console (K-36 README; QUESTIONS.md M3-M3b.5; build prompt 9.4). Live is `removed_at` null, as `team_page_recipients_one_live_per_person` reads it, whatever `added_at` says. No other row marks a tester (K-21 open gap 12: "K-04 and K-22 have no team role"), so v1.12 reads none, and a roster tester who holds no team row answers `notTester`, the closed side, until open gap 62 is ruled.
104. **No live session is `noSession`, whatever the reason** (item 53, step 1). `session_for_token` gives no row for an unknown hash, a session ended for any reason and one whose `expires_at` is not after `at`, and K-21 answers each `signInNeeded`; the read tells them apart for no one. The session is found before any team row is read, so nothing is said of a person or a team row for a hash no live session holds; and no standing names the session or the person (`refuse-tester-standing-names-person`). `at` is the page entry's own clock (`handlers.ts`: `utcSeconds(now)`), taken as given.
105. **The check tester (QUESTIONS.md CQ-214 (a)), as these reads need it.** Staging only, written by the seed card (the audit's D3) and run by seed:staging (the audit's SEED card): a K-04 `persons` row of its own, with no name, role, company or phone handle, so it is no roster phone's person and changes no turn's party (item 52) or guard's targets (item 27); one K-04 email handle of its own at example.com, linked by `person_handles`, which its team row names as its one reach (`team_page_recipients_reachable`), never a roster phone's or a real person's handle, so a staging page reaches it only as an email, which staging sends through the test-phone line's email double (T-1186; `runtime/src/wiring/test-phone/pages.ts`); one live K-36 `team_page_recipients` row, as the test console's member (K-21 default 2; K-21's `team-console-pages.json`: "on staging the test console"), so item 53 answers it `tester`; one K-22 `proof_codes` row of purpose `sign_in`, used, which its session names (`sessions.proof_code_id` is required and unique), its `code_hash` the hash of no code; and one live K-22 `sessions` row, `proven_via` `code`, with no device, whose `token_sha256` is the sha256 of a token made at random on the build Mac and kept there by name, never committed, printed or logged; its length is the seed card's (K-21 open gap 3), and a lost or lapsed token is replaced by a new one and the seed run again, as CQ-218 treats staging's own keys. Making the token and keeping it by name is a live secret step, the user's to run (DL-497, DL-514, DL-518).
106. **The roster's list is the seed's own** (item 54; the audit's R5: "the roster read from the same source the seed writes"). One module beside the seeds, importable by the Worker (constants only, with no file, network or environment access), lists each roster id with the K-04 handle id the seeds write for it (tp-01: `handle-sam`, T-1148's `TEST_OWNER_1`; tp-02 to tp-05: `handle-<roster id>`, T-202's `db/seeds/staging-core.ts`) and its fiction-range number for the seeds' fallback; `staging-core.ts` and `skeleton.ts` take their ids from it, and the binding reads only its roster ids and handle ids. It holds no real number and no email: the number is read from the database the seed wrote (item 54), whether the seed took `STAGING_TEST_PHONES` or the fake handles (NQ-DB-2), so the page and the seed cannot drift. K-21's `test-roster.json` is a contract fixture of examples and is never read at run time (open gap 66).
107. **A roster phone is a phone handle, and its email on file is its one person's one email** (item 54). A handle of another kind, or none, is no phone the Linq double can sign as, so the read answers none (`notTestPhone`). The email is read only when the phone handle has exactly one person (S13-27 rule 28: "One phone can be several people"; default 97's rule), and given only when that person has exactly one email handle; anything else gives null, so the page lists no email (`handlers.ts`: "No email on file for the phone's person: no email is read"), never another person's. An email handle is where an email on file lives for every role: an owner's account email names one (K-22 `accounts_email_fk`), and S1-10 writes it (K-09 v1 saveSetupStep).
108. **A phone's state is its row as stored, and no row is an answer** (item 55, step 1). `stoppedAt` is `stop_at` only while `stopped` is true (DL-193 keeps `stop_at` after a START); no row is `phone` null, which the binding maps, as v1.11's null zone is mapped by its consumer (default 101).
109. **The chat link is the phone's newest, its code and ready text as stored** (item 55, step 2). A test phone may hold links of several businesses and purposes (K-29: a booking's, a staff invite's); the page shows one ("The phone's chat link, or null"), the one a tester's latest step issued, used or not, since the page shows what the phone got. Its address (`ChatLink.link`) is not stored: K-09 v1 `issueChatLink` gives `sms:<Rosie's number>&body=<the ready text>`, Rosie's number a config value (K-21 default 5; Q-51; `chat-link-after-contact-add.json`), so the binding builds it; while staging holds no such value (STATE note 18), the binding answers `chatLink` null (open gap 63).
110. **Live timers: running or missed, every business's, sealed as stored** (item 56). None is an empty list. The db/repo read sets no business scope, since one business's scope would hide another's timers and the copy would drop a live one. The address is the bytes K-34 stores, in PostgreSQL's hex text form, which the copy keeps as a string and never opens (`copies.ts`: "the job's address exactly as K-34 seals it"); nothing on the way decodes, converts or logs it.
111. **The stopped flag is the phone row's, fresh, and no row is an error** (item 57). The port has no answer for none (`StoppedFlagAnswer`), so, as v1.10's question facts read a miss as an error where their port does (default 95), `notFound` (table `phones`) makes a failed re-read and the copy keeps the stricter reading; it is never answered as not stopped. The read is on the primary in a transaction of its own, begun after the call is sent, so it is fresh for every mark noted before it (DL-427).

## Defaults used (T-1277, v1.13)

ADR-016 sections 1, 3, 4 and 6 name the calls, their roles, their transactions and the rules; these are the points they leave to the contract, each ruled here and named for the reviews.

112. **Every time is the database's** (DL-546: "every call that takes a time takes the server's", T-322's security note a; T-1274's database engineer note a, DL-538). A sign-in call's request carries no time; each time it reads or writes is its own transaction's `now()`: the attempt's `started_at` and `expires_at` (K-22 default 16), the code's `sent_at` and `expires_at` and so the row's `not_after`, the session's `started_at`, `last_seen_at` and `expires_at`, a sign-out's `ended_at`, and the `at` given to `sign_in_attempt`, `sign_in_attempt_tried`, `check_proof_code`, `session_for_token` and `proof_code_limits`. One clock: an attempt expires no later than its code (its `started_at` comes before its code's `sent_at`), every count of K-22 rule 9 reads it, and rule 2's optional 25-minute narrowing stays sound, which a pause set by a Worker's skewed clock could break (T-1280's lead note). A fixture's `given.now` is that clock.
113. **The send reads its person and its address itself.** `sendSignInCode` takes the person from the attempt row and the email handle from that person's account (`accounts.email` with `email_proven_at` set, and its `handles` row), never from its caller, as K-22 rule 4 keeps them on the server, so no handle id a caller sends can direct a code anywhere; `startSignIn`'s `personId` tells the server only that a code follows. ADR-016 rule 5's "it names the person and the email handle to send to" is met by the attempt's person and the account read in the send. So the send has two answers the record does not name: `attemptExpired` (no code for an attempt past its 10 minutes, which no check could use) and `noProvenEmail` (the account's email unproven or gone since the start).
114. **One send per contact at a time** (DL-546; T-322's security note b): `sendSignInCode` takes K-22 rule 9's lock, on the attempt's own contact hash, before it reads the attempt, the account or `proof_code_limits`, so two sends of one contact run one after the other and the second reads the first's code; `startSignIn` takes the same lock before rules 1 to 5. The lock key is K-22 default 23's.
115. **A repeat start sends nothing:** its answer's `personId` is null (`startSignIn.started`'s rule), so a lost answer sent again never sends a second code (K-21 v1 schema line 688); a send that never ran for the first is open gap 71.
116. **The code row's K-03 shape is K-03 v1's** (ADR-016's "Not measured" row; card V3: "or reports a NEED for a K-03 minor version"): `codeRowPlan` and `codeFinalParts`, as T-322's `words.ts` builds them, are `rowPlan` and `finalParts` narrowed (no lines, one `words` send, no slot, fixed line, option or link), so no K-03 version is needed.
117. **The job id of a check's entries is the attempt's id** (CQ-258 (a); K-08 v1.2's open gap): ADR-016 section 4 makes the attempt's id the job id of its code's row and entries, so every entry of one sign-in, its code's three, its check's and its session's start, carries one job id, by which Rosie's team reads the whole sign-in; a check's request id is kept nowhere else (only its key's sha256 is), and the trace id ties each entry to its page call. A sign-out is no part of an attempt, so its `session.ended` takes the sign-out's own request id, as K-08 (CQ-44) gives a page's rows "its request or job id".
118. **`secondProofNeeded` writes its code's entry** (CQ-258 (b)): `check_proof_code` used the code (`used_at` set), which is a write K-08's `proof_code.checked` records ("each check of a code typed or texted back"), so `log-<code id>-used` is written; no session started, so no session entry (K-08 v1.2 default 24). A later check of that attempt answered `secondProofNeeded` again changes nothing and logs nothing new.
119. **The entries follow the rows.** `signInEntries` builds every entry from the row it names, one per change of the row not yet logged, each with an id fixed by rule and written `on conflict do nothing`: so no caller's value reaches an entry (no code, hash or token: the security reviewer's "nothing of a code in an entry"), the call can run after the page's answer without changing its time with or without an account, and an entry lost with an isolate is written by the next call for that row. The check's `proof_code.checked` entries are one per wrong try and one for the code's use, as K-08 v1.1 counts a check, while the attempt row records its own tries and gets no entry (ADR-016 section 6).
120. **The call makes the check's key.** `proveSignIn` computes `last_check_sha256` from the `id` the binding answers, so only the device, the attempt id and `requestId` travel, and a page, which never learns `id`, cannot make a key (K-22 default 14); the typed code's keyed hash is the page call's, through the root's hashing port, made for every check.
121. **The standing's roles in K-21's names, customer left out:** `ownBusiness` for K-04's `own_business`, and no `customer` role, which K-21 v1's `SignedInRole` has none of (FS line 701: customers have no sign-in), in role id order, as v1.3 orders a recipient's roles.
122. **`tester` by v1.12's rule until T-1295** (ADR-016 section 5: "the rows T-1248 rules on"): a live team row, read by person (v1.12 default 103), the same row `team` reads, so on staging a team member is a tester and a human tester holding none is not, failing closed, until T-1295's version widens both reads (open gap 62).
123. **The entries' values carry K-22 v1's column names** as frozen (`used_at`, `wrong_tries`, `paused_until`, `person_id`, `purpose` `sign_in`, `sent_to_handle_id`, `channel`, `sent_at`, `expires_at`), as K-08 v1.2 default 27 names a session's; K-08 v1.1 default 17 used the K-09 draft's names (`proven_at`, `route`, `contact_handle_id`) "until K-22 freezes its columns", which it has (open gap 69). `why` is `{"step": "S11-52"}`, the portal sign-in, for the code's and the check's entries, and null for a sign-out (K-08 v1.2 default 25).
124. **A session lasts 30 days** (ADR-016 open point 2; K-21's fixtures): `expires_at` is `started_at` plus 30 days.
125. **Ids are the caller's:** the attempt's two ids from at least 128 random bits each (DL-546; T-1274's security note a; K-21 default 18), never made from the contact, the device or the time; the code's, the row's and the session's ids made by the page call (CQ-09), the session's token 32 bytes (card A). The fixtures' ids are readable stand-ins, as K-04 v1.1 default 16's are.
126. **Way `email` alone:** ADR-016's lane serves no other (section 5; CQ-244), and the page call answers way `text` 503 before any K-05 call, so this version's request admits none; a later minor version adds `text` as an enum value when its card serves it.
127. **A paused start names its rule** (`by`: `hourBound`, `contactPause`, `dayCap`), for the server's span alone: none of the three reads an account, so it tells nothing of one, and the page gets K-21's `paused` and its time only.
128. **`noTurn` comes first in each call's order:** `dispatcherGivesUp` once the row is found and locked, before its claim, version or time; `writeAllSet` as each listed row is locked, before its state; `markPart` before any part or row moves, and only for a mark that carries `followUps`. So a row with no turn is refused whatever else holds, and nothing is written before the refusal.

## Open gaps

1. **Closed by v1.1 (T-071):** K-07 v1 named the states, and its `outbox_claims` (migrated with v1.1) holds the claim and when it lapses; v1.1's `claimOutbox` takes a lapsed claim again (item 13). Under v1 alone a claim still never lapses.
2. **The door is not stored on a reservation** (K-04 v1): `extend` trusts the caller's door, and a promoted AI-door row gets 10 minutes, not 15. Proposed: a K-04 minor version adds `door`, and K-05 then reads it. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
3. **A firing of a row no handle owns** (monthly billing) can write no outbox row: `outbox_rows.turn_id` and `turns.handle_id` are not null in K-04 v1, so `fireClock` with no turn writes none (`refuse-fire-without-turn-writes-outbox`). Who tells the owner, and through which turn, is open (QUESTIONS.md CQ-28). v1.1 narrows it: a firing with no turn may write effect rows under its job id (item 18), so monthly billing's charge can run (its amount waits on Q-22, K-07 gap 1); the message still cannot go. Settling it needs a K-04 change (a turnless outbox row, or a job's turn on the owner's handle), which only adding cannot make; it stays open. v1.13 (T-1277): K-04 v1.1 now holds an outbox row with no turn (its `job_id`), which `sendSignInCode` writes for a sign-in's code (item 59), but `fireClock` keeps v1's refusal (`refuse-fire-without-turn-writes-outbox`), so a firing with no turn still writes no outbox row and CQ-28 stays open (ADR-016 section 1).
4. **The sweep's margin for a missed row** (`missedBefore`) is the runtime's (C-054); no source gives it.
5. **Email parts** keep Resend's message id in `outbox_parts.linq_message_id` until K-12 names it (QUESTIONS.md CQ-29; a K-04 minor version may rename or add the column). Kept in v1.1: `markPart` failed's `providerMessageId` goes to the same column; no core v1.1 table list adds one before K-12 (F7) asks.
6. **Row-level security mechanics** (the per-write scope, the unscoped cap count): the CTO confirms with the security reviewer, and the database engineer names the roles (K-04 rule 4; QUESTIONS.md CQ-30). v1.1 adds three more for that review: `recordProviderEvent` and the effects runner's claims (`claimEffects`, `recordEffectResult`, `serviceBack`) run unscoped on the runtime side, across businesses, while `effects` and `provider_events` are business-scoped tables (K-07); the commit's effects and the reconciliation's refund are written per write under their own business.
7. **Time budgets:** only the commit (300 ms, arch 04 line 223) and the send (1 s, arch 04 line 227) have one; the others are open.
8. **Q-12, Q-15, Q-21, CQ-05:** defaults in use (above), waiting on Jay or the CTO.
9. **The Tool API mock** (C-186, convention 9) answers a request that matches a fixture with its response; K-09's and K-21's fixtures may use this folder's exchange shape (`call`, `given`, `request`, `outcome`), which K-09 decides.
10. **K-07 v1 gaps v1.1 leaves open:** when texts count as working for the "all set" (K-07 gap 12; gap 14 here; the rows themselves are written by items 15, 17 and 25, and K-07 gap 3's link is the event log until a column holds it); an effect refusal other than a decline or a network error (K-07 gap 5: no state, so no outcome here); how an email copy's final parts are written while it holds its text row's plan (K-07 gap 11: `writePlan` still answers `alreadyPlanned`); an owner's refund with no turn (K-07 gap 15).
11. **A provider event's handling:** no v1.1 call writes `provider_events.handled_at` or lists the unhandled events (`provider_events_unhandled_idx`); the tasks that act on them (C-112, the calendar handlers of C-216 and C-217) ask for it in a later minor version.
12. **An effect try longer than its claim:** an effect claim is renewed only by a recorded try (item 21), so a single port call that runs past 60 s may be claimed again by another runner on the same idempotency key. Proposed: the ports' call timeouts stay under 60 s (K-14, K-16, K-11); otherwise a renew call for effects in a later minor version.
13. **A failed row to the owner with no other way left:** the sources give the owner's number to call for a customer's failed row (S12-10), and name no one to tell when the owner's own row fails with no way left; `dispatcherGivesUp` then writes no follow-up (`followUps` empty). Proposed: the team console shows the failed row (C-194). New question for QUESTIONS.md.

15. **Closed by v1.2 (T-072):** `claimEffects` and `serviceBack` append one K-08 v1.1 `effect.claimed` entry per row they claim (item 33; CQ-43). Under v1.1 alone a claim is still not logged.

The walking skeleton's rows (S1-01 and S1-02) each ask a question, so they are not blocked; S1-04's notices, a confirmation and every walked row that asks nothing are. **Needs:** a **K-04 v1.1** core table list adding `outbox_rows.step` (text, null: a K-01 stepId) and `outbox_rows.line_no` (integer, null, with a CHECK `line_no is null or line_no >= 0`); a **migration task** for the database lane after it (two nullable columns and the CHECK, expand only, safe to ship dark); a **K-05 minor version** in which `commitTurn`'s `outboxRowNew` takes both (optional) and `readOutboxRow` reads them first (`line.from` `row`); and the commit's walk (C-087, after T-1139) writing them.

The sensitive switch (RULES 4.6; S13-22), a plug-in switch per kind of work and flow (K-23 `company_kinds.pf_switches_a` and `pf_switches_b`), is **read by v1.6 for the row's business** (item 45: true when any of its kinds of work lists it in either flow, false when none does, null, which the caller reads as on, when it has none), and that fills a row about no booking, which shows no visit's service (before v1.6 the turn face read it as on for every row: DL-326 note b). A booking's own switch, reached from a row only through its booking's service and that service's kind of work, is still not read: a row about a booking is filled as if the switch were on (each slot in its sensitive form, the stricter), whatever the business's switch. A later minor version reads each from its frozen home when the slot filler's card (C-088) asks.

32. **The hold read and the commit are two transactions:** `commitTurn` runs as `rosie_job` in its own transaction (T-306; STATE note 59), so a hold committed between `readOpenHolds` and `commitTurn` binds the next turn, not this commit. The actor runs one turn at a time per handle (arch 10 line 502), so only a write outside the turn (an arrival record: K-09 v1.1) can land in that window.
