47. **A sign-up row before S1-04 reads the switch as `null`** (item 45; DL-391): a business that holds no kinds of work yet has nothing to read the switch from, so the caller reads `null` as on and fills the row in its sensitive form. The walking skeleton's turn 1 is such a row: D-017's seed (T-1148's `db/seeds/skeleton.ts`) writes the barbershop's head, Sam, his handle and their link, and no kind of work (S1-04 has not run, and the demo shops' seeds, T-200's and T-213's, use ids of their own), so turn 1's S1-02 row reads `null` (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-no-kinds`) and is filled in its sensitive form (gentle, the display name alone), while K-09 v1.6's `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` and T-1175's recording 4 have it routine. It reads `false`, as they have it, only once the business holds kinds of work with no switch on (`read-outbox-row-s1-02-not-sensitive`). **Needs** one of, by the lead's ruling: the skeleton's seed gives the barbershop K-23's barber kind of work (the database engineer: one `companies` row and one `company_kinds` row in `db/seeds/skeleton.ts`, as K-23's `seed-company-two-kinds.json` holds them; the guard reads no kinds of work, so JOIN stays a first text); or K-09's S1-02 fixtures and recording 4 take the sensitive form (a K-09 minor version and a new recording); or the security reviewer rules that a section 1 row before S1-04 reads the switch as off, which this version does not do (default 67). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the security reviewer.
48. **A number's chat thread outside S1-01:** `sendState`'s chat (item 28) and the dispatcher's chat target (`runtime/src/dispatch`, `RecipientDirectory.chatFor`) need the handle's chat thread with Linq's chat id (K-04 `threads`, kind chat, `linq_chat_id`), which only S1-01's commit writes (K-09 v1.5 default 135). A customer's first text writes none yet, so a reply to it would end `noChat` at the dispatcher, its chat open or not. v1.7 writes the phone row's flags, never the chat id. **Needs:** the journey lanes give a first text's commit the thread write, as S1-01's has it (a K-09 minor version). New question for QUESTIONS.md. From v1.10 the same gap reaches a reply's receipt: `readChatHandle` finds no thread for such a chat (item 51; `read-chat-handle-unknown`), so the gateway answers 503 until Linq stops retrying, and the reply's delivery is never marked nor its question's clock armed, as for a question never delivered (arch 02 line 127: "Never delivered: no clock runs").
49. **A chat whose service changes** (a person who moves from an iPhone to an Android and keeps the number): `chat_way` keeps the opening text's service (default 72). Replies follow their own text's service and other rows Linq's capability check (arch 07 line 365), but a reader of `chat_way` alone (T-307's `textChannel`, `actions/src/domain/messages-consent`) picks the old way. **Needs:** a rule for when the stored way follows Linq's reports, in a later minor version. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
50. **Texts while PostgreSQL is down:** like the key (item 29), the call needs PostgreSQL at receipt. While PostgreSQL does not answer, the gateway answers 503 and Linq retries for 30 minutes (arch 02 line 109), and the actor's queue (arch 11 line 566) holds the texts received before. Were texts ever stored while PostgreSQL is down (a key served from a copy), the chat open must go with each text, on the actor's write-on-return queue ahead of that text's safety and stop records (`runtime/src/actor/copies`), never dropped. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the runtime engineer.
51. **Two first texts of one number recorded in parallel:** the one whose call takes the row first sets `chat_open_at`, though the other may have been received a second earlier. Kept: the time is when the chat opened, to the second, and the actor orders the texts by their own `receivedAt` (K-06 default 2).
52. **A fresh yes's booking, new or old** (RULES 7.5 and S13-32 line 7242: "a fresh yes on a new booking"; K-04 `phones.stopped`: "a new booking's texts after its fresh yes from this phone"; T-1099's security note a): v1.8 names the booking a yes covers, not when the booking was made, which no frozen part stores, so limit 8 lifts a stop for an own yes on a booking made before the stop as for a new one. Nothing goes on it while the stop stands (default 83). **Needs**, if a fresh yes is ever to send before START (CQ-74 decided the other way): a **K-04 minor version** adding `bookings.created_at` (timestamptz, null for rows made before it, written by the commit that inserts the head), a **migration task** (one nullable column, expand only, safe to ship dark), and a **K-05 minor version** in which `textsConsent` says whether its booking was made after the phone's `stop_at`; or C-102's writer refuses an own yes for a stopped mobile on a booking made before the stop, as T-307's `yes()` already refuses every other yes for it (`stoppedNumber`). New question for QUESTIONS.md, with CQ-74.
53. **A contact's own yes recorded outside a turn** (default 79): K-29 v1's row shows who sent a yes only through the turn that wrote it, or, for a START, by its having no business. Every frozen writer records a yes by text in a turn (K-09: a staff member's ready text rides as a core action; a read-back's yes is bound to its turn) or it is a START, so none meets this today, and a seed's own yes with no turn reads false, the safe side. **Needs**, if a writer ever records a contact's own yes outside a turn (a queue written on return, an import): a **K-29 minor version** adding `consents.from_handle_id` (text, null: the handle the word came from, null for a page, an AI or a job), a **migration task** (one nullable column and its foreign key to `handles`, expand only), and the K-05 minor version that reads it first. New question for QUESTIONS.md.
54. **The order receipts were recorded in is not stored** (default 85): K-04 v1 keeps each part try's `delivered_at`, not which receipt said `firstDelivery`, so a repeat (item 48) names the row's first delivery by the receipts' own times. Where a receipt was recorded after a later-timed one (the poll sent after its 10 s wait while the words' receipt was late: K-07 rule 4; a late receipt of an earlier try after a 4006: K-07 rule 2), the later-timed delivery said `firstDelivery`, its repeat says `firstOfRow` false, and if the crash lost its arming, that arming stays lost: the question takes the never-delivered path, as before v1.9 (STATE note 37). It needs a crash in the milliseconds between `markPart`'s commit and `armClock`, after receipts recorded out of their own order. **Needs**, if the arming must survive that too: a **K-04 minor version** adding `outbox_rows.first_delivered_key` (text, null: the part key whose delivery the row recorded first, written once by the mark that says `firstDelivery`), a **migration task** (one nullable column, expand only, safe to ship dark), and a **K-05 minor version** in which a repeat reads it before the times. New question for QUESTIONS.md, with CQ-201.
55. **The handle's party for a turn is not named** (`runtime/src/turn/types.ts` `TurnDirectory.partyOf`: the handle's K-04 id, the business the turn's calls run under, the person, and the zone its texts' dates resolve in; T-557's handoff: "stateReads.turn ... has no join card yet"; the D-017 path audit's R3; T-1240's card, its first read). Three of its fields have one reading in the stored rows: the handle by its address (`handles_address_key`; none is not known, so no turn runs), the person when the handle has exactly one (`person_handles`; none or several: null, K-09 v1 `caller.personId`'s "when known"), and the zone, the business's `time_zone`. **The business has two readings, and no source picks one.** K-09 v1 says only "The business the call runs under ... Null only where no business exists yet" (`caller.businessId`), and nothing says which of a handle's businesses a turn runs under, or which business a handle whose people hold no role has:
    - **Reading A, live roles only:** the business at which the handle's people hold live roles (`person_roles`, `ended_at` null) when there is exactly one; none gives null, the runtime side, as a sign-up before S1-09 writes a business. Under it test owner 1's JOIN, whose person holds no role (T-1148's seed), runs with no business, so the frontier's sign-up door has no target (K-02 v1 `target.businessId` is required; K-02 v1.1 default 9: "the turn's business as its target"), and ADR-013's default 130 and K-09 v1.5's turn 1 fixtures, which run it under `biz-lees-barbershop`, no longer describe the staging turn: the seed or ADR-013 would change with it.
    - **Reading B, default 130's seeded business, read from K-23:** as A, but a handle whose people hold no live role takes the business of the K-23 company its one person owns (`companies.owner_person_id`, `business_id` not null; T-1148's seed writes `company-lees-barbershop` for Sam), so JOIN runs under `biz-lees-barbershop`, as default 130 and the turn 1 fixtures say. It gives a turn a business at which its sender holds no role: an owner whose role ended at a hand-over (S11-100) while the company row still names them would run turns there, which open gap 46's condition ("never under one with a live owner role") would then have to refuse; and one login may own two companies (K-23 README: "a second company a second business on the same login"), which no source orders.

    Neither reading orders several businesses: K-04's `seed-person-roles.json` gives Sam three live owner roles and Alex two customer roles, and arch 03 line 159 labels other bookings' choices by business without naming the turn's own (K-09 v1.5 open gap 119: journey 1 decides). Nor does either give the zone of a turn with no business, which the port never takes null (UTC, as K-08 gives a row with no business, is the nearest default). A third source, staging's alone, is K-21 v1's test roster, which names tp-01's business (QUESTIONS.md CQ-206). **Needs:** the lead's ruling between A and B, or the roster on staging, with the security reviewer for B; a rule for several businesses and for the zone with none; then a K-05 minor version names the read, with no new column under either reading (its rows: `handles`, `person_handles`, `person_roles`, `businesses`, and under B `companies`, all read as `rosie_job` on the runtime side, the guard's side: K-04 v1 rule 4). Until then the turn's directory stays closed on staging and no turn runs there. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead. **Closed by v1.11 (T-1249; CQ-220; DL-499):** item 52 names the read and rules its business: the one scope the handle's people's live roles and companies name, live roles and companies together (default 98); a company's business only while no other person holds its live owner role and none of the handle's people left it (default 99); `businessUndecided`, never a business picked and never no business in its place, for several scopes or a company the rows contradict (defaults 100 and 102); the zone null with no business, read as UTC (default 101). Reading A alone would leave test owner 1, and every owner journey 1 signs up, with no business (gap 58); reading B alone would run a turn for an ex-owner after S11-100. What is left is gaps 56 to 61.

56. **A handle that acts for several businesses runs no turn** (`severalBusinesses`: default 98). K-04's seed holds such people (Sam's three owner roles, Alex's two customer roles), and so will every customer of two businesses and every owner of two. The sources pick the business from the message (S13-27 rule 29: "Read the role from what the message is about. When two roles fit, ask which one"; arch 02 line 115; arch 03 line 159: "Questions about other bookings add only their options, labeled by business, date and time"), which no read before the turn holds, while K-09 v1 runs every Tool API call of a turn under one business (`caller.businessId`; arch 10 line 546). **Needs:** a design for a turn across its handle's businesses (the journey lanes; K-09 and K-02 minor versions): the frontier reads each business's targets under that business's scope and labels them by business, decide picks by content and asks one lettered "which?" on a tie (S13-12), and the commit writes per write (item 1); or a turn that asks "which business?" first, as S11-15 does. Until then such a handle's texts run no turn, and gap 57's card decides what its person hears. No staging test phone meets it: tp-01 is test owner 1, and tp-02 to tp-05 hold one customer role each at the barber demo shop (T-202's `db/seeds/staging-core.ts`). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead and the CTO.
57. **What an undecided handle's texts get** (default 102). Until a runtime card takes `businessUndecided`, the binding gives the turn no party, and both a run of the turn and its park end at the missing party (`runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` and `runtime/src/turn/park.ts`: `unknownHandle`), so the texts wait and nothing is written: a STOP from such a number is not recorded (RULES 7.2: "It stops texts from the number it answers, at once"), and the person never hears that nothing changed (arch 11 line 576). That fails closed for every business, but not for the stop. **Needs:** a runtime card (C-046 the actor's turn, C-051 the guard, C-180 the park) that, on `businessUndecided`, runs the guard's code path for a STOP, START or HELP with business null (K-09 v1 `caller.businessId`: "or for a call that covers every business (a STOP: RULES 7.2)") and parks every other run at once for Rosie's team, its one row and entry with business null, from the error's handle and person; before any handle that meets gap 56 or default 99 texts Rosie. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the runtime engineer, with the security reviewer.
58. **No step writes the owner's role** (default 98). K-09's table of journeys 1 and 2 (README, `saveSetupStep`) writes `companies` (S1-04) and `businesses` (S1-09), and its one `person_roles` write is S2-02's staff, so an owner who signs up holds no live owner role: the party read finds the business by the company, while v1.4's `readBusinessOwner` reads the owner from the live owner role (default 58) and answers none (`business-owner-none`), so a safety line to the owner finds no one, and nothing that reads roles sees the owner. **Needs:** journey 1's lane writes the owner's live owner role in the commit that writes the business head (S1-09) or takes it live (S1-35), or the architect rules the company as the owner's record for every reader; the party read then names the business by the role, and the company stays its reading only between S1-04's company and that commit. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 1).
59. **A hand-over or a removal leaves the company naming the old owner** (default 99). S11-100's writer (journey 11, not built) ends the old owner's access and starts the new owner's role (gap 41), and nothing frozen says it moves `companies.owner_person_id`; a removal on papers (S13-31: "Or by Rosie's team on papers, like a death notice, a court order or a signed sale"; "Removing an owner is a hand-over (S11-100)"; RULES 9.5) of an owner who never held a role (gap 58) ends nothing the rows show. The party read fails the first two closed (`anotherOwner`, `roleEnded`), but a removed owner with no role, whose business has no other owner, is still given the business by the company. **Needs:** journey 11's lane moves the company to the new owner in the hand-over's commit (a company is "one tax number, one Stripe, one bill", and the new owner "links their own Stripe": S11-100), and a removal records the end of the owner's access in a row this read reads (with gap 58, the owner's role, ended), so no company keeps naming them. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 11), with the security reviewer.
60. **An erased person's link** (gap 28) reaches this read: until C-156's erasure removes an erased person's `person_handles` link (gap 28's proposal), the erased person's live roles and companies still name their business for the number, with `personId` null once `handleSealingKey` has given the number a fresh person (default 97), as they still show in the guard's targets. **Needs:** C-156 follows gap 28's proposal, or ends the erased person's roles, before any key is erased. Kept with gap 28's question.
61. **The zone of a turn with no business is UTC** (default 101). S13-03 and RULES 4.3 name the person's own zone, which no frozen table stores, so a newcomer's or a sign-up's texts read their dates in UTC, and a "tomorrow" sent near midnight UTC names another day than the sender's (read back before it changes anything: RULES 4.2). **Needs:** a person's zone in a frozen part (a K-04 or K-22 minor version), from the person's own answer, if sign-up turns before S1-09 must read dates in the sender's zone. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead (journey 1).
62. **Where the roster's testers who hold no team row are kept** (default 103; the card's first read, reported open as DL-498's lead note asks). K-21 v1 default 2 makes the roster's testers Rosie's team on staging, read from K-36's rows, while T-1250's card (sign-in's record, in review) rules "no team-page row for a human tester (build prompt 9.4)" and takes "the tester's standing from T-1248's source"; no frozen part lists testers any other way (K-21 open gap 12), and a list in a new place is a decision this card does not hold. The readings that fit: (a) K-21 default 2 as frozen: a human tester holds a staging team row whose one reach is a roster test phone or the test console, never their own phone or inbox, so build prompt 9.4 holds and item 53 is complete as frozen; (b) the testers' K-04 person ids, opaque ids only, in staging's roster config (default 106's module), handed to a later minor version's `readTesterStanding` request; (c) one staging-only K-04 `release_flags` row (scope `global`) whose `value` lists them, seeded beside the roster, as K-21 default 16 keeps a staging-only override there, with no column; (d) a K-22 or K-36 minor version that marks a tester, with its migration. Until ruled, a human tester with no team row answers `notTester`, failing closed; nothing waits on it before sign-in's server side serves K-21's sign-in calls (T-1250; CQ-214 (b)), and the check tester (default 105) is not affected. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead, with the security reviewer and the CTO (CQ-214's owner).
63. **Rosie's number on staging** (default 109): no staging config value holds it (STATE note 18: "a ROSIE_NUMBER variable (not a secret) is a new release-engineer task"), so the page shows no chat link until one does. **Needs:** that release-engineer task; the binding then builds K-09's form. New question for QUESTIONS.md, with STATE note 18.
64. **The check tester stands as a team member** (default 105): on staging it may also use what K-21 gives Rosie's team there (the team console's reads and writes), and staging's pages reach it as emails to the double. Staging holds test data only (CQ-218), its token is a key kept by name, and the seed is staging's alone. A standing narrower than the team's needs open gap 62's (b) to (d). New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the security reviewer, with CQ-214.
65. **A roster phone seeded at a real number** (NQ-DB-2: `STAGING_TEST_PHONES` set): item 54 answers that number, and the Linq double signs only the fiction range (`adapters/src/linq/double-webhooks/webhooks.ts`), so the page answers 503 for that phone (the audit's section 3). The page's sends need the fake handles. Kept: it fails closed, by the double's own rule.
66. **The roster's businesses, roles and people disagree with K-21's `test-roster.json` for tp-02 to tp-05** (STATE note 71; ops/cards/T-574.md line 30): v1.12's reads take only a handle, its number and its email, so they do not meet it; `testRoster` (T-574; CQ-206) does, and its card reconciles it by the ids the seed writes (T-574's note: "The roster must name the ids staging holds"). Kept with CQ-206.
67. **K-04 v1.1 is breaking for `readOutboxRow` by ADR-016's own rule** (CQ-248; ADR-016 open point 12): item 65 is the default in use, built by R0 (T-1279) before D (T-1289) lets a job row be written outside a test. If the CTO rules a major version, K-04's changes are renumbered v2 and item 65 moves with them; nothing else here changes. Kept with CQ-248, for the CTO.
68. **Who builds `signInEntries`:** K-08 v1.2 names db/repo's check side (T-1284) as the writer of the session entries, while T-1284's card gives `proveSignIn`, `endSession` and `personStanding` and names no `signInEntries`. **Needs:** the lead adds item 63 to T-1284's Gives or cards it beside it (`db/repo`, C-152), before B2 (T-1285) binds it. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead.
69. **Two spellings of a proof code's entry values:** T-322's `proveCode` and `sendProofCode` (K-09 v1's path, merged) write K-08 v1.1's draft names (`proven_at`, `route`, `contact_handle_id`, purpose `signIn`), while v1.13's sign-in entries write K-22 v1's (default 123). K-09's path is not served in the sign-in lane, so no row holds both yet. **Needs:** one form ruled with K-08 v1.2's open gaps (CQ-258, note a) in K-08's next minor version, and T-322's entries moved to it in their own task. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the lead.
70. **A late entry's time:** `signInEntries` writes an entry when it runs, so a wrong try logged by a later call carries that call's time (the code row keeps only its last wrong try's time, `last_wrong_at`). The count and the pause in each entry are exact. Kept: Rosie's team reads the times of the tries from the attempt's last wrong try and the entries' order.
71. **A send that never ran:** a page call whose isolate ends between its answer and `sendSignInCode` leaves the attempt with no code, and a repeat start sends nothing (default 115), so the person starts again, which counts toward the day's cap; ADR-016 open point 6 covers a dispatch that never ran, not the send. Proposed: accepted on staging (two testers, five starts a day each); journey 1's production card rules it. New question for QUESTIONS.md, for the runtime engineer with the lead.
72. **K-21 README line 116 and the null id** (DL-526): item 60 rules the sign-in check's `unknown` a wrong try, departing from K-21's line, as the lead note allows ("in this version, or in one K-21 README line through its owner"); a K-21 line pointing here is the page-api owner's to add when K-21 next changes. Kept: B2 (T-1285) reads item 60.
73. **v1.13's components in section "Producers and consumers":** the contract test holds that section's component ids to its side list (`tests/contract/data-access.v1.test.ts`, `SIDE_CHECKS`), so v1.13's new components (C-193 and C-095, the sign-in page calls; C-129, the email rows) are named in section "v1.13", "Consumers of v1.13", and join that section with T-1292, which adds their sides, as K-08 v1.2 does with its own (K-08 README, open gaps (v1.2)).

The schema's descriptions of `readOutboxRow.response`, `rowLine` and `rowQuestion.clockRow` say the same; no definition, field or outcome changed. Defaults 53 and 54 added.

Adds `openChatOnText` (item 46), the one call that writes a number's phone row at its texts: the gateway calls it at receipt for each text or photo from a phone handle, after `handleSealingKey` and before the text reaches the actor. It inserts a missing row with its chat open (`inserted`), opens a closed chat that is not stopped (`opened`), and writes nothing on an open chat (`alreadyOpen`), a stopped number (`stopped`) or a repeat of a text that opened the chat (`repeated`); it appends one K-08 v1 `phone.chat_opened` entry when it writes, never carries the number, and answers `notFound` or `notAPhone` for a missing or email handle.
